# Changelog

All notable changes to the **product-workflows** plugin are recorded here.
Format follows [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/).
Versions follow semver at the plugin level.
A section headed `— Unreleased` has not been published yet; where more than one of them stands, they all ship together in the next release.

## [3.8.5] — 2026-09-26

**Update `workflows-core` to 1.7.8 with this release.** `/idea` Phase 3 cites its `grilling-technique` rule *"A decision the source already states is settled"*, which an older `workflows-core` does not carry.

### Fixed — behaviour

- **`/idea`'s grill could argue past a scope boundary its own source had drawn.** A source that excluded something explicitly, but in passing (a parenthetical, a line near the end), had that exclusion captured by `idea-reader` inside `raw_context` prose and nowhere else. The orchestrator then wrote a grill recommendation whose rationale assumed the excluded surface was in scope, and used that breadth as its reason, so the user had to correct the premise before the parent decision could settle. The recommendation happened to survive the correction on a different rationale; unchallenged, it would have recorded a correct decision on a false premise. Seen on a sibling edition's run, where the input reached the orchestrator intact and the failure was at synthesis. `idea-reader` now returns `stated_scope` (every explicit **in** and **out** boundary, each with a verbatim `quote`, its `ref`, and `firmness: firm | tentative`). `/idea` Phase 3 seeds the design tree with the firm entries as decisions already made, which take no question slot. It puts a tentative one to the user to confirm, and checks every recommended answer **and its rationale** against the set before showing it. Arguing that a stated boundary is wrong is still possible, as an explicit challenge quoting it that competes for a slot. Phase 4 writes every entry into `## Rough scope` unless the grill reversed it, naming them before the section is written, and the Final report lists them with any reversal. `idea-format.md` Section 4 says the same of the file.

## [3.8.4] — 2026-09-24

**Update `workflows-core` to 1.7.7 with this release.** Its `next-phase-offer` names the `/brd-interview` hand-over this release adds; beside 1.7.6 it still lists only the packaging, round and re-grounding steps after `/brd-interview`.

### Changed — the marketplace is now `shipwright`, and the repository `ihudak/ai-workflows`

The marketplace was `ihudak-plugins`, at `ihudak/ihudak-claude-plugins`. GitHub redirects the old repository URL, but the marketplace name is part of every install key (`<plugin>@ihudak-plugins`), so moving to the new name means registering the marketplace again. Removing a marketplace uninstalls the plugins installed from it, so reinstall each one you had:

```bash
claude plugin marketplace remove ihudak-plugins
claude plugin marketplace add ihudak/ai-workflows
claude plugin install <plugin>@shipwright
```

Run the last line once per plugin you use, then restart Claude Code. Environment variables and your specs, docs and code repositories are not touched. This plugin's `homepage` and `repository` now point at the new repository.

### Fixed — behaviour

- **`/brd-interview` no longer leaves a decided slice with nowhere to go.** Where every question was settled from the verified findings, so the slice needs no customer review, the run said the BRD was decided but offered neither the packaging step nor any authoring command — although `/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` each gate the slice's `decisions.md` and read no reconciliation record. It now offers them on the conditions `/brd-reconcile`'s advancing list already applies: `/create-ard` and `/specify` on the slice level alone, and `/create-prd` only where the slice is PRD-eligible — a row it claims is `covered-here` — since `/create-prd` refuses one that is not. All routes are printed in prose, per the overflow rule, and the context-hygiene advice covers the hand-over to PA or PE. `/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` now describe their BRD-route input as a *decided* BRD slice rather than a reconciled one.

### Fixed — documentation

- The Workflow overview diagram labelled the `/create-prd` → `/specify` edge "PRD-level spec"; what crosses it is `prd.md`. It now also draws `/prd-ground`'s recommended hand-off to `/update-prd` on a CONFIRMED claim, a PRD-level `specification.md` feeding `/epics`, an Epic-level ARD reaching `/dev-workflows:design`, `/dev-workflows:ready` reading what `/dev-workflows:design` leaves, and the new `/brd-interview` hand-over. Its edges between pipeline commands now name the deliverable where one crosses them, and every node is coloured by the plugin that ships it. The BRD workflow diagram gains the same hand-over, labels on its previously unlabelled edges, and the same colours.

## [3.8.3] — 2026-09-24

**Update `workflows-core` to 1.7.6 with this release**: its `grounding-format` §8 supersedes an on-file finding a verifier contradicts, and §5 supersedes one whose horizon moves; `/prd-ground` now writes both and `/brd-interview` reads them. Its `phase-handoff` §3.4 carries the `/prd-ground` and `/brd-package` stops below. An older `workflows-core` describes an in-place rewrite and row-F stops this release no longer takes.

### Upgrade notes — what an existing tree meets

- **A deferred re-put `[V]` question written by 3.8.1 or 3.8.2 carries no `- **Re-puts:**` line.** From this release on, the write that first records a `[V]` tag at a question's address writes the line, reading `none` where the question puts no decision again. That is the question's first write where it is written `[V]`, and otherwise the write recording its re-tag from `[G]` or its rewrite out of *untagged*. A question whose `[V]` tag is on file with no line is therefore exactly one an earlier version wrote. When `/brd-interview` resumes its round, it puts one tie picker per such question before the queue opens. The picker asks which reopened, held or reverted decision the question puts again, offering only the decisions that fit, or none. The answer is written as the line, so each question is asked about once. A `[V]` carrying the line, `none` included, is never asked about, and nor is a question the run itself tags `[V]`.
- **A self-review written before this release carries no `- **Re-escalates:**` line.** An answer to an `[SR#n]` finding of such a package is frozen fresh, as it was before, and is never tied to an answer the customer gave to the same finding in an earlier package. That is the safe direction: the worst case is a second record where one would have superseded the first. Packages built from this release on write the line where the operator confirms two findings are one.
- **An earlier `/brd-interview` run that was cancelled, aborted or interrupted may have left torn writes.** Before this release, the `[C]` entries and defect lines a run held were written to `interview/customer-questions.md` before its round record existed. A Phase 8 *Cancel* or an abort left them there, with no round record naming them. Such an item is now a torn write (`decision-register-format` §8), and no reader counts it. The next `/brd-interview` run reports each one in Phase 0 and removes it in Phase 9, before appending its own. A run that opens no new round removes them too, and reports what it removed. Until one does, `/brd-package` refuses the folder with `BRD_PACKAGE_TORN_WRITES`, naming each item. Where the slice kept no requirement of its own, `/brd-interview` refuses it, and the stop says so. Two leftovers are not torn writes and stand. A defect line appended to an entry whose round record predates both requirement-defect lines is counted under §8's reading of such a record. And an earlier version wrote an entry for each `[C]` of the round on every run, resumes included, so one `## Round <N>, question <p>` heading can head more than one entry; §8 counts each, so `/brd-interview` reports every such heading in Phase 0, and the operator reduces the entries to one by hand. A re-decision an interrupted run wrote onto a record already on file cannot be undone, so it stands. The round it belongs to, when next opened or resumed, records it as a question already disposed *decided* and puts it to nobody.
- **A package built before this release has no `Package reviewed` line in its prompt**, so a review of it cannot repeat one in section 1 (`customer-review-schema` §4 now requires it). `/brd-reconcile` then finds the package the review answers in this order: a date section 1 names that matches exactly one prompt on file, the only package on file, or else the operator's pick from the dated prompts. Where the operator cannot tell, no self-review is handed over, and every `[SR#n]` answer comes back `unmatched` for a human. Packages built from this release on print the line, and a review that copies it is matched on it.

### Fixed — behaviour

- **`/brd-interview` wrote held `[C]` entries and defect lines before the run was finished** (ii, N2, R23–R30). A Phase 8 *Cancel*, an abort or an interruption between Phase 7 and Phase 9 left entries in `interview/customer-questions.md` that no round record named. Phase 9 is now the only phase that writes a round's deliverables. It writes `code-defect-log.md`, then `decisions.md`, then `customer-questions.md`, then `interview/round-<N>.md` last. The round record is the commit point: an item stamped with a round whose record does not exist or does not name it is a **torn write**. `decision-register-format` §8 defines it once, and every reader cites it and never counts one: `/brd-interview`'s *asked* test and question generation, `/brd-package`, `/brd-reconcile`, `/brd-split`, `/prd-ground`, `/create-prd`, `/create-ard`, `/specify`, `/prd-proposal`, `brd-package-reviewer` and `proposal-reviewer`. `customer-review-reader` reads the files it is handed as they stand, and `/brd-reconcile`, its caller, applies §8 to what it returns. Every phase before Phase 9 holds its part in memory: the `[V]` answers, the held `[C]` entries, a defect line added to a held entry, a `--round N` re-open and a question the round-1 test adds. So a *Cancel* or an abort now writes nothing the run decided, and `docs/commands/brd-interview.md`'s "writes nothing" is true. A round record written before its `code defects:` and `requirement defects:` lines existed first gets a baseline line naming what was on file. A `[CDF#n]` re-disposition is applied after the round record names it, and the next run's Phase 0 completes one a stopped run left. `code-defect-log-format` makes a removed torn write the one exception to never reusing an id.
- **A `[CDF#n]` re-disposition one run completed could be left uncommitted** (M7, M8). Phase 0 completes a re-disposition an interrupted run left, which writes `code-defect-log.md`; a stop, an abort or a *Cancel* before this run's own handoff then left that write in the working tree. The handoff now stages the log wherever `git status --porcelain` reports it changed, and the step 9 report, the abort paragraph and the *Cancel* paragraph say it stays uncommitted until a run reaches its handoff. A run that opens no new round hands the log off too wherever it completed a re-disposition or found a change an earlier run left uncommitted.
- **`/brd-package` could ship a torn write, and inferred two files' merge from the register's** (N16). It now stops with `BRD_PACKAGE_TORN_WRITES` before the register gate, so that gate's remedy never lands a torn write first. A round record that exists but cannot be read stops the run with `BRD_PACKAGE_ROUND_UNREADABLE`. `interview/customer-questions.md` and `code-defect-log.md` ship in the bundle whole, and each is now gated with `require-on-main` where it is in the folder, stopping with `BRD_PACKAGE_SHIPPED_NOT_HANDED_OFF`. The round gate stops with `BRD_PACKAGE_ROUND_NOT_RECORDED` for a round whose record is not even in the folder, named only by a re-decision an interrupted run left. Its remedy names `/brd-interview`: once every round is closed, the round that run opens records each such re-decision, and while a round is still open it resumes that round, the re-decision being recorded in the round it opens once that one closes.
- **A decision reopened by another command had no question source, so it stayed `reopened` for good** (iii, N1, R31, R35, R62). `/brd-reconcile` reopens a decided `[VD#n]` or `[CD#n]` when a customer answer contradicts or constrains it without replacing it, and its propagation sweep reopens one in a dependent BRD. No `/brd-interview` source put the question again, so `/brd-reconcile` kept offering a round that asked nothing. A new source, *A decision reopened elsewhere*, puts every `reopened` record whose question no round still holds unanswered. It is put in the round the run opens, under the record's own tag, against the current findings, with each `Reopened` paragraph quoted as context. A record reopened while a round is open waits for the next round, and the run says so. *Every round is closed* and Phase 11's `nothing-to-review` no longer say that a decision another command moved raises nothing. Now a move to `reopened` raises its question, and a move to `superseded` or `withdrawn` raises nothing. Every question that puts a decision again, whatever its tag, carries a structured `- **Re-puts:** [VD#n]` (or `[CD#n]`) line, and every other `[V]` carries `- **Re-puts:** none`, written by the write that first records its `[V]` tag, so a question re-tagged to `[V]` gets it too. Every reader resolves a re-put through that line and never through prose. See the upgrade notes for a `[V]` an earlier version wrote without it.
- **A reopened decision a propagation sweep reverted while its re-put question waited had no exit** (R63). The sweep's *Reverted* restores a reopened `[VD#n]` to `open` or `decided`, and the question already putting it again then answered a record `/brd-interview` would neither re-decide nor supersede. Such a record is now superseded by the answer, as a record the will-change rule held is, `Superseded <YYYYMMDD>: by [VD#m]` naming the new record, and the tie picker counts it among its candidates. Where that supersession is itself a torn write, `decision-register-format` §8 now restores the `status` the record held before it, read off the record by §6, instead of a fixed `open`: `open` where it carries no `conditional_on` and every finding it rests on reads `horizon: will-change`, and `decided` otherwise.
- **`--round <highest+1>` could open a round beside one still holding unanswered questions** (R34). It now stops with `BRD_INTERVIEW_ROUND_STILL_OPEN`, naming the open round and the step each of its holding states needs, in the order they must run. The two deliberate concurrent openings stand: Phase 11's `defects-unasked` and a `--round 1` re-open. `/brd-reconcile` offers *Work another round* for a decision it reopened only where the bare `/brd-interview` run would move something (R32): where no round is open, and where the open round holds a *deferred*, *untagged* or *needs grounding* question, which that run resumes, naming `/prd-ground` first for the last. Where the open round holds only questions *held for the customer*, the offer is not made, and the reopened decision is named as waiting behind *Package again*. A record its propagation sweep reopened in a dependent BRD is named with `/brd-interview` on that BRD's key.
- **`/brd-interview` Phase 12's key events listed "a cancelled `[V]` queue"** (iv), which no path that reaches Phase 12 produces. The entry is removed.
- **`/brd-interview`'s legacy anchor saw only history reachable from HEAD** (xii, N3). A round record written before the `generated against:` line was compared at `git log -1 -- <record>`. That missed a record committed only on another branch, and could anchor on a squash commit whose grounding files already carried later changes. The anchor is now the earliest commit on any ref (`log --all`) whose blob for the record equals the record as it stands on disk, resolved against the whole candidate set. Where no candidate matches, every finding reads as unchanged, and the report says no commit on any ref holds the record as it stands. Where the matched commit itself changed a file under `grounding/`, the report names it as a commit that may postdate the record's write. The final report states the anchor's outcome either way.
- **A `/prd-ground` verifier `contradict` rewrote an on-file finding in place** (vii, D2, R68, R72). The rewrite reopened nothing, and a later `prior_verdict` recorded the rewritten verdict. Phase 7 now splits on origin. An own-run finding, which nothing cites yet, is still rewritten under the same id. An on-file finding is superseded, keeping its verdict as `prior_verdict`, and a successor with the next id carries the verifier's verdict, the same `claim`, `commit`, `altitude`, `horizon` and `prerequisite`, a `control` where it owes one, `consumed_by: none`, `outcome: contradict` and a `supersedes` note. `/brd-interview`'s successor tests hold for it unmodified. A class-4 `[DG#n]` citing a `[CG#n]` superseded this way is superseded by Phase 8's cascade, its note naming the run that replaces it: `--no-code` where the `[CG#n]` has a successor, and a plain re-run where it has none. As after a `--rebaseline` pass, citations into the old id still resolve, the coverage ledger's `evidence` column lists both ids, and a `consumed_by` stamp stays on the block it was written to. `decision-register-format` §4 cause 1 names the route. **A `contradict` whose finding owes a control and whose return carries no `own_control` writes no outcome at all.** On an own-run finding the run stops with the new `PRD_GROUND_VERIFY_INCOMPLETE` before Phase 8 writes anything, and fires `emit-block`. On an on-file finding nothing is superseded and no successor appended, the block keeps every field, and the Final report names it as not verified by this run.
- **A decision resting on several findings was reopened only when every one of them was superseded** (N12, R15). `decision-register-format` §4 cause 1 fires on any superseding finding. *A decision the re-grounding moved* now takes a record where **any** `evidence` finding reads `SUPERSEDED`. The confirmation test runs per superseded finding, a finding not superseded stands as cited, and the record is confirmed only where every superseded finding confirms.
- **Phase 6 moved an on-file finding's `horizon` and `prerequisite` in place** (N14, R18, R21). It now supersedes the finding and appends a successor with the same verdict, evidence and control and the new horizon. That successor is own-run, so Phase 7 verifies it. An on-file `[DG#n]` no frame set can place is superseded with no successor, and its note names the horizon Phase 6 would have written.
- **A design finding could lose its frame citations and so never be placed in a frame set** (N11, N15, R16, R19, R75). A `[DG#n]` rewritten in place, and any `[DG#n]` successor, keeps every frame citation of the evidence it replaces beside the verifier's own evidence. A held record resting on a superseded finding **no successor will come to** is put again at once, naming that finding and which of two cases applies, rather than waiting on a successor: it cannot be matched to one — its source cannot be decided, or it is a frame-only finding carrying no field token — or the finding, or one of its successors, each since superseded, carries the note `superseded: frame set <frame-set> re-ground`, which `/prd-ground`'s frame-set rule now writes on every block it supersedes. Any other finding with no successor yet waits, the class-4 finding the cascade retired among them, since its note names the run that re-derives it.
- **`/brd-interview` could not confirm a frame-only class-1 finding re-emitted by a later run** (R67, R73, R80). Successor test 3 matched a finding's `claim` by the requirement id it opens with, and a class-1 `[DG#n]` has none. `design-grounder` now writes a class-1 `claim` as `none — frame-only: <field>`, `<field>` being the label of the one unasked-for field exactly as the frame displays it, so two such fields on one frame are two findings. A successor of such a finding reads the same `claim`, token included, compared exactly, and cites the same frames: the evidence paths its set's index names equal the superseded finding's. A plain re-run that re-emits the finding therefore confirms the decision resting on it, and a finding about another field or frame never does. A legacy class-1 finding carrying no field token cannot be matched, so a decision resting on it is reopened, or put again where held, once; the answer is recorded against the current findings, which carry the token.
- **A plain `/prd-ground` re-run retired class-4 `[DG#n]` findings that nothing regenerated** (N13, R17, R65, R66). The frame-set supersession rule now retires a prior class-4 `[DG#n]` only where Phase 5 re-ground its claim against the repository its cited `[CG#n]` is pinned to, or under `--no-code`. Otherwise the finding stands, and only the cascade retires it. On a repository whose `HEAD` still matches its recorded pin, a plain re-run re-grounds only the claims no `[CG#n]` on file answers at that pin, a re-cut row among them, and each dispatch carries only the claims its repository still has to answer; a new `--depends-on` no longer re-grounds anything, since Phase 6 supersedes the on-file findings for their horizon. A frame set the design pass reconciled counts as re-ground whether or not it yielded a finding, so an agreeing re-run supersedes the set's prior class 1–3 findings rather than leaving them standing. Phase 7's opening set is now stated: every unsuperseded on-file `[CG#n]` pinned to a repository whose `HEAD` matched, on a plain re-run and a rebaseline alike, none under `--no-code`, and never an on-file `[DG#n]`.
- **A `/prd-ground` run that stopped after Phase 3 left its new pin recorded without its findings** (R74). Phase 3 appended `baselines.md` before Phase 5 and Phase 7 could stop, so a `--rebaseline` re-run then saw `HEAD` match the new pin and never superseded the old-pin findings. Phase 3 now holds each dated entry, and Phase 8 appends it after the two finding files, in the same write as the findings pinned to it; nothing is appended under `--no-code`. A run that stops in Phase 5 or 7 leaves the recorded pin as it found it, so a `--rebaseline` re-run supersedes the old findings, and a plain re-run stops with `PRD_GROUND_NEEDS_REBASELINE` as before.
- **`/prd-ground` Phase 8's field list** omitted `prerequisite` (viii) and wrote `consumed_by: none` on every block (N10). It now lists `prerequisite` on every finding reading `horizon: will-change`. `consumed_by: none` goes only on a block this run appends, and an on-file block keeps its stamps.
- **`/prd-ground`'s two Phase 0 gates had no stated order** (ix). The order changed which stop was printed. The ledger's gate resolves first, including whether the file is in the folder at all on row F, and the inventory's second. Where both stop, the ledger's stop prints, save one case: the ledger is in the folder and on no ref and the inventory is missing, where the inventory's `PRD_GROUND_NO_INVENTORY` prints. Under that order, rows of `PRD_GROUND_NO_INVENTORY`'s remedy table that could no longer be reached, and whose "no command has committed this slice's folder" was false where they could, are replaced (N9). That stop now prints only where the ledger was written, so a slice's inventory was written and lost. A parent re-run never re-creates it, so the remedy is to restore it.
- **`/prd-ground` could not tell an unfinished carve from a declined handoff** (xi, R4, R6). `/brd-split` writes a slice's three files before its walk places a row, so a carve stopped mid-walk leaves the same files as a finished one whose handoff was declined. `PRD_GROUND_NOT_HANDED_OFF` told the operator to commit them, and said a re-run was a no-op. Row F branch (b) now reads the parent's ledger first. Where the parent still holds an `unallocated` row, the new stop `PRD_GROUND_CARVE_UNFINISHED` says, by what the ledger shows and not why, that the slice's files are provisional. It says nothing in the folder is to be committed, and names the instructed `/brd-split <PARENT-KEY> "<how to cut it>"`. A `/brd-intake` re-run over the parent leaves the same state. `PRD_GROUND_NOT_HANDED_OFF` stands only where the parent is fully allocated and the slice agrees with it.
- **A slice out of step with its parent passed every `/prd-ground` gate and was grounded** (N5, R3). A carve cancelled mid-walk whose provisional files were committed by hand passed both gates, and so did a slice whose `claims:` named a `[BR#n]` its inventory did not hold. On the BRD route, once both gates pass, the run compares the slice's `claims:`, its inventory's rows and the parent's rows reading `covered-by: <this slice>`. Where they differ it stops with `PRD_GROUND_SLICE_UNRECONCILED`, naming each id by the set that lacks it, and grounds nothing. The remedy is the parent's instructed re-run where an `unallocated` row remains, the bare `/brd-split <PARENT-KEY>` otherwise, and no form where the parent's ledger cannot be read. The same stop now covers a `brd-link.md` that names no `parent:`.
- **`/prd-ground` took interrupted and damaged slice states for ordinary ones** (M5, M6, R77, NB3). A `PRD-` folder inside a BRD folder — a `BRD-` one, or a legacy unprefixed one §5.1's positive evidence places — with no `brd-link.md` was read as an idea-route PRD folder and grounded. Where it is empty, it is the folder a carve created and never linked, and it now stops with `PRD_GROUND_CARVE_INTERRUPTED`, whose remedy is to delete the empty folder and re-run the instructed `/brd-split`, never `/create-prd`. Where it holds anything, it is a slice that lost its `brd-link.md`, and it stops with `PRD_GROUND_LINK_MISSING`, which never names removal: it names the commit on any ref that wrote the file to restore it from, or, where none did, what the file must say. A slice's inventory or ledger that is on the default branch but missing from the working tree stopped with a remedy for a lost file; step 6a now stops with `PRD_GROUND_RESTORE_FROM_DEFAULT`, naming the restore.
- **`/prd-ground` had no stop for a slice's own inventory or ledger that is present and cannot be read** (area C re-review). `/brd-split` marks such a child unreconcilable, and `/prd-ground` passed it on to fail at Phase 8's ledger write. Step 6a now stops with `PRD_GROUND_SLICE_FILE_UNREADABLE`, naming the file and the error, and saying no `/brd-split` run repairs it.
- **`/brd-split` Phase 3 could propose a slice key an existing folder already carries** (NB3). A key now counts as used wherever an immediate subdirectory is named `PRD-<candidate>` or begins `PRD-<candidate>-`, linked or not, tested per candidate and never parsed out of a name, and an operator-supplied key that collides is re-prompted.
- **`/brd-split` reconciled only the children its own walk touched** (N7, R5, R7, R71). A stale provisional claim from an interrupted run survived. Once the parent was fully allocated, a bare re-run was a no-op, so nothing repaired a slice out of step. Phase 4 Step 3 now reconciles every standing child against the parent's `covered-by` rows, and writes nothing for a child already in step. A new reconcile path runs where no row is `unallocated` but a child stands empty or out of step: Step 3 first, then the empty-child resolution. Running Step 3 first gives back a claim to a child a cancelled carve left empty, so that child is not offered for removal. A child whose inventory or ledger is missing or unreadable is unreconcilable: it is reported and never reconciled, and it is never a walk's target, a re-cut's donor or a re-cut's receiver, the run naming it and its missing file beside each list that leaves it out. The run names `/prd-ground <CHILD-KEY>` as the command whose stops tell that child's states apart and name the repair — `PRD_GROUND_NO_INVENTORY`, `PRD_GROUND_NEEDS_SPLIT` and `PRD_GROUND_RESTORE_FROM_DEFAULT` among them. The *Cancel* paragraph says a slice's files stay provisional until Step 3 reconciles them, and that `/prd-ground` refuses the slice until then. A re-cut interrupted between its two ledger writes leaves the donor's row `deferred-to`; the next run's Step 3 leaves it standing, and §6.1 counts it as an orphan row through the parent. The command's `description:`, the docs page and `coverage-ledger-format` no longer call a bare re-run on a fully-allocated parent a no-op.
- **An ordinary `/brd-split` walk could assign a row to a child holding an orphan row for it** (N8, R11, R12). That is reachable after a `/brd-intake` re-run over the parent, and the child then claimed a row its own ledger gave to another owner. The walk now applies §3.2's per-row receiver exclusion to orphan rows: a child holding a row for a `[BR#n]` it does not claim is not offered that `[BR#n]`, and the walk says why. Nor is a child that still claims the `[BR#n]` while its own row reads `covered-by`, the trace of a withdrawal a re-cut began and never finished, nor an unreconcilable child. Every other child that still claims the `[BR#n]` is offered it whatever its own row reads, so an intake re-run strips no settled claim. `coverage-ledger-format` §2 names three routes to an orphan row, and §6.1 counts every orphan row through the parent's current disposition. §5.2's "never `covered-here`" is corrected: an orphan row left as the slice's own earlier decision can read `covered-here`, and the gate-set narrowing is what keeps it from making a slice eligible. The Final report of `/prd-ground`, `/brd-interview`, `/brd-package` and `/brd-reconcile` now counts orphan rows from all three routes, through the parent's current disposition.
- **`/brd-reconcile` had no disposition for a corrected resend answering a `decided` record the same way** (x, N4, D1, R37, R40, R43). Phase 4 now skips a candidate whose confirmed `chosen` equals the target record's and whose reason is byte-equal to the record's after whitespace collapse. The same applies to the same `chosen` with no reason stated, since a missing reason is not a different one. It is reported as *already reconciled, re-affirmed by* the review. It mints nothing, marks nothing answered and adds nothing to the propagation sweep. The skip covers `[C]`, `[AS#n]` and `[SR#n]` targets, and runs only on a confirmed mapping. Step 3's "replaces" now means an answer to the record's own question, whatever it chooses, so the same option with a different reason supersedes. The section-7 matching table, and `customer-review-reader`'s matching rule, describe a question whatever its entry's state, instead of reading as a filter on held questions, which had made the corrected-resend branch dead.
- **`/brd-reconcile` froze an answer against a record that was no longer live** (N17, N18, N19, R38, R39, R44, R45, R70). Every target now resolves to its question's live record: the earliest record frozen against it, followed through every supersession, `- **Re-puts:**` lines included, to the successor that stands. The skip and supersede rules compare against that record. An answer whose live record is `withdrawn` freezes nothing. A held entry whose `- **Re-puts:**` line names a record since withdrawn closes on the first review that answers it, whichever option the operator takes: *Record it for a human* closes it naming the withdrawal, and *Reject* closes it with the reason recorded. `/brd-package` renders only held entries, so no later package carries it. A candidate whose chain holds a record this same review froze is skipped, whatever its status now, so re-running an earlier review never reverts a later one's answer. A record frozen `open` for want of its reason is completed by the same `chosen` with a reason, skipped on the same `chosen` with none, and superseded by a different one. The same `chosen` with no reason never reaches the missing-reason picker. A different `chosen` with no reason, on a question an earlier review already answered, is not held again: its `open` record is named under *what still needs a human*, since no package re-asks it.
- **`/brd-reconcile` matched `[SR#n]` answers against the wrong package's self-review** (N20, R42, R46, R48). Every package numbers its findings from `[SR#1]` again. Now the reader is handed only the self-review of the package the review answers, resolved as the upgrade notes above describe, and never another. `/brd-package` prints a `Package reviewed: <BRD-KEY> <YYYYMMDD>` line into the prompt, and `customer-review-schema` §4 requires section 1 to repeat it. `customer-review-reader` returns it as `package_reviewed`. Where no package can be determined, every `[SR#n]` answer is `unmatched` and goes to a human. The reconciliation record names the package and how it was determined.
- **A finding re-escalated in a later package was frozen beside the customer's earlier answer to it** (N21, R47, R49). When a re-package's finding takes `escalated-to-customer`, `/brd-package`'s disposition gate checks it against earlier packages' escalated findings. A match needs the same class and the same single-record target, compared as whole values. Each match is shown beside the earlier finding's words. Only where the operator confirms the two are one finding does the self-review write `- **Re-escalates:** self-review-<YYYYMMDD>.md [SR#k]`. `/brd-reconcile` follows that operator-confirmed line to the earlier answer's live record. `brd-package-reviewer` now writes a target that is one record as that record's bracketed id alone, which is what makes the comparison mechanical.
- **`/create-prd` did not say whether a `prd.md` with no `kind: prd` counts as found** (v, D3). Step 6 now says any file at `<feature-folder>/prd.md` counts as found, whatever its frontmatter, as `/update-prd` treats it. It also says why `workflows-core:addressing` §5's three kind-gating commands differ: they consume a PRD's content, and `/create-prd` must not overwrite one. A `prd.md` that exists but cannot be read stops Phase 1 step 2 with `CREATE_PRD_PRIOR_UNREADABLE`, before any grill or write. `/create-prd`'s `CREATE_PRD_EPIC_FOLDER` remedy and `/epics`' `EPICS_NO_PRD` rows name `/update-prd` beside `/create-prd` where the folder's `prd.md` asserts no `kind: prd`.
- **Every `/create-prd` Overwrite lost the prior PRD** (N6, N22, R50, R52, R54). The three Overwrite labels promised "(archives the current one)", but no step took an archive, and Phase 5 wrote `prd.md` with no existence guard. Immediately before the run's first write to `prd.md`, now usually Phase 3's live authoring, the run copies an existing file into `revisions/` under `/update-prd`'s naming. This happens on every route, with no exemption, once per run. On the BRD route it also comes before the first write to `decisions.md`. A failed copy stops with `CREATE_PRD_ARCHIVE_FAILED` before anything is written. The archive path is staged with the PRD and reported. The Phase 3 pointer to "(Phase 0)" now names Phase 1 step 2.
- **`/update-prd` lost the prior revision on every update, and on a second update the same day** (N23, N24, R51, R53). The grill wrote `prd.md` live in Phase 3, before Phase 5's archive. The archive is now taken immediately before the run's first write to `prd.md`, once per run. Where the dated name is taken by any file, it uses the first free name with `-2`, `-3`, … inserted before `.md`, for both name forms. `revision_of:` records the name actually written. A base that exists and cannot be read stops with `UPDATE_PRD_BASE_UNREADABLE`, and a failed copy with `UPDATE_PRD_ARCHIVE_FAILED`, both before anything is written. `proposal-format` §2's archive rule takes the same first-free rule and cites `/update-prd` Phase 3, and `/prd-proposal` and `/brd-proposal` now hand off the archive at the name that rule actually wrote, suffix included, instead of the unsuffixed dated name; their docs pages say so.
- **`/epics` refused on files it read before the specs-repo preflight had settled the checkout's branch** (N26, N27, N29). Step 1a listed a BRD container's slices from their `brd-link.md`, and step 1b read `prd.md` for `EPICS_NO_PRD`, both before the preflight. So a stale plugin branch could hide a slice or make a merged PRD look absent. Step 1 now fixes the run key set from carrier frontmatter alone, including the parent's key on an Epic-level folder, and runs the preflight before step 1a or 1b reads anything. `EPICS_PRD_NO_REQUIREMENTS` fired only after Phase 1's questions and Phase 2's grounding. It now runs in Phase 0, right after step 1b accepts the run.
- **`/create-ard`, `/specify`, `/update-prd` and `/idea` refused on files they read before the specs-repo preflight** (R69). The same shape as `/epics`': `CREATE_ARD_BRD_NOT_SLICED` and `SPECIFY_BRD_NOT_SLICED` listed slices by their `brd-link.md`, `UPDATE_PRD_NO_PRD` and `UPDATE_PRD_BASE_UNREADABLE` read `prd.md`, `UPDATE_PRD_EPIC_FOLDER`'s remedy read the folder above, and `IDEA_NOT_AN_IDEA_FOLDER` read a slice's `brd-link.md`, `prd.md` and ledger, each before the preflight. `/create-ard`, `/specify` and `/update-prd` now fix the run key set from carrier frontmatter once resolution returns `found`, run the preflight, and only then place the folder and take their refusals; `/idea` runs it before it resolves the folder at all. In `/create-ard`, `/specify` and `/update-prd` an address that resolves to nothing still stops before the preflight, as before.
- **`/prd-proposal` and `/brd-proposal` ran the specs-repo preflight before resolving their address** (R81). With no key set stated, the preflight ran keyless, and could switch away from the slice's own unmerged `prd/` branch on an `@<path>` run. Each now resolves the address first (step 2), `@<path>` included, fixes the run key set from what resolution returned and carrier frontmatter alone — the resolved key, and on an Epic-level folder the key its parent's carrier asserts — and runs the preflight (step 3) before its container, slice or Epic refusal or any gate reads the folder; nothing between the resolution and the preflight reads the folder. An address that resolves to nothing stops with no preflight. The docs pages say so.
- **A slice's `coverage-ledger.md` that exists and cannot be read had no row in any remedy table** (M5). `coverage-ledger-format` §5.2 now says it is the same state as a missing one: its rows are `unresolved` (§6.2), neither data refusal can be evaluated, and a consumer names no option and reports the file by path with the read error. `/create-prd`, `/update-prd`, `/epics`, `/idea`, `/prd-proposal`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` widen their missing-ledger rows to it, and the docs pages of `/update-prd`, `/epics`, `/idea` and `/prd-proposal`, which name the missing case, name this one beside it.

## [3.8.2] — 2026-09-23

### Changed
- `docs/reference/model-routing.md`'s fallback chain now starts at `claude-opus-5-5`, matching `workflows-core` 1.7.5.

## [3.8.1] — 2026-09-23

**Update `workflows-core` to 1.7.4 with this release**: its `phase-handoff` §3.4 register row and `next-phase-offer`'s `/brd-reconcile` offers describe the `decisions.md` gate these commands now run; an older one says none of those offers carries `<merge-clause>`, its `grounding-format` defines no `prior_verdict`, the field `/prd-ground` now writes on a superseded finding and `/brd-interview` reads, and its `escalation-rules` does not list `/brd-reconcile`'s new conflicting-answer picker among the pickers whose free-text answer is normalised.

### Changed
- The design-spec citations in `/epics` and in the `brd-format`, `bundle-packaging`, `code-defect-log-format`, `coverage-ledger-format`, `customer-review-schema`, `decision-register-format`, `interview-tagging`, `linked-sources` and `proposal-format` references now say the spec was removed from the tree on 2026-09-23 and name the `git show 62e791e8:<path>` that retrieves it; the section and decision ids they cite are unchanged. Wording only, no behaviour.

### Fixed — behaviour

- **`/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` read a BRD slice's `decisions.md` ungated.** A declined or unmerged handoff of `/brd-reconcile`, `/brd-interview` or `/create-prd` let a PRD, ARD or specification freeze against `[VD#n]`/`[CD#n]` records nobody had merged, and the stated reason for leaving the register ungated — that each command lands its deliverable and the next refuses to start until it is — was false at that hop. Each now runs `require-on-main` on the register in Phase 0 (`/create-prd` step 7a; right after the PRD gate in the other two): a stopping row stops `*_REGISTER_NOT_ON_MAIN`, a register in the folder but on no ref stops `*_REGISTER_NOT_HANDED_OFF`, and a register absent from the folder is reported and the run proceeds as before. `/brd-reconcile`'s offers of all three, `/create-ard`'s `/create-prd <SLICE-KEY>` option and `/specify`'s `/create-prd` recommendation now carry `<merge-clause>`. The command pages, `brd-workflow.md`, `workflow.md` and `docs/commands/brd-reconcile.md` describe the gate, and no longer say nothing downstream refuses an unsettled register. The same reason in `/create-ard` and `/specify` also covered the grounding files, and was false for them too: a `/prd-ground --rebaseline` run, which `/brd-reconcile` sends a challenged code claim to, rewrites them with nothing gating them before these commands. Neither command re-gates them — grounding is optional to both and read as evidence, which is now the stated reason — and Phase 2 reports a findings file it finds uncommitted.
- **`/brd-reconcile` applies the will-change rule (D19) before freezing a `[CD#n]`.** It wrote every confirmed answer `decided` whatever its evidence, so a customer answer resting only on findings a prerequisite is about to falsify entered the register as settled. It now runs `/brd-interview`'s own test on each record it would write `decided`, a completed `open` record included. Where every finding carries `horizon: will-change` the answer is still recorded verbatim, as `conditional_on: <BRD-KEY>/<decision-id>` where every such finding names one prerequisite carrying its BRD key, and as `status: open` where they name more than one or any names an unqualified prerequisite — a bare id is never resolved or parsed into a key, and the value is named for a human — and it is listed under *what still needs a human*. A record held `open` this way closes its question as *answered by the customer*, so its round can close and the later `/brd-interview` round that is its exit can be proposed — unless a propagation sweep's citation pass, reaching it by a changed id it names, withdraws or reverts it first; a record open for want of its reason alone stays held. A later answer against a record the rule held open mints a new `[CD#n]` that supersedes it rather than being written onto it as a missing reason, and a re-run over the same review skips it as already reconciled. `decision-register-format` §6 and its opening consumer paragraph, `docs/commands/brd-reconcile.md` and `docs/reference/references.md` say so.
- **A settled decision a re-grounding moved was never reopened, and the will-change rule's exit did not exist.** `decision-register-format` §4 named a superseding finding as a cause that may reopen a `decided` record, and nothing reopened one: a record whose every finding a `--rebaseline` pass superseded stayed `decided` silently. And a `[VD#n]` the will-change rule held — `open`, or `conditional_on` its prerequisite — had no exit a question source raised once the prerequisite shipped, the `[CD#n]` this release's `/brd-reconcile` holds the same way none either. `/brd-interview` now has one source for both (*A decision the re-grounding moved*). It takes a record every `evidence` finding of which reads `SUPERSEDED` — never one whose `evidence` names no finding (`evidence: []`, or any `[AS#n]`), and never a `[CD#n]` frozen `open` for want of its reason, whose question still travels in the next package. **A superseded finding's successors** are read off the grounding files: a finding of the same prefix, not itself `SUPERSEDED`, whose `claim` opens with the same requirement id, grounded against the same source — for a `[CG#n]` the repository whose `grounding/baselines.md` entry records a pin equal to its `commit`, for a `[DG#n]` the frame set whose index names every frame its `evidence` cites — and minted after it; a test that cannot be decided is not passed, so a sibling repository's or frame set's finding on the same requirement is never a successor. **A held record** is put again once every finding it rests on is `SUPERSEDED`, each has a successor, and no successor still carries `horizon: will-change`: a `--rebaseline` pass supersedes every finding it re-grounds whether or not the prerequisite shipped, and keeps `will-change` on the successor until it does, so the observation that it shipped is a successor no longer `will-change`, and until then the record is reported as waiting on its prerequisite rather than put to the customer on every pass. **A plain `decided` record** is reopened, its `Reopened` paragraph naming the successor findings, unless the successors confirm its premise: every superseded finding carries the `prior_verdict` `/prd-ground` now keeps on it and has a successor, and every successor carries that verdict and the superseded finding's own horizon — so a successor that moved from `will-change` to `current` reopens it, the prerequisite having shipped. One mechanical test for a `[VD#n]` and a `[CD#n]` alike; a record whose successors confirm raises nothing, and one resting on a finding with no `prior_verdict` — superseded before the field existed — is reopened rather than confirmation assumed. The record's question is raised in the next round this run opens, under the tag it had, which never moves, against the successor findings; a re-put `[C]` carries a `- **Re-puts:**` line naming the record, and, for a held record, its still-open `settles` defect, which is how that defect reaches the customer a second time. What the answer does turns on the record's `status` as the answer's writer reads it — `/brd-interview` for a `[VD#n]`, `/brd-reconcile` for a `[CD#n]`, reading the register as it stood before that run wrote anything: `reopened` is re-decided in place; `open` or `decided` is superseded by the new record; `withdrawn` or `superseded`, terminal, does not move, and the new record is written beside it, both named under *what still needs a human*. Where one `/brd-reconcile` run's answers reach one record more than once, a `- **Re-puts:**` line naming it decides it. No other question source raises a question on those successors, whatever their verdict and horizon. *The will-change rule*'s *Defer it until the prerequisite ships* now says the question stays *decided* and its round closes, the record being what waits; it is not the *deferred* holding state. `decision-register-format` §4 and §6, `docs/brd-workflow.md`, `docs/commands/brd-interview.md` and `docs/commands/brd-reconcile.md` state the exits, and say a record written `conditional_on` also leaves by the propagation sweep, which may revert or reopen it in place, and that the sweep's citation pass reaches a held record of either kind that names an id its reconciliation changed — one held `open` included, which it may withdraw or revert. `decision-register-format`'s consumer paragraph and `docs/reference/references.md` said `/brd-reconcile` "writes the `[CD#n]` records — the only command that does", true until `/brd-interview`'s reopen wrote onto one; both now say it *mints* them, and name the reopen among `/brd-interview`'s writes.
- **Superseding a finding kept no record of the verdict it carried**, which the reopen test above needs to tell a re-grounding that came back as it stood from one that moved a decision's ground. `/prd-ground` now writes the finding's verdict as `prior_verdict` (`workflows-core:grounding-format` §2) wherever it marks one `SUPERSEDED` — the `--rebaseline` pass, the class-4 supersession beside it, the Phase 7 sweep's no-design-pass branch, a `--no-code` re-ground of a frame set, and a `contradict` whose re-derived verdict is `SUPERSEDED` — and `/brd-interview`'s test reads it from the grounding files for both registers, never from a `[C]` entry's `- **Findings:**` line, which stays for `/brd-package` to render and count and for `/brd-reconcile` to copy into a `[CD#n]`'s `evidence`. `code-grounder` and `design-grounder` listed `SUPERSEDED` among the verdicts they return; each now says it never emits one, a re-grounding run being what writes it with its `prior_verdict`. `/brd-split`'s and `/brd-interview`'s closed-field-set gates admit the field unchanged, since it is one of §2's. `decision-register-format` §4, `docs/brd-workflow.md` and `docs/commands/brd-interview.md` state the test, and `docs/commands/prd-ground.md` says a `--rebaseline` pass keeps each superseded finding's verdict as `prior_verdict`.
- **`/brd-interview` opened a later round with nothing to ask.** With every round closed it opened round `<highest + 1>` whenever a finding had been added or superseded, a verifier outcome had changed or a decision had moved to `reopened` or `superseded` since the last round closed — and a `--rebaseline` pass supersedes every finding it re-grounds, so a round opened after one could hold no question, and a round record with nothing in it reads as a round nobody finished. It now generates that round's questions first, from what changed (a new *A later round is generated from what changed* rule: the round-1 sources applied to each finding added, or whose verdict or verifier outcome changed, since the last round was generated, less every superseded finding and every successor the reopen source reads, plus that source and the requirement-defect test), and opens the round only where some source puts a question. Otherwise it opens no round and writes no round record, and reports each change and why it raised nothing — a re-grounding as *re-grounded, nothing moved*, naming each record its successors confirmed and each held record still waiting. A decision another command moved to `reopened` or `superseded` raises nothing by that move alone, so `/brd-interview`'s *Work another round now* option, which said to take it "only if findings or decisions have changed", and its nothing-to-review report, which named a decision reopening as a way a round becomes askable, drop the decisions, and `/brd-package`'s `BRD_PACKAGE_NOTHING_TO_REVIEW` no longer says a decision reopened or superseded in `decisions.md` makes a round askable as a `--rebaseline` does. `docs/commands/brd-interview.md`, which said a run finding nothing changed proposes no new round, and `docs/commands/brd-package.md`, which said a decision reopened or superseded in the register can make a round askable, say so.
- **`/brd-interview` anchored what had changed on the last round's record, and so lost a finding changed while that round was open.** It proposed a later round from a finding added or superseded "since that round's record was written", or "since the last round closed", but a round's record is re-written by every run that works it: a finding a `--rebaseline` pass added or changed while that round was still open predated both its last write and its closure, and no later round ever asked about it. The first write of every round record now carries one `generated against:` line — every finding on file under `grounding/` not reading `SUPERSEDED` when the round's questions were generated, each with the verdict and verifier outcome it then carried, or `none` — which no later write adds or edits, and the *Every round is closed* branch compares the grounding against the last round's line: a finding on file that the line does not list and that does not read `SUPERSEDED` was added, one whose verdict or outcome differs from the line changed, and one the line lists that now reads `SUPERSEDED` was superseded, whenever the change happened. A round record written before the line existed is compared at its last write instead — the grounding files as they stood at the commit that last wrote it — which still misses a change made while that round was open, and reads every finding as unchanged where no commit carries the record. *A later round holds only what became askable* said "once the previous round was answered", twice; it now says after the previous round's questions were written. `/brd-reconcile`, which appends to a round record, said only that it never rewrites what the round asked; it now says every line an earlier write put there, the `generated against:` line among them, stands as written. `/brd-package`'s two citations of that test and `docs/commands/brd-interview.md`'s `--round` entry name the new anchor, and that page's round-record entry documents the line.
- **`superseded` named its replacement in no field.** `decision-register-format` §3 said a superseded record names the decision that replaced it, and no writer had a form for it. §4 now fixes a closing `Superseded <YYYYMMDD>: by [XD#m]` paragraph appended to `argumentation`, beside `Reopened`, `Reverted` and `Withdrawn`, the replacement always a record of the same BRD's `decisions.md`, and says superseding moves `status` and adds that paragraph and nothing else. `/brd-interview`, for a `[VD#n]` the will-change rule held, and `/brd-reconcile`'s steps 2 and 3, for an `[AS#n]` and for a `[VD#n]` or `[CD#n]` a later customer answer replaces, write it, and `bundle-packaging`'s list of plugin-written paragraphs a quotation never includes now names it.
- **`/brd-reconcile` reopened any record a frozen answer contradicted or constrained.** Its *Freeze the customer decisions* step 3 wrote `status: reopened` on every record in the slice's register the answer contradicted or constrained without replacing, but `reopened` follows `decided`: a `[CD#n]` frozen `open` for want of its reason, one the will-change rule held `open`, one already `reopened`, and a `superseded` or `withdrawn` one cannot take it. Now only a `decided` record is reopened there, and a record the answer replaces is superseded — one the will-change rule held `open` by any answer to its own question, and a `reopened` one by an answer whose entry does not name it on a `- **Re-puts:**` line, as a corrected resend's does not — while one frozen `open` for want of its reason is completed by an answer to its question, minting nothing, and a `reopened` one a `- **Re-puts:**` line names is re-decided in place. A record not `decided` that the answer contradicts or constrains without replacing keeps its status and is named, with the constraining `[CD#n]`, under *what still needs a human*; a terminal record takes nothing; and one the rule wrote `conditional_on` its prerequisite is `decided` and may take either. Where one run's answers reach one record more than once, the outcome does not depend on the order the candidates are taken in: a `- **Re-puts:**` line naming the record decides it by the status it held before the phase, whatever the other answers do; where no entry names it on such a line, the answer replacing it decides it by that status — superseded by that `[CD#n]`, completed by it where the record was frozen `open` for want of its reason, and nothing where it is terminal; and two or more answers that only contradict or constrain a `decided` record reopen it once, its `Reopened` paragraph naming every one. The reconciliation record's *what changed* lists every `[CD#n]` completed from `open` and every record step 3 left at its status, and its *what still needs a human* every record left at its status, every terminal record a `- **Re-puts:**` line named and every record reached more than once, with every `[CD#n]` that reached it. `docs/commands/brd-reconcile.md` says so.
- **`/brd-reconcile` could freeze two customer answers to one question.** Nothing stopped two candidates of one review, each confirmed onto the same held `[C]` question, `[AS#n]` or escalated `[SR#n]`, from both being frozen, which left *Freeze the customer decisions* two answers replacing one record with nothing to choose between them. *Confirm every candidate* now shows a candidate confirmed onto a target another candidate of the walk already took beside that one, and puts a conflicting-answer picker — freeze this one, keep the earlier one, or ask the customer and freeze neither — triggered by the shared target whether or not the reader flagged a `conflict`. The candidate set aside is not frozen and moves nothing, Phase 5 mints no `[CD#n]` for it, and the reconciliation record names it under *what still needs a human* with the customer's words and the candidate frozen in its place (or that the one kept went back to the customer for its reason); a third candidate onto the same target is put against the one kept, and where none was kept a later candidate goes back to the customer with it; the picker is put before the missing-reason picker, and a candidate that picker already sent back to the customer triggers nothing. `workflows-core` 1.7.4's `escalation-rules` lists the picker among those whose free-text answer is normalised, and `docs/commands/brd-reconcile.md` says so.
- **`/brd-reconcile`'s propagation sweep never swept the dependents of a `[CD#n]` it reopened or superseded, or of a `[VD#n]` it superseded.** Its changed-id set named every `[CD#n]` frozen and every `[VD#n]` reopened in the run, so a `[CD#n]` its step 3 reopened or superseded — and one a `- **Re-puts:**` line re-decided in place, or an answer completed from `open` — and a `[VD#n]` a customer answer superseded moved without a dependent BRD being told. The set now names every `[CD#n]` frozen, completed from `open`, re-decided in place, reopened or superseded in the run, and every `[VD#n]` reopened or superseded.
- **`/brd-reconcile`'s propagation-sweep picker offered writes a dependent's record could not take.** It offered *Reopened* on any `[VD#n]` or `[CD#n]` item, though `reopened` follows `decided`, and all four dispositions on a `superseded` or `withdrawn` record, which is terminal. *Reopened* is now unavailable on an item whose record is not `decided`, the item being named under *what still needs a human* where the change is something it must be decided against; and an item whose record is `superseded` or `withdrawn` is not presented at all — its sweep row is written `inherited-unchanged`, the record terminal and nothing reaching it, and it is named under *what still needs a human* where the change would have moved it. The reconciliation record's *what still needs a human* list, which the sweep already told the run to name an item in where the array dropped the disposition it would have taken, listed no such item; it now lists those and the terminal items, each with the dependent's record, the changed id or prerequisite that reached it, and what the register cannot carry — for a withheld *Reverted*, the earlier position the record no longer preserves or the findings the will-change rule fired on.
- **`/brd-reconcile`'s reconciliation record listed nowhere a row the customer asked to be built that was still `unallocated`.** *Update the coverage ledger* names such a row as `/product-workflows:brd-split <BRD-KEY>`'s — a `--sent` review of a slice never interviewed can find one — but the record's *what still needs a human* listed only a row that already carried a fate. It now lists the unallocated row too, naming `/product-workflows:brd-split <BRD-KEY>` as the run that allocates it.
- **`/brd-reconcile`'s propagation sweep could revert a dependent's decision onto will-change ground unresolved.** `decision-register-format` §4 writes `evidence` and `conditional_on` under §6's will-change rule, but the sweep's `reverted` write never tested the restored `evidence`. It now does, and where the rule fires on a restored position that carries neither a `conditional_on` nor `status: open`, *Reverted* is unavailable for that item and the item is named under *what still needs a human* — a reversion restores and decides nothing, so it cannot pick a resolution for the dependent.
- **The standing-empty-child remedy named a bare `/brd-split <PARENT-KEY>`**, which stops with `BRD_SPLIT_NEEDS_INSTRUCTION` whenever the parent still holds an `unallocated` row. `coverage-ledger-format` §5.2 and the tables in `/idea`, `/prd-proposal`, `/update-prd`, `/epics`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` now take the form from the parent's own ledger: with a slicing instruction where an `unallocated` row remains, bare where none does, and neither — the ledger reported by path — where it cannot be read. `/create-prd` step 7 gains the unreadable case, and `/create-ard`'s array splits in two. The command pages say the same.
- **`/prd-ground`'s `PRD_GROUND_NO_INVENTORY` on a slice named `/brd-split <PARENT-KEY> "<how to cut it>"` unconditionally, as the run that writes the slice's inventory from the rows the parent delegated to it.** A parent run's reconcile step touches a slice's inventory only for the rows its walk moves onto or off the slice, so the rows a slice already claimed are never written back, and on a fully-allocated parent that run carves nothing. The remedy is now a table on the slice's `claims:` and the parent's ledger: a slice claiming nothing takes `PRD_GROUND_EMPTY_INVENTORY`'s remedy; one claiming rows, with no `coverage-ledger.md` in its folder and none of those rows recorded `covered-by` it on the parent's ledger, was never written — a parent split interrupted after writing its `brd-link.md`, a folder no command has committed — and, since a parent re-run writes a slice's rows only for the rows it adds, is sent to remove that folder, in a commit on every branch carrying it where it was committed by hand, the default branch included, and re-run the split with an instruction where a claimed row is still `unallocated`, or to empty the left-over `claims:` by hand and re-run the parent in the form its ledger calls for where none is; one the parent's walk did write lost the file afterwards, and is told to restore it from the ref that carried it, since no parent re-run re-creates the rows it held; and an unreadable parent ledger names no `/brd-split` form. `PRD_GROUND_NEEDS_SPLIT` takes the same table, on two of its three reads — the third, whether a ledger stands in the slice's folder, being the one that stop has already answered: it had no remedy for a slice claiming nothing under a fully-allocated parent — a standing empty child, which a parent re-run does resolve — nor for an unreadable parent ledger. That stop also said the slice's inventory and ledger exist on no ref and in no folder, and that the slice's files were lost and should be restored, where it tests the ledger alone and the inventory may stand; it now names the ledger, says the ledger was lost with the inventory where that is missing too, and asks for what is missing to be restored. Its never-written row adds that removing the folder discards any inventory the interrupted run had copied into it, which loses nothing, the parent's inventory still carrying every row. `docs/commands/prd-ground.md` and `workflows-core`'s `phase-handoff` §3.4 row say so.
- **`/brd-interview` and `/brd-split` treated a slice with no inventory file as a slice with an empty one**, telling the operator the slice claims nothing. A `/brd-split` on the parent interrupted after writing the slice's `brd-link.md` leaves claims with no inventory, and so does a file lost after it was written. The absent case now stops with `BRD_INTERVIEW_NO_INVENTORY` or `BRD_SPLIT_NO_INVENTORY`, each naming both causes, whose remedy is `/prd-ground`'s `PRD_GROUND_NO_INVENTORY` table, cited.
- **`PRD_GROUND_EMPTY_INVENTORY` named a `/brd-split` form even where the parent's ledger could not be read**, and so did its copies in `/brd-interview` and `/brd-split`. Which form runs is that ledger's to say, so each now names neither there, and every remedy table handing that stop's remedy on inherits the case.
- **`/prd-ground` dispatched findings already reading `SUPERSEDED` to its verifier.** A `[CG#n]` a `--rebaseline` pass had superseded is pinned to a commit the repository has left, so the verifier stopped the run with `COMMIT_MISMATCH`, and the `--rebaseline` re-run that stop names dispatched the same finding again and stopped the same way; any other re-derivation returned a live verdict, which the `contradict` branch would write — a retired finding brought back beside its successor. Phase 7 now leaves every `SUPERSEDED` finding out of the dispatch set, whichever run superseded it, and out of the class-4 sweep, its outcome as the retiring run left it. `docs/commands/prd-ground.md` says so, and the command's description, that page and `docs/README.md`, which said it re-derives every finding, now say every live finding.
- **`/create-ard` and `/specify` stopped on a folder `workflows-core:addressing` §4.1 places at no level naming only the folder and what it carries.** Where that folder holds an `idea.md` and no `prd.md` — a legacy idea-route folder handed off before the kind prefixes — the stop now also names `/create-prd <KEY>`, whose `prd.md` places it (`workflows-core` 1.7.4's §4.1).
- **`/brd-proposal`'s readiness walk had no row for a slice whose proposal is current**, so a priced, current slice had no recommendation and no defined question. A fourth row includes it without asking, and the report names it as included rather than as the operator's decision. `docs/commands/brd-proposal.md` covers the stale and the current slice.
- **`/brd-interview`, `/brd-package`, `/brd-reconcile` and `/brd-split` had no refusal for an idea-route PRD folder** — a `PRD-` folder carrying no `brd-link.md`. The three route commands' root refusal accepts every `PRD-` folder, so `/brd-interview` reached an empty-inventory stop naming `/brd-intake`, or, where idea-route `/prd-ground` had written grounding, interviewed a folder with no ledger or inventory; `/brd-package` sent it to `/brd-interview`; `/brd-reconcile --sent` reached its inputs with nothing to reconcile into; and `/brd-split` read the folder as a root owning a source document. Each now stops before any gate — `BRD_INTERVIEW_NOT_A_SLICE`, `BRD_PACKAGE_NOT_A_SLICE`, `BRD_RECONCILE_NOT_A_SLICE`, `BRD_SPLIT_NOT_A_BRD` — deciding the folder by `/prd-ground`'s own `route: idea` test and naming `/create-ard` and `/specify` as the way that route goes on; `/brd-reconcile`'s also names `/update-prd` where the folder holds a `prd.md`, since `/update-prd` stops on one that holds none. Their docs pages, their capture-at-block lists and `brd-workflow.md` say so.
- **`/specify` staged the grounding files and the slice's `decisions.md` at bare paths on a per-Epic run**, where `feature_folder` is the `EPIC-` subfolder and those files sit in the PRD folder above it, so the `consumed_by` stamps went uncommitted. It now names them at `prd_dir`. `/create-ard`'s Epic-level run staged the slice's register the same way, and now names it at the slice folder.
- **`/brd-intake` took any folder its key resolved to for a re-run over a BRD.** `resolve-address` searches every level, so a key naming an idea-route PRD folder or an Epic folder resolved there, and only a slice was refused. Neither holds an inventory, so the re-run confirmation was skipped silently and Phase 2 would copy the customer's document into it, with an inventory and a coverage ledger written beside its `prd.md` or `epic.md`. Phase 0 step 7 now places a found folder as `workflows-core:addressing` §4.1 does and runs only on a root: an Epic folder stops with `BRD_INTAKE_EPIC_LEVEL`, and a `PRD-` folder no BRD carved, or a folder §4.1 places at no level, with `BRD_INTAKE_NOT_A_BRD`, before anything is copied, each leaving the folder as it stands and asking for an unused key. `docs/commands/brd-intake.md` and `brd-workflow.md` say so.
- **`/brd-interview`, `/brd-package`, `/brd-reconcile` and `/brd-split` had no refusal for an Epic folder.** Their level test read only the `BRD-` and `PRD-` prefixes, so an `EPIC-` folder passed it and the idea-route test, then reached a row-F remedy written for a BRD — `/brd-intake` over the Epic from `/brd-interview` and `/brd-split`, `/brd-interview` from `/brd-package` — or, under `/brd-reconcile --sent`, went past the gate with nothing to reconcile into. Each now stops before any gate with `BRD_INTERVIEW_EPIC_LEVEL`, `BRD_PACKAGE_EPIC_LEVEL`, `BRD_RECONCILE_EPIC_LEVEL` or `BRD_SPLIT_EPIC_LEVEL`, tested on the `EPIC-` prefix or, with no prefix, a resolved `kind: epic`. The remedy names the command on the slice above the Epic where there is one, and no command where there is none. The capture-at-block lists, the four command pages and `brd-workflow.md` list them.
- **`/create-ard`'s Epic-level run never read or stamped the grounding it was built on.** Its `grounding/` read, theme seeding, `consumed_by: ARD` stamps, staged paths and final report all tested the resolved folder, which on an Epic-level run is the `EPIC-` folder, and `/prd-ground` never grounds one. They now name `prd_dir`, the PRD folder above the Epic (the slice, on the BRD route), which is how `/specify` stages the same files. The optionality gauge alone still sizes the resolved folder, since it sizes the Epic and not its PRD. `docs/commands/create-ard.md` says so.
- **`/specify` addressed at an Epic never read or stamped the grounding it was built on.** Its `grounding/` read, theme seeding, `consumed_by: specification` stamps, staging condition, Phase 1 display and final report all tested the resolved folder, which on `/specify <EPIC-KEY>` is the `EPIC-` folder — and `/prd-ground` refuses an Epic, so none ever holds a `grounding/`. Phase 0 step 1 now defines `prd_dir` — the resolved folder on a `PRD-` address, the folder above it on an `EPIC-` one — as `/create-ard` does, and every one of those sites, and the BRD route's reads of the slice's `spec-seed.md` and `grounding/baselines.md`, names it. A per-Epic run reached through the picker on a PRD address was unaffected. Phase 0 step 0 also said the BRD route is a `PRD-` folder carrying `brd-link.md` "and nothing else", beside step 3's rule that an Epic addressed inside a slice is on the BRD route; it now names that Epic and reads the route off `prd_dir`.
- **Four stops sent an operator to re-run `/brd-intake` over a folder `/brd-intake` refuses.** `/brd-interview`'s no-parent `BRD_INTERVIEW_EMPTY_INVENTORY`, `/prd-ground`'s no-parent `PRD_GROUND_NO_INVENTORY` and `PRD_GROUND_NEEDS_INTAKE`, and `/brd-split`'s full-mode `BRD_SPLIT_EMPTY_INVENTORY` on a folder carrying neither BRD file all named that re-run, while `/brd-intake` now re-runs only over a BRD container and stops a folder carrying neither file with `BRD_INTAKE_NOT_A_BRD`. Each such stop now names the folder and what it carries, and a fresh intake under a new key — and, where the folder holds an `idea.md` and no `prd.md`, the legacy idea-route folder `workflows-core:addressing` §5 resolves on its name, `/create-prd <KEY>` too, which accepts that folder and writes the `prd.md` a re-run then routes on, as `PRD_GROUND_NEEDS_PRD` and `BRD_SPLIT_NOT_A_BRD` already do for a `PRD-` folder in the same state; `/brd-split`'s full-mode stop keeps the re-run for a container. The justification that such a folder "may be a legacy root BRD whose intake was interrupted" no longer decides the remedy: since 3.7.0 `/brd-intake` writes the inventory's header before it copies anything, and a folder an earlier intake left with `brd/source/` alone carries neither BRD file and is refused by `/brd-intake` too, so the remedy is the same. The stop classifications of `/brd-interview` and `/prd-ground` now file those no-parent forms — and `/brd-interview`'s key, level and idea-route refusals with them — as argument halts, a key naming the wrong folder — `/prd-ground`'s save on a legacy folder holding only an `idea.md`, whose missing input is the PRD. `docs/commands/brd-interview.md`, `docs/commands/prd-ground.md` and `docs/commands/brd-split.md` say so.
- **`/prd-ground`'s `PRD_GROUND_INVENTORY_NOT_HANDED_OFF` told the operator to commit and merge an inventory whose handoff was never offered.** A `/brd-split` on the parent interrupted after writing the slice's `brd/brd-inventory.md` and before its `coverage-ledger.md` leaves exactly that folder, and the stop called it a declined handoff. The stop now fires only where a `coverage-ledger.md` stands in the folder too; otherwise the run takes the ledger's branch, whose never-written row disposes of the inventory. The branch headings for a missing inventory or ledger now say *never produced, or lost after it was written*, and row F is described as conflating three states, not two; `docs/commands/prd-ground.md` and `workflows-core:phase-handoff` §3.4 say the same.

### Fixed — claims the wider exclusivity probe found false

- `/brd-reconcile` Phase 14 said each option "carries its own condition in its own text", `/create-prd`'s the one dropped rather than annotated. Every re-entry option whose trigger did not fire is dropped too; it now says an option whose condition does not hold is dropped, never annotated, and `docs/commands/brd-reconcile.md` agrees. That page also said nothing in the route runs after `/brd-reconcile`: a reopened decision goes to `/brd-interview`, a held question to `/brd-package`, a challenged claim to `/prd-ground --rebaseline`.
- `/brd-reconcile` said a review of a hand-authored package "can never be reconciled by any route". `--sent` exists for exactly that case.
- `docs/commands/brd-reconcile.md` said the command runs again on the same review only once a recorded-not-written dependent has its register on the default branch; a run cancelled or stopped partway is re-run on the same review too, skipping what it already froze.
- `/brd-reconcile`'s propagation sweep said any BRD at any level may declare `depends-on:`, "so … a BRD depending on a sibling" is found. Only a slice declares it — `/prd-ground`, `/brd-package` and `/brd-reconcile`'s own correction phase write the field and all three refuse a root — while the key it names may sit at any level; the scan still searches every level for a hand-edited root. The same passage said a child declares "never `depends-on:`", which a slice's own `--depends-on` falsifies; what is true is that a child is carved with no `depends-on:` naming its parent. `/brd-package`, `docs/commands/brd-package.md`, `docs/commands/brd-reconcile.md` (which also said "at either level") and `docs/brd-workflow.md`'s Parameters table, which said `/brd-package`'s `--depends-on` is repeatable "at either level" in the cell saying only a slice is packaged, are corrected with it.
- `/brd-reconcile`'s propagation sweep said "two of the four" dispositions are unavailable on some items and the array is presented "without the one that is"; on an `[AS#n]` whose earlier position a re-decision overwrote, *Reopened* and *Reverted* both are. It now says the array is presented without whichever of them is unavailable — one, or both — and that a terminal record's item is not presented at all. `docs/commands/brd-reconcile.md`'s list of withheld dispositions named `reopened` only on an assumption and `reverted` only where the earlier position is lost; it now also names `reopened` on a decision that is not `decided`, `reverted` where the restored evidence fires the will-change rule and the restored position carries none of its resolutions, and a terminal record's item. The page also said a withheld disposition's item is named for a human instead; it takes one of the others, and is named for a human as well where the withheld one is what it would have taken.
- `/brd-split`'s Handoff listed five readers of `brd-link.md` as if complete; eighteen commands name it. It now says "among them" and gives the `grep -l` recipe, which lists them. It also cited `commands/design.md` Phase 0, a `dev-workflows` file, now `/dev-workflows:design` Phase 0; said the empty-inventory stops stop on `claims:`, when they count inventory rows; and said an `allocate-only` handoff declares "exactly two" files, when the no-op path declares none.
- `/brd-split` said it runs "at either of the two levels `<BRD-KEY>` can name"; it runs on a root BRD or one of its slices, Phase 0 step 5 refusing an idea-route PRD folder and an Epic folder and step 8 stopping any other folder that is not a BRD container.
- `/brd-split` Phase 0 step 8 said that on a slice "step 6 already covers this before this step is ever reached". Step 6 stops an empty inventory only where grounding is on no ref; a slice ground before its parent's walk withdrew every claim reaches step 8, and the sentence now says what happens to it there.
- `/brd-intake` said three of `BRD_INTAKE_SLICE`'s five slots were substituted; the stop has six and four are. It also said the three seed consumers read their seed only out of the `PRD-` slice and that seeds on the root were read by nothing; each falls back to the parent BRD folder. And it said `/create-ard` and `/specify` on the BRD route derive `ard/<SLICE-KEY>-<slug>` and `spec/<SLICE-KEY>-<slug>`; that is a PRD-level run, and an Epic-level run under a slice derives `ard/<EPIC>-<eslug>` or `spec/<EPIC>-<eslug>`.
- `/brd-interview`'s no-parent empty-inventory stop warned against `/prd-ground` because it "stops on the same emptiness". `/prd-ground` refuses that folder before it reads any inventory, and the stop now says so.
- `/brd-interview`'s and `/brd-split`'s `*_MALFORMED_FINDING` stops, `/brd-interview`'s closing option offering `/prd-ground --rebaseline` and `/brd-package`'s `BRD_PACKAGE_NOTHING_TO_REVIEW` said a `--rebaseline` pass re-derives every finding, or the findings, against current commits. It re-grounds every claim, superseding the findings it replaces, and its verifier re-derives only live ones; each now says it re-grounds every claim against current commits.
- `/brd-interview`'s Phase 8 said its `Cancel` stops the run "with the decision still `open`, which is itself one of the three" resolutions, and Phase 6 that an abort leaves every decision already taken that pass written. Both stop before the register phase, the only writer of `decisions.md` and the round record, so nothing that pass decided is written and the next run puts the question again. Both now say so, and what is already on disk — a held `[C]` entry and a defect line appended to one, which no round record yet names — and how a round this run opened is generated again.
- `/brd-package`'s final report listed "the four artifacts written"; the handoff also lands `brd-link.md` when a prerequisite was added.
- `/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/update-prd` said two of a declined, a push-failed and a nothing-to-commit handoff open no pull request; none of the three does, and §4.1 lists other outcomes.
- `/create-prd` step 7's "Only the second case" and "In the second" pointed at the missing-ledger row, not the empty-gate-set row; its Phase 7 cost key "(or `brd_key`…)" implied a second value, and a commit-prefix note cited a retired "key the handoff has not yet minted". Pre-feature "as today" wording in `/create-prd`, `/create-ard`, `/specify` and `/epics` is reworded, and `/create-ard`'s and `/specify`'s handoffs drop "matching today's branch names", a leftover from before `handoff-to-main` named the branches.
- `/epics` said "there are no PRs to diff"; it has no implementation record and no commit to diff. `/update-prd` said it "reads no BRD tree"; it reads `brd-link.md` and the ledger to word its stops, and now says it reads no BRD tree for content. `docs/commands/update-prd.md` said the same twice, and is corrected with it.
