- `/prd-ground` Phase 11's capture-at-block list omitted Phase 0 stops, and now states the rule first and marks the list as examples; step 2 said every other flag carries over on either route, when `--depends-on` is refused on the idea route.
- `/prd-ground`'s `PRD_GROUND_INVENTORY_NOT_HANDED_OFF` told the operator not to re-run `/brd-intake` because it "would rewrite the inventory and orphan the coverage-ledger dispositions". `/brd-intake` refuses a slice (`BRD_INTAKE_SLICE`) and any folder that is not a container (`BRD_INTAKE_NOT_A_BRD`), and every container is refused as a root before this stop, so the stop and `docs/commands/prd-ground.md` now say it names no `/brd-intake` run for that reason.
- `/brd-interview`, `/brd-package`, `/brd-reconcile`, `/brd-split` and `/prd-ground` said `resolve-address` searches "either of the two levels" — a BRD folder or a slice — and their `*_NOT_FOUND` stops said "(both levels searched)". `workflows-core:addressing` §3 bounds the glob at three levels, and each command's own Epic refusal exists because an `EPIC-` folder resolves. Each lead-in now names the three levels and every folder kind they return, and each stop names every level §3 bounds plus §5's legacy fallback, as `SPECIFY_BRD_NOT_FOUND` already did. `/brd-reconcile`'s dependents scan and slice-folder note, `/brd-split`'s note on a child's key, `brd-workflow.md`, `workflow.md`, and the command pages for `/brd-interview`, `/brd-package`, `/brd-reconcile`, `/brd-split` and `/specify` carried the same two-level claim and are corrected with them.
- `coverage-ledger-format` §5.1's consumer list of the positive-evidence container test omitted `/idea`, `/update-prd` and `docs-workflows`' `/document` (keyed) and `/release-notes`; it adds them, the §4.1 recipe, and `/brd-intake` and `/brd-split`, which take the test without refusing a container: `/brd-intake` through `workflows-core:addressing` §4.1 to accept a re-run, `/brd-split` to refuse the legacy idea-route shape and to word its full-mode empty-inventory stop.
- `bundle-packaging`'s table of qualified structured fields omitted `rejected: <SLICE-KEY>/[CD#n]`.
- `brd-workflow.md`'s Parameters table and `workflow.md`'s handover table omitted `--docs <path>` on every row whose command parses it — five commands, eight rows across the two tables; `brd-workflow.md` said the root key "has no further step of its own", when it still takes `/brd-split`, `/brd-intake`, `/frames` and `/brd-proposal`; and it and `docs/commands/idea.md` now say `/document` reads an image already in `attachments/` but puts new screenshots in `Doc screenshots/`, so `/idea` stays that folder's only writer.
- `docs/reference/resume-and-checkpoints.md` gave the terminal order without follow-ups, twice.
- `docs/commands/brd-interview.md` described its empty-inventory stop as one whose `/prd-ground` "would stop on the same emptiness", named re-intake with a corrected source over a no-parent folder, and `/brd-split` on the parent for every slice with a missing or empty inventory — no parent re-run re-creates the inventory of a slice that claims rows and has lost it — and neither it nor `docs/commands/brd-split.md` named the `BRD_INTERVIEW_NO_INVENTORY` / `BRD_SPLIT_NO_INVENTORY` stops for a slice with no inventory file. Both pages now name all three row-F outcomes and follow `/prd-ground`'s remedy table, and the interview page says `/prd-ground` refuses a no-parent folder before it reads any inventory.
- `docs/commands/prd-ground.md` said `PRD_GROUND_NEEDS_SPLIT` names `/brd-split` on the parent; it does only where that run can still change the slice. The page also named a `/brd-intake` re-run for a "BRD with a source document of its own" at three places no container reaches, sent the legacy no-ledger shape to an "interrupted-intake" branch as though a re-intake were its remedy, and never named `PRD_GROUND_NO_INVENTORY` or `PRD_GROUND_INVENTORY_NOT_HANDED_OFF`; `docs/commands/brd-split.md` named the full-mode `/brd-intake` re-run unconditionally.
- `docs/commands/specify.md` said everything about pickers, Epic counts and the resolved folder describes the keyed route only and that the BRD route "runs none of it"; Phase 2's Steps A and B run on every route, a slice being a PRD folder that can hold Epics. The page now says the route adds to them, and names an Epic inside a slice as a BRD-route address. Its grounding, stamping and `/epics`-offer sentences now name the PRD folder rather than the resolved folder. It also gave the BRD-route branch as `spec/<SLICE-KEY>-<slug>` for every run, where an Epic-level run lands on `spec/<EPIC>-<eslug>`; `/create-ard` and `docs/commands/create-ard.md` said the same of `ard/<SLICE-KEY>-<slug>` and of the slice folder as the feature folder, which an Epic-level run under a slice also falsified.
- `docs/commands/brd-intake.md` said a slice's `brd/` directory is the parent's, one hop up. A slice holds its own `brd/brd-inventory.md`, created by `/brd-split` on the parent; what is the parent's is the source and the records beside it, which is what `BRD_INTAKE_SLICE` itself says.

## [3.8.0] — 2026-09-22

**Update `workflows-core` to 1.7.3 with this release**: its `phase-handoff` register still lists the rendered `.html` among `/specify`'s handed-off artifacts until then — wording only, no behaviour.

### Changed

- **`/brd-reconcile`'s stale cross-reference sweep never edits an effort proposal.** The sweep reads every markdown file under the parent BRD, and a slice's `proposal.md` and `proposal-brief.md`, the umbrella's, and every archived revision of either were in that scope with nothing to stop a hit in their prose being `updated` in place — rewriting a figure's argument in a document the customer may already have been sent, with no archived prior and no record that anything moved. A hit there is now always `needs-a-human` and the file is never edited: the sweep's table carries a row for it, and *what still needs a human* names the fix — a re-run of `/prd-proposal <SLICE-KEY>` for a slice's proposal and `/brd-proposal <PARENT-KEY>` for the umbrella, the slice's first where both carry the hit, since the umbrella reads each slice's figures from that slice's own file; a hit only in an archived revision names no re-run, a revision being history. **Why**: a proposal has its own revision model. The next run of the command that wrote it archives the prior revision and classifies each change as a correction or a re-estimate, corrections listed first and never netted into a total (`references/proposal-format.md` §12) — which an in-place prose edit bypasses entirely. The *Apply the required corrections* phase carries the twin row: a package `/brd-package` builds carries no proposal, but a `--sent` package can, so a section-12 row asking to edit any line of a proposal, its brief or an archived revision is `refused-with-reason`, the file is never edited, and the row names the same re-run and goes to *what still needs a human*. `docs/commands/brd-reconcile.md`'s Phase 6 bullet, which had called `code-defect-log.md` "the one exception" whose prose is not corrected in place, now names the proposal as the second and adds its re-run to the channels a refusal names. `references/proposal-format.md` §0, `docs/roles-and-phases.md`, `docs/reference/proposal-format.md`, both proposal commands and their docs pages now name this scan beside the proposal's own-folder reads, as a prose scan and never a proposal read, and `docs/commands/brd-reconcile.md` adds the proposal to the sweep's never-edited list.

### Removed — `/specify` no longer renders an HTML copy of the specification

Phase 6 used to run `scripts/specification-to-html.py` over the finished `specification.md` and write a self-contained `.html` beside it, and Phase 7 staged that file with the rest of the feature folder. It is gone: the script is deleted, Phase 6 now opens on the `spec-reviewer` dispatch (its steps renumbered 1–2), and neither the files Phase 7 writes nor its `deliverable_paths` name an `.html` any more. **Why it goes rather than growing**: it rendered one of the roughly ten kinds of document this family writes into `$SPECS_PATH`, every one of which is markdown that the forge and the editors people read the specs tree in already render, and `workflows-core:phase-handoff`'s register had classed the file **unread** — handed off, with no reader found. A renderer for one kind was an inconsistency, and one for every kind would be a product nobody asked for. **Population: every `/specify` run.** Existing `.html` files in a specs repository are left where they are — nothing in the family reads them, and `workflows-core:source-truth` and `doc-planner` already ignore any rendered HTML mirror they meet. `specification-format.md` keeps its test-case micro-format and its open-questions heading depths, restated on their own grounds: both were justified by the renderer's parser, and both still bind, because `spec-reviewer`, `/design` and `/implement` read a test case field by field and heading depth is what places an open-questions block under its story or its criterion.

### Removed — `specification-format.md`'s provenance section

The section named the repository the format was first imported from and told a reader to re-sync the file from it by hand. That repository is not public, so the instruction was one no reader of this plugin could follow; the format is maintained here, and `specification-format.md` now says nothing about where it came from. `dev-workflows`' `design-format.md` drops the same comparison (4.2.1).

### Fixed — behaviour

- **`/idea` wrote its deliverable under `/specifications/` at the filesystem root when `$SPECS_PATH` was unset** (behaviour). Nothing checked the variable before `resolve-address` and Phase 4's write, and an empty value put `idea.md` under `/specifications/`. Phase 0 step 1 now stops first with `choices: ["Set SPECS_PATH (enter the path)", "Cancel"]` (`workflows-core:escalation-rules` *Required path environment variable unset*). `docs/commands/idea.md` said the variable was "not needed to start the run at all" and that an unresolvable path was "a silent no-op rather than a stop". It now lists the variable as required and states the stop.
- **`/create-prd` and `/update-prd` accepted an Epic folder** (behaviour). `resolve-address` searches every level, the Epic level included. On an `EPIC-` folder, `/create-prd` took the idea route and wrote `prd.md` into the Epic folder, below the PRD it was split from. `/update-prd` found no `prd.md` and named `/create-prd` on the same address, which then did the same. Population: every run of either command given an Epic key whose `EPIC-` folder exists. `/create-prd` step 5b now stops with `CREATE_PRD_EPIC_FOLDER`, and `/update-prd` step 3a with `UPDATE_PRD_EPIC_FOLDER`. Both test the directory prefix, or a resolved `kind: epic` on an unprefixed folder, and both name the PRD in the folder above as the likely target. Both docs pages now list the stop. The docs pages of `/create-ard`, `/specify`, `/epics` and `/update-prd` now say `/create-prd` refuses "three BRD-route shapes", which is how the commands put it. Where the folder above holds a PRD, the remedy's *re-refine this Epic* clause names `/epics <KEY>` only where the Epic folder holds an `epic.md`, since `/epics` refuses one that does not (`EPICS_NO_PRD`), and `/epics <PRD-KEY>` otherwise; and where the folder above holds none, the remedy says `/epics` reports that shape as `EPICS_EPIC_NOT_UNDER_PRD` or `EPICS_NO_PRD` by the same test, where it had named the first alone. `/prd-proposal`'s matching remedy says the same.
- **`/brd-package` classed `BRD_PACKAGE_REVIEW_UNACCOUNTED` as a stop that does not fire `emit-block`.** That stop means `brd-package-reviewer` broke its return contract twice on a dispatch this command owns. That is an agent-contract gap, the rule `/brd-reconcile` already applies to `BRD_RECONCILE_READER_CONTRACT`. It now fires `emit-block`, beside the three reference-integrity gaps and — with the reclassification below — two record-integrity gaps, six qualifying stops in all. The invariant now states its test as a rule, so every other stop — the bundle-integrity checks that report what the assembled bundle holds (`BRD_PACKAGE_DEAD_CITATION` and the two beside it) among them — is classed by it rather than by a list that had left them out.
- **`/prd-proposal` and `/brd-proposal` accepted an Epic folder** (behaviour). `resolve-address` searches every level, the Epic level included, and neither command refused an `EPIC-` folder. `/prd-proposal` reached its PRD gate, found no `prd.md`, and `PRD_PROPOSAL_NEEDS_PRD` named `/create-prd` with the Epic's key, which refuses that folder (`CREATE_PRD_EPIC_FOLDER`). `/brd-proposal` refused only a `PRD-` prefix, so an `EPIC-` folder passed, held no slices, and `BRD_PROPOSAL_NO_SLICES` named `/brd-split` with the Epic's key. Population: every run of either command given an Epic key whose `EPIC-` folder exists. `/prd-proposal` step 4a now stops with `PRD_PROPOSAL_EPIC_FOLDER`, naming the PRD above as the one to price, in the remedy shape `/create-prd` step 5b and `/update-prd` step 3a use. `/brd-proposal` step 4a stops with `BRD_PROPOSAL_EPIC_FOLDER`, naming `/prd-proposal` for the PRD above and itself for the programme above that, each only where a file on disk records the key. Both test the directory prefix, or a resolved `kind: epic` on an unprefixed folder; both are user halts in the capture-at-block list; both docs pages list the stop.
- **`/specify`'s Epic-level `### Next step` could offer `/create-prd <EPIC-KEY>`** (behaviour). It tested the resolved folder for a `prd.md` and, finding none, named `/create-prd <ADDRESS>`. An Epic folder holds no `prd.md` by construction, and `/create-prd` refuses it. Population: narrow — an Epic-level run whose parent PRD gate took the BRD route's absent branch, an Epic under a slice holding no `prd.md`. The offer now tests the folder Phase 0's PRD gate tested (the parent, on an Epic-level run) and names that folder's own key. `/create-ard`'s matching offer now says an Epic-level array names neither `/epics` nor `/create-prd`, so the Epic folder is never tested. Both now say `/create-prd` refuses "three BRD-route shapes", its Epic-folder refusal being a fourth refusal outside that set.
- **`/epics` sent an `EPIC-` folder with no `epic.md` to `/create-prd`, and `EPICS_EPIC_NOT_UNDER_PRD` said "No command in this plugin authors a PRD above an Epic folder that already exists"** (behaviour). The first fell to `EPICS_NO_PRD`'s idea-route row, which named `/create-prd <KEY>` with the Epic's key; `/create-prd` refuses it. That row is now its own, naming `/epics <PRD-KEY>` where the folder above holds a PRD, `/create-prd <PARENT-KEY>` where the folder above is a `PRD-` folder with no PRD, and no command anywhere else. The second was false where the folder above is a `PRD-` folder holding no `prd.md`: `/create-prd <PARENT-KEY>` authors one there. The stop now names that run in that case, subject to the slice rows of `EPICS_NO_PRD`'s table applied to that folder, and keeps the `git mv` remedy only for a folder with no `PRD-` folder above it. It is also classed as a user halt. The note that step 6 commits nothing now names `_coverage.md` beside the `epic.md` files. The new row names `/epics <PARENT-KEY>` as the run after `/create-prd <PARENT-KEY>`, and no longer says outright that the folder may be removed: the stop says what it holds, and that it may go once the operator has checked nothing in it is wanted.
- **Four stops are now classed as plugin gaps and fire `emit-block`** (behaviour). `/brd-interview`'s `BRD_INTERVIEW_MALFORMED_FINDING` and `/brd-split`'s `BRD_SPLIT_MALFORMED_FINDING` were user halts, but the keys they find in practice are `grounding-verifier`'s return fields, `own_verdict` above all, which reach a finding block only through a plugin command transcribing a return into the record — a record-integrity gap of the plugin's own making, the test `/brd-reconcile` applies to `BRD_RECONCILE_READER_CONTRACT`. `/brd-package`'s `BRD_PACKAGE_CORPUS_UNREADABLE` and `BRD_PACKAGE_SET_UNREADABLE` were classed with the bundle-integrity checks as facts about the bundle, but each stop's own text calls it a parse or read of the plugin's own records failing on content that is there, which no sentence in the package can fix. All four now fire `emit-block`; `/brd-package`'s invariant names six qualifying stops, and `/brd-split`'s no longer says none of Phase 0's stops qualify. `/brd-reconcile`'s `BRD_RECONCILE_EMPTY_DIGEST` was weighed by the same test and still does not fire — its return is well-formed, over the customer's own file rather than a plugin record, and the stop cannot tell a failed parse from a self-contradicting review — and the invariant now says that, where it had called the return a description of the file.
- **`/idea`'s `IDEA_NOT_AN_IDEA_FOLDER` and `/prd-proposal`'s `PRD_PROPOSAL_NEEDS_PRD` named runs that refuse the folder** (behaviour). `/idea` named `/create-prd <KEY>` for every BRD-route slice, though `/create-prd` refuses a slice whose gate set still has an `unallocated` row (`CREATE_PRD_BRD_UNALLOCATED`) or no `covered-here` row (`CREATE_PRD_BRD_NOT_ELIGIBLE`); named `/update-prd <PRD-KEY>` and `/epics <KEY>` for every Epic folder, though they stop `UPDATE_PRD_NO_PRD` where the folder above holds no `prd.md` and `EPICS_NO_PRD` where the Epic folder holds no `epic.md`; and offered `/brd-split` on a container without saying it is a no-op on a ledger with nothing `unallocated`. `/prd-proposal` said *"Author one first: /create-prd <KEY>"* on every folder. Both now resolve the remedy from the gate-set table `/update-prd` Phase 0 step 4 and `/epics` Phase 0 step 1b use, and the Epic rows from the conditionals `/create-prd` step 5b and `/update-prd` step 3a use, so every run a slice or Epic row names can itself take the folder — the `/create-prd <KEY>` after an allocate-only `/brd-split <KEY>` is named only where that walk leaves a claimed row `covered-here`. The BRD-container row is the one exception, and each command says so where it states the rule: it names each slice's `/create-prd <SLICE-KEY>` as where a PRD belongs, not as a run promised to start, and names `/brd-split <KEY>` with its no-op case said beside it. `/prd-proposal`'s Epic-folder remedy applies that table to the parent it names. Both docs pages say so.
- **The slice-remedy tables overlapped, and none covered a missing ledger** (behaviour). The row for a slice with no `covered-here` row and a non-empty gate set did not exclude `unallocated`, so a freshly carved slice with every claimed row `unallocated` matched both it and the `unallocated` row, and that row said nothing moves a terminal row back to `unallocated` — about rows that were never terminal. `/create-prd` tests `unallocated` first, so the `unallocated` row is the right one; the non-empty row now requires no row `unallocated` as well, in `/idea`, `/prd-proposal`, `/update-prd` step 4, `/epics` step 1b, the `/specify` and `/create-ard` next-step tables (and `/create-ard`'s matching array and final-report sentence), and `coverage-ledger-format.md` §5.2's refusal table. No copy covered a slice whose `coverage-ledger.md` is absent while its `brd-link.md` claims rows, which `/create-prd` step 7 and §5.2 answer by naming no command and reporting the missing file; each command table now carries that row, and so does `/brd-reconcile`'s Phase 14 offer test, reachable there on a `--sent` run; §5.2 states the rule the rows cite. The sentences in `/prd-proposal`, `/update-prd` and `/epics` saying the table's stop is the one place the command opens a coverage ledger now also name the Epic-folder stop that applies the table to the folder above.
- **`/create-prd`, `/update-prd` and `/epics` named `/create-prd <PARENT-KEY>` on a `PRD-` folder above an Epic without testing it** (behaviour). Where that folder is a slice, `/create-prd` applies its two data refusals there. `/create-prd` step 5b, `/update-prd` step 3a and `EPICS_EPIC_NOT_UNDER_PRD` now name it subject to the slice rows applied to that folder, as `/idea`, `/prd-proposal` and `EPICS_NO_PRD` already did, so the remedy `/prd-proposal` calls "the one both stops give" is one remedy. The `/create-prd`, `/update-prd` and `/idea` docs pages say the same.

### Fixed — claims the wider exclusivity probe found false

- **`brd-package-reviewer` called `package.defects` "the one input above that is optional".** It refuses only without `brd_dir`, `package.decisions` and `package.grounding`, so seeds, ledger, questions and prior reviews are not required either. It now says `package.defects` is optional, as is every input but those three.
- **`prd-reviewer` said "nothing mints keys any more".** `/brd-split` mints slice keys and `/epics` mints Epic keys. It now says no tracker mints keys, and the keys the plugin mints itself are minted against the same grammar.
- **`proposal-reviewer` called a verdict read as class-1 evidence "the one thing §8 of the format forbids"** — an unnamed format, and neither candidate §8 forbids only that. It now names `workflows-core:grounding-format` §8 and says it forbids exactly that.
- **`coverage-ledger-format.md` §1 called the ledger "the only place a requirement's fate is recorded".** `/brd-split`'s `slices.md` writes down each fate its run assigned, with a rationale. The ledger is now "the only authoritative record" of a fate, and `slices.md` is named as a narrative nothing gates on.
- **`coverage-ledger-format.md` §2 gave `evidence` "exactly one writer once the ledger exists", `/prd-ground`.** A `/brd-intake` re-run over a source-owning BRD rewrites an existing ledger whole and empties the column. The rule is now about the one writer *of a value*, and the re-run is named beside the two creators as writing the column empty.
- **`idea-format.md` called `/idea` "the command that creates `PRD-<KEY>-<slug>/`".** `/create-prd` creates the folder on its first write where none exists, and `/brd-split` creates slice folders. It now says `/idea` creates the folder whenever it is the first command to write there.
- **`docs/commands/brd-split.md` called `/brd-intake` "the only other command that writes" a child's ledger or inventory.** `/prd-ground` writes the ledger's `evidence` and `/brd-reconcile` writes dispositions. It now says `/brd-intake` is the only other command that *creates* either.
- **`docs/commands/create-ard.md` named the next-step offer as "the one place the run does open the ledger".** The final report opens it too, to leave `covered-by` rows' findings out of the unconsumed list. The page now names both places, neither gating the run, as the command does. `docs/commands/specify.md` said "two places" and now names the same three the command does: the zero-Epics choice, the next-step offer and the final report.
- **`docs/commands/create-prd.md` called the sibling re-cut "the one way" an empty slice stops claiming nothing, and offered the bare `/brd-split <PARENT-KEY>` without conditions.** Where the parent's ledger still has an `unallocated` row, the ordinary walk can offer `covered-by` against the slice, and a run with rows to place stops with `BRD_SPLIT_NEEDS_INSTRUCTION` without an instruction. The page now depends on the parent's ledger in the same way `BRD_SPLIT_EMPTY_INVENTORY` does, and names both ways out.
- **`docs/reference/proposal-format.md` said "every other artifact this plugin writes is read by you".** `/brd-package`'s bundle goes to the customer. It now says most of what the plugin writes is read by you, and that the bundle a customer sees comes with a prompt that explains the plugin's records.
- **`docs/reference/model-routing.md` called `/brd-package`'s rendered prompt the one artifact "somebody outside the organisation reads with nobody present to correct it".** A proposal is customer-read too. It now uses the command's wording: the one artifact an outside party pastes into an agent and runs, with nobody from the delivery team present to correct it.
- **`docs/commands/create-ard.md` and `docs/commands/specify.md` opened the BRD route with "No input outside the specs tree".** Both routes read code through `$REPOS_PATH`. The heading is now "No tracker is consulted". `specify.md` also said `SPECIFY_NEEDS_KEY` was "unreachable" because it reported a retired front-end. It is a live Phase 0 stop on a prompt with no address, and the page now says so. Two more stale sites on that page are fixed too: "no non-tracker behaviour" now reads "no direct-prompt behaviour", and "specifying from the export directly" now reads "from the resolved folder".
- **`docs/commands/create-prd.md` and `docs/commands/update-prd.md` said no command consumes `brd_key`, `brd_parent` or `depends_on`.** `/brd-proposal` reads `depends_on` for slice order, and `prd-reviewer` checks the `brd_key`/`brd_parent` pair. Both pages now say no command of the build ladder reads any of the three, and name those two readers.
- **This changelog's 3.6.0 entry said the "four-part stop contract" `/prd-ground` cited "exists nowhere in the tree".** Its recipe, `grep -rn 'stop contract'`, was case-sensitive and missed `workflows-core:phase-handoff` §4.4 *Stop contract*, which was in the tree then. The entry keeps its text and now carries a note: that contract governs only `require-on-main` stops, so the cut pointer stays cut, and the recipe should be run as `grep -rni`.
- **Three present-tense claims in older entries, now annotated, their original text kept:** 3.4.0's "the one reader is the sibling umbrella `/brd-proposal`" carries a *(Corrected in 3.8.0: …)* note saying it is the one command that reads *another folder's* proposal as a proposal (`/brd-reconcile`'s stale cross-reference sweep reads one only as prose). 3.3.1's "`prd.md`, the only name the plugin writes" carries one saying it is the only canonical PRD name, because `/update-prd` also archives under `revisions/`. 3.6.0's "the one place that name is re-derived" carries an *(at this release; …)* note, because 3.7.0 moved the rule to `brd-format.md` §1.1, which has two readers.
- **`/prd-ground` said grounding "happens at the slice and nowhere else"** — in its opening and in Phase 0 step 5a's heading — though the same step grounds an idea-route PRD folder. Both now say a root BRD is never ground and grounding happens at a `PRD-` folder, a slice or an idea-route PRD folder. `/brd-split` steps 6 and 7 carried the same sentence and now say grounding happens at a `PRD-` folder, on that route a slice. The command's docs page says the same.
- **The root-level stops of `/prd-ground`, `/brd-interview`, `/brd-package` and `/brd-reconcile` said a leftover root-level artifact "is left in place and nothing reads it".** `/brd-proposal`'s defect sweep and `/brd-reconcile`'s stale-cross-reference sweep read root artifacts. Each stop now says the command itself never reads it (`/brd-reconcile`: never reconciles against it).
- **Five refusal-inference sentences, in three commands, called a gated file "written exclusively by" one command.** `grounding/code-grounding.md` (`/brd-interview`, one site) is also stamped `consumed_by` by `/create-ard`, `/specify` and `/update-prd`; and `customer-review-prompt-<date>.md` (`/brd-reconcile`, three sites) is bannered by `/brd-reconcile` itself. Each of those four now says the file is *created* only by its producer, names the later writers, and keeps the inference, since every one of them also refuses a root. The fifth, `/brd-package`'s, is about `decisions.md`, which `/create-prd` (an `[AS#n]`, and its own `consumed_by` stamps), `/brd-reconcile` and the `/create-ard` and `/specify` stamps also write; it now names every command that writes the file — `/brd-interview` among them — and says each refuses a root, which is what the inference needs.
- **`/brd-package` called the rendered customer prompt "the one artifact … a person outside the organisation reads without anyone present".** A proposal is read by the customer too. It is now the one artifact an outside party pastes into an agent and runs, with nobody from the delivery team present — a proposal is read, not run.
- **`/create-ard` named Phase 7's offer as "the one place this run does open" the ledger, and `/specify` said "two places".** Both Final reports open the ledger as well, to leave `covered-by` rows' findings out of the unconsumed list. `/create-ard` now names two places and `/specify` three, none gating the run. `/brd-reconcile`, which said neither command reads the claims or the ledger "at all", now says not as an authoring input.
- **`/create-ard`, `/specify` and `/epics` said, in their container-refusal remedy, that "this command reads no coverage ledger".** Each opens a ledger elsewhere (the offers and Final reports above; `/epics` in one data stop). Each now says the *remedy* opens none. `docs/brd-workflow.md` (the parameter table and the next-step paragraph), `docs/workflow.md`'s handoff table and `docs/roles-and-phases.md` said `/create-ard` and `/specify` read no ledger, and now say neither reads or authors from it.
- **`/create-prd`'s empty-gate-set row called the sibling re-cut "the one way" an empty slice stops claiming nothing, and named the bare `/brd-split <PARENT-KEY>` unconditionally.** The ordinary walk can offer `covered-by` against the slice where the parent still holds an `unallocated` row, and a run with rows to place stops with `BRD_SPLIT_NEEDS_INSTRUCTION` without an instruction. The row now splits on the parent's ledger and names the instruction form where one is needed.
- **`/brd-split` called the re-cut the only path on which a standing child's ledger enters `deliverable_paths` because a row was taken off it.** An ordinary walk after a cancelled run can give a still-`unallocated` row of a provisionally keyed slice to a sibling. It is now the only path that takes off a row the child had *committed* to, its ledger reading `deferred-to`.
- **`/brd-split` Phase 0 step 5 called `brd-link.md`'s `parent:` "the same signal `/prd-ground` uses to tell a slice from a root, and the only reliable one".** `/prd-ground` tells a slice from a root by the directory prefix and reads `parent:` at step 6. The step now says so, and calls `parent:` reliable unlike a segment count. The command's docs page, which said "the only reliable signal", now says the same.
- **`/brd-split` called `--no-design` the one way a slice reaches build with a frame set unreconciled.** The pre-section pass can let one through unknown. It is now the one way with a frame set *known* to be unreconciled.
- **`/brd-intake` called a revised source's copy "the one thing a later intake writes again".** `brd-format` §1's rule is about when a copy under `brd/` is written again; the command now quotes it: a re-run over a revised source is the one time.
- **The PRD's single filename.** `/create-ard` and `/specify` gated "the only PRD name", `/create-prd` called `prd.md` "the only name this plugin writes", and `/update-prd` (its description and step 4) called it "the only copy"; `/update-prd` archives dated copies under `revisions/`. Each now says the canonical name, or the only *current* copy, and names the archive as never read as a PRD. The `/update-prd` docs page carried the same "only copy there is" and now matches.
- **`/create-ard` and `/specify` said grounding "on this route is optional and nothing gates on it".** On the BRD route `/brd-split` and `/brd-interview` gate on grounding. Both commands, and both docs pages (which said "grounding upstream … is optional"), now say it is optional to this command and nothing in this run gates on it.
- **`/update-prd` said no command consumes `brd_key`, `brd_parent` or `depends_on`.** `/brd-proposal` reads `depends_on` for slice order and `prd-reviewer` checks the pair. It now says no command of the build ladder reads any of the three.
- **`/prd-proposal` said "nothing reads" the brief or the archived revisions.** `proposal-reviewer` reads the brief inside the same run. It now says no later run reads either. Its sibling-census paragraph used *current* without defining it; it now gives `/brd-proposal` Phase 3's modification-time test and says that test opens no sibling's proposal.
- **`/prd-proposal` and `/brd-proposal` grill a proposal profile without declaring a depth**, which `workflows-core:grilling-technique` requires of every caller. Both now declare it relentless, done when every profile field has been asked and none is left as a recorded gap, and unable to close while `roles`, `productivity` or `calendar` lacks an answer — the three a team, a schedule and a productivity basis are stated from. `/brd-proposal`'s copy points at the profile `/prd-proposal` Phase 2 defines, since it carries no profile block of its own.
- **`/idea` said `grounding-format` §6.1 makes a missing frame-set index "unrecoverable".** §6.1 makes it a refusal, recovered by writing an index — `/frames`, or by hand. The sentence now says so.
- **The `/brd-package` docs page was headed "Everything it emits is read outside your organisation".** The run also writes an adversarial self-review that never leaves the folder. The heading now says *sends*, and the paragraph names what stays behind.
- **`docs/roles-and-phases.md` called `/dev-workflows:ready` "the one caller allowed to keep going past a stop like this", and said an absent artifact always falls back.** This plugin's `/brd-reconcile` also reads a stopping row without stopping, at two sites and never at its ordinary Phase 0 gate — its cross-BRD write guard, and a `--sent` run's redundancy check at Phase 0 step 6, which reads the gate inverted (`workflows-core:phase-handoff` §3.7) — and the commands whose gated input was never optional stop on its absence (§3.4). `/ready` is now the one caller whose own gate never stops it, `/brd-reconcile`'s two sites are named, and the absent-artifact bullet names the §3.4 exceptions.
- **`/brd-reconcile`'s next-step offer said `/create-ard` and `/specify` "neither reads outside the specs tree", and its docs page's offer table said each "reads only the specs tree" and reads neither `claims:` nor the ledger.** Both reach code through `$REPOS_PATH`, and both open the ledger to shape their offers and report. The command now says neither consults a tracker and each resolves its own code reads, and the table says each consults no tracker and reads neither `claims:` nor the ledger as an authoring input.
- **`docs/brd-workflow.md`'s parameter table said of `/prd-ground` "Only a slice is ground".** An idea-route PRD folder is ground too. The row now says a root BRD is never ground and grounding runs at a `PRD-` folder — a slice, or an idea-route PRD folder.
- **`/create-prd`'s empty-gate-set row said an instructed `/brd-split` run "can instead re-cut", and `/prd-ground`'s matching stop said the same**, while `docs/commands/create-prd.md` said *additionally*. The instructed run still runs its Phase 4.5 on the re-cut path and can offer to remove or keep the slice, so both commands now say *additionally*.
- **`code-defect-log-format.md` named `brd-package-reviewer` as the log's one reader.** `/brd-package`'s Phase 0 gate, `/brd-reconcile` (its inputs and its propagation sweep), `/prd-proposal`'s defect sweep and `proposal-reviewer` read it too. The header now names them as "among them" and gives a `grep -l` over the plugin's commands, agents and references that lists every file naming the log, saying it is no list of readers — `/prd-proposal` reads the log through `references/proposal-format.md` §7 and its own command file never names it. `docs/reference/references.md`'s entry for the file now names the same readers, `/brd-package`'s Phase 0 gate among them.
- **`bundle-packaging.md` named `/brd-package`, `brd-package-reviewer` and `/brd-reconcile` as its consumers.** `/brd-interview` and `/prd-ground` cite its §1.1, §6.2 and §6.3 for what they write that a bundle later carries. The header now names both and gives the `grep -l` recipe. Its list of "every other working record" excluded from a bundle, which omitted several such files, now reads "among them".
- **`coverage-ledger-format.md` §3.2 said `/brd-split`'s Step 3 "today visits only rows that were `unallocated`", calling the widening to a re-cut row "the command's change to make".** The command made it: Step 3 visits the rows Step 2R re-pointed too. The paragraph now says so.
- **`coverage-ledger-format.md` said a slice's `covered-by` row "is written by the parent's Phase 4 walk".** Phase 4.5's removal repair also writes `covered-by: <PARENT-KEY>` onto another child's row. It now names both.
- **`coverage-ledger-format.md` §3.1 said every consumer that puts a requirement "in front of a human" never reads a `covered-by` row.** The sibling re-cut puts one in front of the operator as an allocation. The rule is now about putting a requirement to a customer or into an interview round, and the re-cut is named beside it.
- **`coverage-ledger-format.md` said "two commands write a disposition once the ledger exists" and "exactly two commands write" the BRD bookkeeping.** `/brd-intake` re-run rewrites an existing ledger (the paragraph's own exception), and `/prd-ground` and `/brd-reconcile` write into a ledger. They now say two commands *allocate or settle* a row and two commands *create* the bookkeeping.
- **`coverage-ledger-format.md` §5.1 said only `/prd-ground`, `/brd-split` and `/brd-package` ever write `brd-link.md`.** `/brd-reconcile` also adds a `depends-on:` entry where a returned review's correction asks for one (its *Apply the required corrections* table's `brd-link.md` row: "a row asking to add one is `applied`"). It now names four, all but `/brd-split` writing only `depends-on:`; the argument the sentence serves is unchanged.
- **`coverage-ledger-format.md` §5.1's consumer paragraph listed product-workflows' consumers only.** `dev-workflows`' `/design`, `/implement` and `/ready` take the same positive test through `workflows-core:addressing` §4.1. They are now named, with a note that the paragraph's recipe reaches them only through their remedy text.
- **`decision-register-format.md` named the register's writers and three readers as its consumers**, while its own §1 `altitude` row names `/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` as readers. The header now names those and the other readers, and gives the `grep -l` that lists every command and agent naming the register, saying it is no list of readers — `customer-review-reader` is handed the register by path and never names it.
- **`interview-tagging.md` omitted `/create-prd`**, which applies §2's test to triage a gap its grill could not close, **and `customer-review-reader`**, which cites §2. Both are named now.
- **`proposal-format.md` said "a `decisions.md` has exactly two writers", `/brd-interview` and `/brd-reconcile`.** `/create-prd` writes an `[AS#n]`, and the build ladder stamps `consumed_by`. It now says only those two put a decision in it, and that every other write also happens only on a BRD slice, so the tier-1 cap holds either way.
- **`proposal-format.md` §11 said "every other artifact the plugin writes is read by the operator or pasted into a tracker".** `/brd-package`'s bundle goes to the customer. It now says most is read by the operator, and that what a customer sees arrives with a prompt explaining the records. This matches the docs page that round 1 fixed.
- **`epic-reviewer` said three dimensions are conditioned on `refinement_targets`.** *Epic independence*'s refinement-mode exception reads it too. It is now named as a fourth.
- **`docs/workflow.md` said all twelve BRD edges appear "unchanged, in style and in label".** The two edges through `/prd-ground` carry different labels from `docs/brd-workflow.md`'s. It now says so, and says the labels do not contradict each other. The same page said `/create-ard` and `/specify` add no condition because "neither reads a resolved folder, gates a PRD, or reads the ledger". It now uses the reason round 1 wrote into `docs/brd-workflow.md`.
- **`docs/brd-workflow.md` said nothing in the handover box "opens a question" and that each "only reads what this route already wrote".** `/create-prd` writes an `[AS#n]`, and all three read `$DOCS_PATH` (`/create-ard` and `/specify` also read `$REPOS_PATH`). The box paragraph now says so. Its *What lands where* tree drew every artifact at the root's level. It is now split: the root holds `brd/`, the ledger, `slices.md`, `design/` and bookkeeping, and grounding, the register, the interview, the package and the reconciliation sit in the `PRD-` slice. A note covers a root written before grounding moved. The tree's `brd-link.md` and `decisions.md` comments now name `/brd-reconcile` and `/create-prd` as writers.
- **`docs/reference/references.md`**: the `coverage-ledger-format.md` entry said §5.1 is shared "by the four commands that refuse a container", which is more than four. It now points to §5.1 for the list. The `brd-format.md` entry adds `figure-reader` and `/brd-package`, and the `decision-register-format.md` entry adds `/create-prd`'s `[AS#n]`.
- **`docs/roles-and-phases.md` gave the companion plugins "five more lifecycle phases — three and two", and its Dev paragraph listed no `docs-audit`.** `workflows-core:cost-emission` §7 gives `docs-workflows` three (`documenting`, `docs-scaffold`, `docs-audit`). Both sentences now say six, and the page cites §7.
- **`docs/reference/agents.md` said three further agents ship elsewhere.** Commands also dispatch `workflows-core:doc-fixer` and `prose-style:prose-style-checker`. It now names five and the plugin each ships in.
- **`docs/commands/brd-split.md` called `/brd-split` "the only command of the route that runs more than once"** — every route command re-runs. It now says the only one that runs at both levels. It also counted "the three stops" naming a bare re-run, which is more than three; the count is dropped. It called the re-cut "the one case" the command re-allocates a fated row, twice, while Phase 4.5's removal repair rewrites fated rows too. Both sites, and the same claim on `docs/commands/prd-ground.md`, now name that repair as the exception.
- **`docs/commands/prd-ground.md` said the `prd` prefix is shared with `/create-prd` and `/update-prd`.** `/prd-proposal` opens on it too. It is named now.
- **`docs/commands/create-prd.md` called the `consumed_by` stamp "the only write it makes into any BRD file"**, which the same page contradicts with the `[AS#n]` it writes. It is now the only edit to an *existing* record, and the page says a new `[AS#n]` may be added.
- **`docs/commands/brd-interview.md` said a row "its holder is still committed to is moved by no command".** `/brd-reconcile` moves rows on a frozen `[CD#n]`. It now says no command re-points such a row onto another slice.
- **`docs/commands/epics.md` described seventeen commands as offering "a branch + commit + push + pull-request handoff for their own deliverable".** That set omits `/vuln`, `/upgrade` and the docs commands, which draft pull requests too. The seventeen it names are the commands that hand their deliverable onto the specs repo's default branch through `handoff-to-main`, and it now says that.
- **`docs/commands/update-prd.md` called `/update-prd` "the one authoring command deliberately excluded from `require-on-main`".** `/idea` and `/brd-intake` never run it either. It now names both, and says it is the one authoring command that works from a PRD earlier phases wrote while gating on none of them.
- **`docs/getting-started.md` said the marketplace also ships `obsidian-llm-wiki` and `acli`.** It ships `guideline-reviewers` too. It is named now.
- **Seed files had "one writer" on the `/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` docs pages** (`/brd-intake --sort-existing`). `/brd-reconcile` corrects a seed's prose in place. Each page now calls `--sort-existing` the one command that *creates* a seed, and says `/brd-reconcile` only corrects one. `docs/commands/create-prd.md`'s container bullet likewise says `/brd-intake` and `/brd-split` *create* the ledger and inventory, rather than being their only writers.
- **`docs/commands/brd-split.md` said that on an empty root `/prd-ground` "would stop on the same emptiness".** `/prd-ground` refuses a root outright (`PRD_GROUND_ROOT_LEVEL`). The page now says that.
- **`docs/commands/brd-reconcile.md` said a decision moves "only through the four sweep dispositions or the two reopening causes".** That leaves out the command's own freeze and the `consumed_by` stamps `/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` write. The page now names both.
- **`coverage-ledger-format.md` §5 said "only the parent's walk writes" a slice's orphan row.** The parent's Phase 4.5 removal repair writes one too. It now names the parent's *run*: its walk, or that repair.
- **`docs/commands/epics.md` called the positive BRD test "the one rule `/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` share".** More commands share it. It now says every command that tests a folder's level shares it.
- **Three `/brd-intake` claims.** Its empty-root stop said `/prd-ground` stops with `PRD_GROUND_EMPTY_INVENTORY` and that `/brd-split` and `/brd-interview` "stop the same way". On a root, `/prd-ground` and `/brd-interview` refuse outright (`PRD_GROUND_ROOT_LEVEL`, `BRD_INTERVIEW_ROOT_LEVEL`), and `/brd-split` is the command that stops with an empty-inventory stop, `BRD_SPLIT_EMPTY_INVENTORY`. The stop now says so. `BRD_INTAKE_SLICE` said a slice's inventory and ledger are "for `/brd-split` to write"; other commands write the ledger later, and they are now *created by* `/brd-split` on the parent. The branch-prefix note said `brd` is shared by every `/brd-*` command; `/prd-ground` takes it on the BRD route too, as `workflows-core:phase-handoff` §2.9's `prefix` row says. It also counts no container refusals now.
- **`/brd-split`'s full-mode `BRD_SPLIT_EMPTY_INVENTORY` said `/prd-ground` "stops on the same emptiness"** — the command's own copy of the claim the docs page carried. `/prd-ground` refuses a root outright. The stop now says that. The same command also said "three stops" name it as the fix for an empty child, and that "only the parent's walk" writes a slice's orphan row. More stops and offers name it, so the count is dropped. Phase 4.5's removal repair writes orphan rows too, so both sites now name the parent's run. It also said `/brd-split` "is the only `/brd-*` command that can ever change" the `unallocated` term of the no-op test. `/brd-reconcile`'s ledger phase takes a row out of `unallocated` on a frozen `[CD#n]`, so it now says `/brd-split` alone puts a row in, and names what else takes one out. Its re-cut paragraph now names the key repair a removal performs as the other case in which it rewrites a fated row, as its docs page already does.
- **`/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` named the root key in the routes for a contradicted decision.** They told the operator to run `/prd-ground <BRD-KEY> --rebaseline`, `/brd-interview <BRD-KEY>`, `/brd-package <BRD-KEY>` and `/brd-reconcile <BRD-KEY>`. Every one of those four refuses a root. Each route now names `<SLICE-KEY>`, and `--rebaseline` only where the pinned code has moved. `/create-prd`'s feedback emitter and Final report now name the slice key too.
- **`/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` said only `/prd-ground`, `/brd-split` and `/brd-package` ever write `brd-link.md`.** `/brd-reconcile` merges a `depends-on:` too — the command-side copies of the `coverage-ledger-format.md` §5.1 claim fixed above. The argument they serve is about a root, and no command writes a `brd-link.md` into a root: `/brd-split` places one in each slice it carves, and every command that merges `depends-on:` refuses a root. Each now says that. `/create-ard` also listed `grounding/`, `interview/` and `decisions.md` in a `BRD-` folder, and `/specify` and `/epics` listed `grounding/`; under the current model those sit in a slice, and at root only in a tree written under the earlier two-level model. `/create-ard` now says that and the other two drop the entry. `/create-ard`, `/specify`, `/epics`, `/prd-proposal` and `/brd-proposal` named a closed set (three commands, or the family's — four, in `/prd-proposal`) as the takers of `coverage-ledger-format.md` §5.1's test, and now say every container or root refusal in this plugin takes it, directly or — as `/idea`'s does — through `workflows-core:addressing` §4.1, which cites it; `/prd-ground`, `/brd-interview`, `/brd-package` and `/brd-reconcile` now scope their "every consumer of this test" to this plugin, and say a consumer takes it directly or through `workflows-core:addressing` §4.1. The three authoring commands' seed notes now say `--sort-existing` *creates* a seed, as their docs pages already do.
- **`/create-ard` and `/specify` said the idea route has no grounding.** Each said the folder stays at tier 1 because "neither grounding nor a settled register exists", or "until the folder is grounded". `/prd-ground` runs on an idea-route folder too. Tier 2 also needs a settled register, which nothing on the idea route produces, so both now say the folder stays at tier 1 for that reason.
- **`/create-prd` said four other stops name it as the run that creates a `PRD-` folder.** `/epics`' `EPICS_NOT_FOUND` and `/prd-ground`'s `PRD_GROUND_NOT_FOUND` do too. The count is dropped and both are named.
- **Four `/prd-ground` claims.** It said `/create-ard` and `/specify` read `prd.md` "from the specs repo's default branch, never from a working tree". Each gates `prd.md` with its own `require-on-main`: one on a branch stops them, and one on no ref is reported as no authored PRD. It now says that. It called its verifier's non-`OK` statuses "the four statuses", and there are more; it now says every status other than `OK`. It called `/create-ard` and `/specify` "the two consumers" of what it gated and wrote, which other readers falsify — `/prd-proposal` grades on the same grounding; it now calls them the two downstream authors. A pointer "thirty lines below" now names the rule it points at.
- **`/specify` named "four" of its stops as user halts in its capture-at-block invariant.** It has more. The sentence now says every `SPECIFY_*` stop is argument- or tree-shaped.
- **Three `/brd-interview` claims.** It said "both rules" that read a question's state share one vocabulary. `/brd-package`'s gate on the rounds and `/brd-reconcile`'s closing of a round read it too, so it now says every rule. It said the `- **Requirement defect:**` line is the one "both readers" take. `/brd-package` renders it into the customer's copy of the question as well, so it now says every reader. Its capture-at-block invariant listed its user-halt stops and left out `BRD_INTERVIEW_BAD_ROUND`, `BRD_INTERVIEW_NO_FINDINGS` and `BRD_INTERVIEW_MALFORMED_FINDING`. It now states the test as a rule, so a stop added later is classed by it, and names the first two; the third, `BRD_INTERVIEW_MALFORMED_FINDING`, now qualifies under that rule (*Fixed — behaviour*).
- **Four `/brd-reconcile` claims.** Its *Reject* paragraph said "This command mints no `[C]`, writes nothing into the question set, and closes no round". The command as a whole closes each `[C]` it froze an answer to and a round those answers settle, so the sentence now names *Reject* as its subject. Its *Apply the required corrections* row said `depends-on:` is merged by "two other commands"; they are now named (`/prd-ground`, `/brd-package`), and the row says it is the one field of `brd-link.md` this command writes. Its register row said a record's fields move only through the sweep's dispositions and §4's reopening causes; it now names the command's own freeze and the `consumed_by` stamp as well, as its docs page already does. Its capture-at-block invariant listed its user-halt stops and left out `BRD_RECONCILE_SENT_NOT_READABLE`, `BRD_RECONCILE_SENT_REDUNDANT` and `BRD_RECONCILE_EMPTY_DIGEST`. It now states the test as a rule and names them.
- **`/prd-proposal` said three times that `/brd-proposal` is "the one command that reads another folder's proposal".** `/brd-reconcile`'s stale cross-reference sweep reads every proposal under the parent BRD too, as prose. All three now say *as a proposal*, which is the distinction the sentences beside them already drew; 3.6.0's quotation of `proposal-format.md` §0 carries a correction note saying the same.
- **`/create-ard` and `/specify` offered `/prd-proposal <KEY>` in their commercial-step prose.** On an Epic-level run `<KEY>` is the Epic's, which `/prd-proposal` step 4a refuses. Both now name `<PRD>`, the PRD folder's own key.
- **`/create-ard`, `/specify`, `/epics`, `/prd-proposal` and `/brd-proposal` said every container or root refusal in this plugin takes `coverage-ledger-format.md` §5.1's test from that section**, and `/prd-ground`, `/brd-interview`, `/brd-package` and `/brd-reconcile` said every consumer of the test does. `/idea`'s `IDEA_NOT_AN_IDEA_FOLDER` takes it through `workflows-core:addressing` §4.1. Each now says *directly or through* §4.1.
- **`/create-ard`'s next-step precondition put a clause about Epic-level runs between the test and its two outcomes**, so *where there is one* read as though it meant an Epic-level run. The clause now follows the outcomes. `/create-prd` step 5a now says, as step 5b already did, that the refusal is taken ahead of step 3's ladder.
- **`/brd-proposal` step 4a said step 4 "refuses only a `PRD-` prefix"** — true only among prefixed folders; step 4 also refuses an unprefixed slice by positive evidence. It now says so. Its report paragraph named "Price the slice first" as the answer that ends the walk; "Re-price it first" ends it too, and is named now.
- **`/specify`'s handoff `body_facts` named "the `<BRD-KEY>` this specification was seeded from".** `/specify` runs on a slice and reads its seeds from the slice folder. It now names the `<SLICE-KEY>`.
- **`docs/brd-workflow.md` said no command writes the retired root-level artifacts "any more".** A slice's `/brd-reconcile` stale cross-reference sweep reads every markdown file under the parent and can update an argumentation in a leftover root `decisions.md`. It now says no command *creates* them, and names that edit. The tree's root `design/` line now says `/frames` indexes it and no grounding reads it, a root never being ground.
- **`/brd-split`'s capture-at-block invariant was still a closed list, and left out `BRD_SPLIT_NEEDS_INSTRUCTION` and `BRD_SPLIT_NO_FINDINGS`.** It now states the test first, as `/brd-interview`'s, `/brd-package`'s and `/brd-reconcile`'s do — every stop but `BRD_SPLIT_MALFORMED_FINDING` reports the operator's argument, the tree or the environment and is classified by that test, never by a list — and names each of its `BRD_SPLIT_*` stops by its class, with the operator's `Cancel` halts in Phases 1.5, 2, 3 and 4 beside them as user halts.
- **`docs/commands/epics.md` said a stand-alone `EPIC-` folder is refused with `EPICS_EPIC_NOT_UNDER_PRD`.** Only where it holds an `epic.md`; one holding none is refused with `EPICS_NO_PRD` wherever it sits. The page now says so and describes that stop's own row for an empty `EPIC-` folder; the command's opening summary and its *NEVER partition* invariant, which said the same, now name both stops, and the page's input bullet names the empty folder among the refused shapes. The command's frontmatter description and Phase 0 step 1b's lead, which said `/epics` accepts "an EPIC- folder that has a PRD above it" and listed two refused shapes, now say an `EPIC-` folder holding its `epic.md`, and name the empty one among what is refused.
- **`docs/brd-workflow.md`'s tree put `attachments/` and `design/idea-sources/` "/idea vendored" inside a `PRD-` slice.** `/idea` refuses a BRD-route slice (`IDEA_NOT_AN_IDEA_FOLDER`), so nothing on this route writes either. The tree keeps `attachments/` as a reserved name no command writes here, and the paragraph beneath it says why.

## [3.7.0] — 2026-09-22

Design authority: `docs/superpowers/specs/2026-09-18-brd-figures-and-appendices-design.md`, whose §14 — one link walk shared by `/brd-intake` and `/idea` — governs where it and an earlier section of that file disagree. Every claim below is read off the shipped commands, agents and references; where the spec proposed something the tree does differently, the tree is what is recorded. **Update `workflows-core` to 1.7.1 with this release**: two of its references now name `figure-reader` and the two artifacts this release adds, one of them now defines a frame-set index row's `Linked from` the way `/idea` writes it, `escalation-rules` names the form of `/brd-reconcile`'s confirmation picker for an answer matching no question, `addressing` §4.1 now owns how a folder is placed at a level — the rule `/create-ard`, `/specify`, `/idea` and `/prd-ground` cite, and the definition of a prefix the container refusals of `/create-prd`, `/epics`, `/prd-proposal` and `/brd-proposal` take — `implementation-format` §1 now says where the implementation record `/specify` reads its candidate repositories from lives, and a machine updating this plugin alone reads the old wording beside the new behaviour. **One of the dependencies is behaviour, not wording**: this release gives `coverage-ledger.md`, which `/brd-intake` writes at a root BRD's top level, frontmatter carrying `key:` beside `kind: coverage-ledger`, and only 1.7.1's `addressing` §4 passes over a `kind:` naming a document. 1.7.0's §4 read a folder's kind off the first artifact carrying both fields, so beside it a root BRD would resolve as kind `coverage-ledger`.

### Fixed — the coverage ledger's `evidence` column had no writer, so every package told the customer nothing had been checked

`coverage-ledger-format.md` §2 defines `evidence` as the `[CG#n]`/`[DG#n]` findings bearing on a requirement, "empty until grounding has run"; `/brd-intake` Phase 5 and `/brd-split` Phase 3 each write it empty and name `/prd-ground` as whose job filling it is; and `/prd-ground` never wrote it. Its Phase 8 wrote the two grounding files and nothing else, its `deliverable_paths` on `route: brd` named no ledger, and its one mention of the column only forbade a documentation page in it. So the column was empty on every ledger in every tree for ever, however much grounding had run — and since `bundle-packaging.md` §1.1 puts the ledger **and** both grounding files in the customer bundle, a package handed the customer the one document that lists every requirement and cannot tell a checked one from an unchecked one, beside a grounding document showing which had been checked. A live `/brd-package` run shipped exactly that, its own adversarial reviewer raising it unprompted on both passes. **Population: every coverage ledger written on any tree, at every release the column has existed, and every bundle built from one.** `/prd-ground` Phase 8 now rebuilds the column as its last write, over every row of the ledger of the BRD it just ground: a row takes the id of every finding whose `claim` names that row's `[BR#n]`, a row no finding names keeps an empty cell, and nothing else in the row is touched — this command still allocates nothing and writes no disposition. It runs in every mode, `--no-code` included, since a design-only run still produces findings the column would otherwise never index, and not at all on `route: idea`, which has no ledger. **The column indexes and never adjudicates** — the verdict stays in the grounding file the id resolves to, which is why a `SUPERSEDED` finding stays listed rather than being filtered out — and the ledger joins that route's `deliverable_paths` only where the rebuild changed it, so an unchanged file is not named in a handoff. The Final report now states how many rows took an id and how many were left empty; the ledger line itself is unmoved, since §6 counts by `disposition`.

### Fixed — `/brd-interview` offered a `(Recommended)` push on a specs repo with no remote

`workflows-core:phase-handoff` §4.3 requires §2.1's push-target probe to run **before** its consent array is presented, and a one-line notice above the array where that probe finds no `origin`. `/brd-interview` Phase 10 said only "present its §4.3 choice array verbatim" and then quoted the array — which is what quoting an array invites — so a live run presented the array, ran no probe, and offered a `(Recommended)` push-and-open-PR that could not succeed, with nothing said: the exact state §2.1 records that probe as having been added for, met again. **Population: every `/brd-interview` run against a specs repository with no `origin` remote — on 3.6.x as well.** The phase now says to execute §4.3 in full rather than its array alone, names the probe and the notice, and carries the probe's `remote` value into `handoff-to-main`, which §2.1 expects carried rather than probed twice. **It was family-wide rather than this command's alone** — of the fifteen commands that quote a §4.3 array, only `/brd-package` named the probe, and it does so because it needs the `remote` value for its own delivery-route decision — and the half that fixes the other fourteen is `workflows-core` 1.7.1's, which names the step above the arrays where a reader who copies one will meet it.

### Fixed — an open requirement defect was never put to the customer

`/brd-intake` Phase 4 confirms each requirement defect into `brd/brd-defect-log.md` with resolution `open`, and `brd-format.md` §4 names two resolutions only the customer can supply, `customer-amended` and `withdrawn`. Nothing asked the customer for either. On 3.6.x `/brd-interview` generated its questions from grounding findings, horizons, design reconciliations, ledger dispositions and assertions without evidence — the strings `[DEF#` and `brd-defect-log` occurred in it zero times; `/brd-package` shipped the log in the bundle only so a ledger row's `rejected: [DEF#n]` would resolve for the reviewer, and no part of its prompt put an entry to anyone; and `customer-review-schema.md` §4 carries no defect section. So an `ambiguity` — two competent readings of one requirement — reached the customer only if they happened to open the log and comment on it, and the PRD was authored on whichever reading the delivery team assumed. **Population: every BRD intaken with a confirmed `ambiguity`, `conflict`, `duplicate`, `untestable` or `scope-leak` defect.** `unsourced` is not among them: grounding settles that class, and a finding that cannot settle it is already a question under the `NOT-PROVABLE` source.

An open requirement defect of those five classes is now a question source of `/brd-interview`'s own, the sixth bullet of Phase 3's *Round 1 is generated from the grounding*. It is asked **once across the slices**, by the slice holding the lowest-numbered of its rows still live — `covered-here` or `deferred-to` in that slice's own ledger — its rows being **every row it joins**, the one its entry is raised on and every one the entry names, so a split whose first row no slice builds is still owned through a sibling row one does; or, where none is live, by its **carrier** — the slice holding the lowest-numbered of those rows rejected citing it — on that row's own question (below), whatever the defect's class; and never again once any slice's `interview/customer-questions.md` records it. A defect whose owner cannot yet be decided, because a row it joins is still `unallocated` or a sibling's ledger or question set cannot be read, is withheld and reported rather than guessed. The question is always `[C]` (`interview-tagging.md` §1): it states what the defect records — the two readings, the two requirements that cannot both hold, the missing observable outcome — and names the image where the row was drawn from one. It travels in the package as every held `[C]` does — the review schema gains no section, and the answer comes back in its decision log — with its held entry naming the `[DEF#n]`. A row it names as context that the asking slice does not claim — a sibling's, or one the root settled — is written `<PARENT-KEY> [BR#n]`, by the rule the entry *a row a slice does not claim, cited bare, stopped its package* (below) records, which binds every question and everything written from one. `/brd-reconcile` copies that id into the answering `[CD#n]`'s new `settles` field (`decision-register-format.md` §1), from the held entry and never from the customer's prose, and its Phase 8, which wrote two of the log's resolutions, now writes a third — `resolved-by: <SLICE-KEY>/[CD#n]`, which `brd-format.md` §4 now admits beside the grounding resolution. That one was the bare `resolved-by: [CG#n]` on 3.6.x and is now qualified too, `resolved-by: <SLICE-KEY>/[CG#n]`: both carry the key of the slice whose decision or finding settled the defect, in the shape `conditional_on` already gives a record of another BRD, because each slice numbers its own `[CD#n]` and `[CG#n]` from 1 while the defect log is the parent's, so a bare `[CD#2]` there would name no one record. No command writes the `[CG#n]` form, so only a value written into a defect log by hand is affected, and it needs its slice key added: `/brd-package` reports a bare one rather than resolving it (`bundle-packaging.md` §6.2), and checks a qualified one naming the packaged slice against that slice's own register or grounding. `customer-amended` is qualified the same way, `customer-amended <SLICE-KEY> <date>`: the amended text is in the returned review of the slice that reconciled it, and once a BRD has two slices a bare date does not say whose. **Population: every defect a customer amends; a value 3.6.x wrote bare is read as it stands, naming no slice.** A settled defect takes `withdrawn` where the answer drops the requirement — every `open` defect raised on it, whether or not the answer's `settles` names it — which the ledger then records `rejected: [DEF#n]`; `customer-amended <SLICE-KEY> <date>` where the review supplied corrected text; and otherwise `resolved-by: <SLICE-KEY>/[CD#n]`. Every round record `/brd-interview` writes now carries a `requirement defects:` line saying which defects that round asked and which it withheld, and why — every open defect of the five classes joining a row of the slice's in-scope set, and every defect a rejected row of the slice carries, an `unsourced` one included, since the carrier reads the defect a rejection cites whatever its class.

**Where no slice owns a defect, this route never asks it, and the record says so rather than calling it settled.** A defect whose rows are all `superseded-by`, never delegated, or rejected citing something else has no live row and no carrier: it stays `open` in the parent's log, and the account line of a slice holding one of its rows withholds it as `no live row`. A defect every row of which the root's own walk settled — deferred, rejected or superseded there, never delegated — is in no slice's in-scope set: a slice that once claimed such a row keeps it only as an orphan row, which is out of that slice's scope as well (below), so no account line names it, and the root is never interviewed. `/brd-interview` states that limit beside the carrier rule; this release does not route such a defect to the customer.

**One question per rejected row, whichever slice asks.** Each rejected row's held entry now carries a `- **Rejected row:** [BR#n]` line. Where a defect this slice carries has a rejected row whose question is already on file and still *held for the customer* — a slice interviewed on 3.6.x, whose round-1 question predates the defect line, is the ordinary case — the run appends the `- **Requirement defect:**` line to that entry rather than raising a second question; an entry written before 3.7.0 is matched as the one held entry naming that row's `[BR#n]` and asking about its rejection, and where none or more than one could be it, a question is raised and the candidates are named. The question's text is not rewritten. Where that earlier question was already answered, the answer does not settle the defect — `settles` is copied from the entry's line when an answer is frozen, and that answer was frozen without one — so the defect is asked anew, by design, naming the earlier `[CD#n]` as context; and where it was frozen `open`, `/brd-reconcile` writes its `settles` from the line when it completes the record. And a rejected row whose defect any slice owns through a live row — this slice included — raises no question of its own: for a defect of the five classes, the defect's question names every row it joins as context, the rejected one among them, so a question of the row's own would put that row to the customer twice; an `unsourced` defect joins a live row only in a log entry written before the one-row listing, is grounding's to settle on that live row, and its rejected row raises nothing either. **Population: every slice holding a row rejected on a defect some slice owns through a live row, and 3.6.x slices holding a question about a row rejected on a defect no live row owns.** `brd-format.md` §4's `resolved-by` row, which named the answer to the question the defect raised, now names a rejected row's question that carries it as well.

**A slice interviewed before this release** has a round 1 whose record carries no such line, and its defects belong in round 1 — a question round 1 could have asked is never moved to a later round. Where round 1 is still open, the next bare `/product-workflows:brd-interview <SLICE-KEY>` appends them to it. Where round 1 is closed, the run names them, and its next-step offer is `/product-workflows:brd-interview <SLICE-KEY> --round 1` with the cause *requirement defects became a question source*, in place of the packaging step, until they are asked. The cost is one more interview run and one more package round with the customer for every such slice; what it buys is the questions the first package should have carried.

### Fixed — a defect whose rows were all rejected was never asked, and so never settled

`/brd-interview` raised a question for "an in-scope ledger row resolved `deferred-to` or `rejected` whose consequence for the rest of the BRD is unstated", and named no artifact where a consequence counts as stated and no tag. `/brd-split` records a rationale for a `deferred-to` row only, so a rejection's consequence was unstated by construction and whether to ask was left to judgement. And the question never carried the defect the rejection cites: no question carried a `[DEF#n]` at all, so `/brd-reconcile` could never copy one into a `settles` field, and a rejection's defect stayed `open` in the parent's log for good. The requirement-defect source this release adds (above) decides ownership over **live** rows only, so without the carrier below it would have left a `[DEF#n]` every row of which was rejected on it with no owner as well. **Population: every slice holding a row `rejected: [DEF#n]` on 3.6.x.** Phase 3 now asks every in-scope `rejected` row not already settled, always `[C]` (whether the customer accepts not getting what they asked for is theirs to say). A row is settled where it reads `rejected: <SLICE-KEY>/[CD#n]` (the customer's own withdrawal, below), or where the defect it cites is no longer `open`, is already asked, is owned through a live row by any slice, or is carried by another slice. Where no row a defect joins is live, its **carrier** (above) owns it: that row's question carries the defect on its `- **Requirement defect:**` line, states what the defect records, and counts as asking it, and `/brd-reconcile` resolves it `resolved-by: <SLICE-KEY>/[CD#n]` on the customer's answer — accepting the rejection or not; the row does not move, and `withdrawn` is not written, because the customer dropped nothing the delivery side had not already dropped. Where that defect is a `conflict` or `duplicate` the answer settles by keeping one row and dropping the other, the two rules meet, and Phase 8 states their order once: the rejected row never moves — kept, it goes to *what still needs a human* — while the defect's resolution and the other row's write follow that phase's rules for keeping one row and dropping the other. A defect with neither a live row nor a carrier is withheld as `no live row` (above). A `deferred-to` row raises a question only where no rationale was recorded — no `slices.md` block and no `[CD#n]` deferring it. **What it costs**: one more `[C]` question per rejected row a package now carries, and never a second one for the same row (above).

### Fixed — a slice asked the customer about rows its parent had withdrawn and settled

`/brd-interview` Phase 3 scoped a round to the ledger rows reading `covered-here`, `deferred-to`, `rejected` or `superseded-by`, read off the `disposition` column and never off `claims:`. An **orphan row** — a ledger row for a `[BR#n]` the slice's `claims:` no longer names (`coverage-ledger-format.md` §2) — carries the fate its parent's walk settled across unchanged where that fate is `rejected: [DEF#n]` or `superseded-by: [BR#n]` (§3's orphan table), so it read exactly like a row the slice had rejected or superseded itself: a requirement the root rejected was put to the customer by a slice that no longer claims it, and under this release's carrier rule its orphan row would have made that slice the carrier of the root's defect. **Population: every slice holding an orphan row its parent's walk rejected or superseded — on 3.6.x as well.** An orphan row is now out of the question scope whatever it reads, the scope reading `claims:` for that one test and for nothing else, and the carrier test counts only a row the parent's ledger records `covered-by` the slice. `/brd-package`'s review scope, which stated the same four dispositions as the package's scope, now leaves an orphan row out as well, naming it, cited `<PARENT-KEY> [BR#n]` (below), with the BRD that holds it — and no longer says every row out of scope is covered by a separate package, which a row the parent kept or settled itself never is; `coverage-ledger-format.md` §3.1, the rule both cite, now says why the one test that reads `claims:` is the orphan test. The all-delegated stop, which that scoping now reaches for a slice every one of whose rows is an orphan row, said every row was `covered-by` another BRD, that the inventory was not empty, and that an instruction on `/brd-split <BRD-KEY>` could re-cut a row onto "another child of" the slice — a slice has no child, its inventory is empty once every claim is withdrawn, and the re-cut runs on the parent. It now names the orphan rows, says why the empty-inventory gate does not see such a slice, and sends the operator to `/brd-split` on the parent, which resolves a child left claiming nothing; `/brd-package`'s account of a folder that stop leaves without a register, and the documentation page, say the same.

### Fixed — a row a slice does not claim, cited bare, stopped its package

A slice's package carries the slice's own inventory, which holds only the rows it claims (`brd-format.md` §2.1), and `/brd-package`'s citation-resolution check resolves a bare `[BR#n]` against that inventory alone (`bundle-packaging.md` §6.2, relation 1), so a bare mention of a sibling's row, an orphan row or a row the root settled stopped the run with `BRD_PACKAGE_DEAD_CITATION`. Four writers produced one: `/brd-interview`, which on 3.6.x let a question cite a delegated row as context with no qualifier; `/create-prd`, whose `[AS#n]` for a gap only the customer can settle goes into the same register; `/brd-reconcile`, whose freeze writes a `[CD#n]`'s `statement` as confirmed and copies its `options_considered` from the question, naming whatever row the question named; and `/brd-package` itself, whose review scope named each row out of scope with the BRD that holds it — `[BR#12] — held by ACME-90-02` — in a prompt part the run generates, which no operator edit can repair. **Population: every package of a slice holding a `covered-by` or orphan row — on 3.6.x as well — and every package whose questions or records cite a row the slice does not claim, 3.6.x's delegated context included.** Each now writes such a row `<PARENT-KEY> [BR#n]`, the one qualified prose spelling §6.2 gives another BRD's id, which relation 1 discharges, and reads which form a row takes off the slice's inventory: `/brd-interview` in every question and everything written from one (*A row this slice does not claim*); `/create-prd` in the `[AS#n]` it writes; `/brd-reconcile` in every field its freeze writes save the customer's own words, qualifying as it copies them the options of a question that names such a row bare, one recorded before 3.7.0 or put by a package the route did not build; and `/brd-package` in its review scope, a `superseded-by` row's successor included wherever the slice does not claim it. The customer's quoted reason, and an answer quoted outside the options, are never requalified: `bundle-packaging.md` §6.3 now counts both as verbatim customer content, so a bare id the customer wrote there is reported rather than stopped on, as one visible in their screenshot already was, and §2's de-Obsidianising pass leaves a link in either as written. **An entry already on file is not rewritten**: a question, a held entry or a register record written before 3.7.0 that cites such a row bare still stops the package, and `BRD_PACKAGE_DEAD_CITATION`'s message and the documentation page now say to qualify that id by hand where the stop names it, changing nothing else in the sentence. `bundle-packaging.md` §7.1, which said the review scope carries no requirement ids, now says it carries them for those rows, and §7.2 that its set-resolution check does not reach that list.

### Fixed — a slice whose rounds recorded no decision could not be packaged

`/brd-interview` wrote `decisions.md` as one block per `[VD#n]` and `[AS#n]` a round recorded, and a round whose questions were `[C]` or answered from findings records neither — its `[C]` questions go to `interview/customer-questions.md`. So no register was written; `/brd-interview`'s next-step offer, which checked `/brd-package`'s rounds gate and its something-to-review gate but not its register gate, offered packaging; and `/brd-package` Phase 0 step 6 stopped `BRD_PACKAGE_NEEDS_INTERVIEW`, saying no interview had ever run. Its remedy, another `/brd-interview` run, resumed the same round and again wrote no register — a loop. **Population: every slice whose rounds hold a `[C]` question and record no `[VD#n]` or `[AS#n]` — on 3.6.x any such slice, and with this release every slice owning an open requirement defect of the five classes above whose grounding raised no `[V]` question and no `[AS#n]`**, which the new question source turns into a round of `[C]` alone. `/brd-interview` now writes `decisions.md` on every run that records a round — the single header line `# Decision register: <BRD-KEY>` where no round recorded a decision (`decision-register-format.md` §1) — and hands it off with the round record, and its next-step offer checks `/brd-package`'s register gate as well as the other two.
