The no-new-round path writes it too, where none is on file, so a slice caught in the loop leaves it on its next `/brd-interview` run — writing the register as it resumes an open round, or on that path where every round is closed; only an all-delegated slice, which that command stops on, is left without one, and it has nothing to package. `/brd-package`'s rounds gate now takes the rounds it must find from the held `[C]` entries as well as from the register's records, so the record of a round of held `[C]` questions is gated too; before, such a round put nothing in the register and was never checked. `/brd-package` reads the header-only register as an empty corpus (`bundle-packaging.md` §6.1), and `/brd-reconcile` freezes its first `[CD#n]` into it.

### Fixed — the `/brd-interview` page said, twice, that an unchanged interview run hands off nothing

`docs/commands/brd-interview.md` said, in two sentences, that a run that finds every round closed and nothing changed "reaches the handoff with nothing to commit", and that `decisions.md` is written "on every run that records a round". Since the fix above that made a `[C]`-only slice packageable, that run writes `decisions.md` as its header line where none is on file and hands it off. **Population: docs readers only; the command already did this.** Both sentences now say so.

### Fixed — `/brd-interview` computed a next-step state it could never reach

Phase 11 tested for an absent `interview/` round record and offered `package_offerable: not-interviewed`, but every path that reaches Phase 11 has written a round record — a run that stops before writing one, the all-delegated stop among them, never gets there. **Population: none — the state was dead; the fix removes a branch no run could take.** The value and its list are gone, and Phase 11 says why `/brd-package`'s `BRD_PACKAGE_NOT_INTERVIEWED` can never meet an offer made there. Two sentences beside it were corrected with it: the claim that "only the all-delegated stop" leaves a folder without a register, and `/brd-package` step 7's claim that an empty round set "reaches step 8's `BRD_PACKAGE_NOTHING_TO_REVIEW`" — step 8 stops it unless `interview/` holds a round record and an open `[AS#n]` leaves something to review. The `/brd-package` docs page also said `BRD_PACKAGE_NOT_INTERVIEWED` needs "no `[VD#n]`"; it fires whatever the register holds. **Population, for those three corrections: every reader of them — no run's behaviour changed**, since the register claim and the round-set claim each described a path the commands already took differently, and the page described a stop its command already fired on a wider condition.

### Fixed — a first interview generated its round-1 questions twice

`/brd-interview` Phase 2's no-round-record branch runs *Round 1 is generated from the grounding* to decide whether there is a round to open, and Phase 3 then opened by generating the round's questions again, with nothing saying the second pass was the first one's set. **Population: every first `/brd-interview` run on a slice, where a literal reading writes each question twice.** Phase 3 now says that where Phase 2 already generated round 1 it works that set and generates nothing again — and that on a round it resumes or re-opens, whatever the cause, it generates nothing beyond the questions the round-1 test and the one-question-per-row rule append.

### Fixed — a customer decision's altitude was invented

`/brd-reconcile` Phase 5 filled a `[CD#n]`'s `altitude` with "the altitude the question carried", and no held `[C]` question carried one — `/brd-interview` wrote none into `interview/customer-questions.md` — so every `[CD#n]` got an altitude the run made up, and `/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` filter the register on it. **Population: every `[CD#n]` frozen on 3.6.x.** Each held `[C]` entry now carries a `- **Altitude:**` line, decided by the test `decision-register-format.md` §1 now states as a mapping — `product` where the answer is the PRD's to state, `architecture` the ARD's, `implementation` the specification's, the value each of the three filters on; a `[CD#n]` copies it, or an `[AS#n]`'s own, or, for an escalated `[SR#n]`, the altitude of what its target names. Where there is nothing to copy — an entry written before this release, or a finding aimed at a document passage — the run decides it by the same test and says so in the reconciliation record. An answer matching no question the package put needs none: it is never frozen (below).

### Fixed — a customer's answer outside the options put could not be recorded

`decision-register-format.md` §1 required `chosen` to be one member of `options_considered`, and `/brd-reconcile` takes `options_considered` from what the package put and never from the answer — so a customer who chose neither reading, or dropped the requirement instead, broke one rule or the other, and Phase 4 gave the case no route. **Population: every customer answer outside the options its question, assumption or escalated finding put.** A `[CD#n]`'s `chosen` may now carry the customer's own answer after one fixed marker, `chosen: "outside the options: <the customer's answer, verbatim>"` — or, for an answer spanning lines or carrying a `"` or a `\`, the same marker opening a literal block scalar, so YAML reads the answer back exactly — with `options_considered` left as put; Phase 4 shows the operator whether an answer is outside the options and names *Confirm* as its route. A `[VD#n]` is unchanged: an option the operator names still joins `options_considered` before it is chosen.

### Fixed — a yes-or-no customer question had no recordable options

A `[CD#n]`'s `options_considered` is "what the package actually put in front of the customer, taken from the `[C]` question", and a question put as yes or no lists no options, so nothing said what the record should carry. **Population: every `[CD#n]` answering a yes-or-no question, an assumption or an escalated self-review finding on 3.6.x.** `decision-register-format.md` §1 now fixes it: such a question records `["yes", "no"]`, and one that listed its options records them as put; a `[CD#n]` answering an `[AS#n]`, which asserts and offers nothing, records `["as assumed", "not as assumed"]`, and one answering an escalated `[SR#n]` records `["as the package states", "as the finding argues"]`; `/brd-reconcile` Phase 5 and `/brd-interview`'s `[VD#n]` record cite the rule.

### Fixed — a decision resting on no finding had two spellings, and the will-change rule fired on it

`decision-register-format.md` §1 made only `defects`, `settles` and `conditional_on` "omitted when absent", and said nothing of a decision resting on no finding, so a writer could write `evidence: []` or leave the field out. And §6's test — the rule fires where *every* finding in the list is `will-change` — is vacuously true of an empty list, so read literally it refused to close a decision that rests on no finding at all. **Population: every `[VD#n]` or `[CD#n]` resting on no finding — a customer's business decision typically does.** §1 now fixes one form, `evidence: []`, never omitted — a known empty list, not the not-applicable case `workflows-core:grounding-format` §2.1 omits a field for, whose reading of an empty value `workflows-core` 1.7.1 scopes to finding records — and §6 and `/brd-interview`'s will-change phase say the rule does not fire on it; thinning a list to nothing is still the evasion §6 refuses.

### Fixed — a closed round's record still said it was open

`/brd-reconcile` Phase 5 closes a held `[C]` question by appending to `interview/round-<N>.md`, which is append-only, so a round record that stated its own state kept stating `open` above the closure, and nothing recorded that the round had closed. Its Phase 7 said the round records were "bannered too" while its own list of files to banner left them out. **Population: every round `/brd-reconcile` closed.** Every write of a round record now ends with one `Status:` line — `Status: open — waiting on <holding states>` or `Status: closed <YYYYMMDD> — <why>` — recording the round's state, which its questions' dispositions decide, a reader taking the **last** line and the dispositions winning where the two disagree; `/brd-reconcile` appends the closing line where its answers leave every question in the round with a terminal disposition, and none otherwise — a last line it leaves naming a holding state no question is in any more, such as *held for the customer* beside a question still *deferred*, is outvoted by the dispositions until the next `/brd-interview` write appends one that agrees. Round records are appended to, never bannered, and a stale-reference sweep hit inside one is `needs-a-human`, as in any dated snapshot, rather than an `updated` edit. A record carrying no `Status:` line is read off its questions' dispositions until its next write adds one. `/brd-interview` also no longer says a round holding a `[C]` "stays open indefinitely": it stays open until `/brd-reconcile` closes it with the customer's answer.

### Fixed — the review's traceability section had an open vocabulary

`customer-review-schema.md` §4's section 4 offered "confirmed, corrected, or missing entirely" without saying whether that set was closed, and had no value for a requirement the customer drops, or for one the package already said would not be built; two reader dispatches read byte-identical rows differently. **Population: every returned review.** Section 4 now takes exactly one of four values — `confirmed` (including a requirement the package says will not be built, where the customer agrees), `corrected`, `missing`, `withdrawn` — and a `withdrawn` row says why in the row itself, the answer to any question the package put about that requirement going in section 7 as every answer does. The row is rendered into every customer prompt and stays plugin-free. `/brd-reconcile` moves nothing on a withdrawal answering no question the package put — in section 4 alone or in a section-7 row matching nothing — and names it under *what still needs a human* with the customer's words: an answer matching no question is never frozen as a `[CD#n]`, since nothing records what it was offered, what it settles or which round it closes, and the operator records it for a human or rejects it — or, where the reader matched it to nothing although it answers a question the package put, names that question, which re-points the candidate onto the confirmation picker's four options and is recorded in the reconciliation record.

### Fixed — a customer's withdrawal of a requirement with no defect could not be recorded

`/brd-reconcile` Phase 9 wrote `rejected: [DEF#n]` only by citing a defect-log entry Phase 8 resolved `withdrawn`, so a customer withdrawing a requirement that carried no open defect left the row `covered-here`. **Population: every customer withdrawal, answering a question the package put, of a requirement carrying no open defect** — a withdrawal answering no question is never frozen (above). Phase 8 now resolves every `open` defect raised on a withdrawn requirement `withdrawn`, whether or not the answer's `settles` names it, so the new spelling is written exactly where no such defect exists. `coverage-ledger-format.md` §3's `rejected` now has two spellings: `rejected: [DEF#n]` as before, and `rejected: <SLICE-KEY>/[CD#n]`, citing the customer decision that withdrew the requirement, qualified by the slice's key as `resolved-by` is — written only by `/brd-reconcile`, onto its own slice's ledger. Both count as `rejected` in the ledger line, and §3 now says how to tell a customer's withdrawal from the delivery side's rejection: the citation — a `[CD#n]`, or a `[DEF#n]` the log resolves `withdrawn`. `/brd-interview` asks no question about a row the customer withdrew.

### Fixed — a customer asking for a row with a fate to be built was sent to a `/brd-split` run that moves nothing

`/brd-reconcile` Phase 8 said a customer answer asking for a `rejected` row to be built after all is an allocation, which Phase 9 names rather than writes, and Phase 9 named every row it would otherwise allocate in the reconciliation record with `/product-workflows:brd-split <BRD-KEY>` as the fix; its Phase 6 refusal of a section-12 row asking for a disposition named the same run as the channel. `/brd-split` on a slice walks only `unallocated` rows, and the row asked about carries a fate, so the named run moved nothing for it. **Population: every reconciliation whose customer asks for a row already rejected, deferred or superseded to be built — on 3.6.x as well.** The phases now say plainly that no command reverses such a disposition — no row of a slice's ledger returns to `unallocated`, and `/brd-split` on a slice walks no row that carries a fate, its sibling re-cut running on the parent and moving only a row a slice deferred, on an instruction a person gives — and record the row, with the customer's words, in the reconciliation record's *what still needs a human*, naming no command; `/brd-split` is named only for a row still `unallocated` — which a slice this route interviewed never holds, `/brd-interview` refusing an unallocated ledger, and which a `--sent` review of a slice never interviewed can find. The command's documentation page says the same.

### Fixed — `/brd-reconcile` named a route for an uncovered requirement that no command takes

A requirement the customer asked for that no `[BR#n]` covers was recorded under *what still needs a human* "naming the two real routes: an amendment logged against the defect log, or a fresh source document through `/product-workflows:brd-intake`". No command logs an amendment for it: `customer-amended` resolves a defect raised on a `[BR#n]` the inventory already holds, and such a requirement has none. **Population: every reconciliation whose customer asks for a requirement the package missed — on 3.6.x as well.** The record now names the one route a command takes — a revised source document, intaken over the root with `/brd-intake <PARENT-KEY> @<revised-file>`, whose read extracts the requirement as a new `[BR#n]`, at the cost its Phase 0 step 7 names — and otherwise leaves it to a person to take up with the customer; the documentation page says the same.

### Fixed — a `--sent` reconcile of a slice holding no register had nowhere to freeze an answer

`/brd-reconcile` Phase 0 step 2 said `--sent` admits a hand-authored package and *"does not make the route's earlier phases optional"*: a folder that never reached `/brd-interview` *"still has no `decisions.md` for a `[CD#n]` to be frozen against"*. Its step 7 and *Next steps* meanwhile provide for a `--sent` review of a slice never interviewed, and its Phase 5 assumed the register on file because `/brd-interview` writes it and `/brd-package` gates on it — neither of which a `--sent` run passes through. So nothing said what such a run does, and a `[CD#n]` answering a held `[C]` question on a slice interviewed before 3.7.0 over rounds that recorded no decision, which holds its questions and no register, had no file to be written into. **Population: every `--sent` run on a slice holding no `decisions.md` — one never interviewed, or one whose rounds, interviewed before 3.7.0, recorded no decision.** Such a slice is admitted, and Phase 5 now creates the register where none is on file, as its header line alone, whether or not it then freezes a record; `decision-register-format.md` §1 names `/brd-reconcile` among the commands that create the file. On a slice never interviewed, with no `[C]` question and no `[SR#n]` on file, a candidate can answer only an `[AS#n]` `/create-prd` recorded there, and any other is `unmatched` and goes to a human. Step 8 and the Phase 3 dispatch read the register and the question set where they are on file. `/brd-package`, which said a register on no ref means the interview's handoff was declined, now names the three commands that create one, and both documentation pages say the same. Phase 13's handoff declared `interview/round-<N>.md` and `interview/customer-questions.md` unconditionally, and on a slice never interviewed, which holds neither, `workflows-core:phase-handoff` §2.3 names a declared path nothing wrote as a declaration unaccounted for; it now declares both where on file, and the documentation page says the question set is updated where one is on file.

### Fixed — `/brd-reconcile` offered an effort proposal for decisions it had not frozen, on a slice with no PRD to price

Phase 14's advancing branch named `/product-workflows:prd-proposal <SLICE-KEY>` in prose, for the reason that *"until this run froze the customer's `[CD#n]`s there were none"*, so a proposal written now could cite them. A run that froze none — every candidate sent to a human, or a `--sent` review of a slice never interviewed whose answers matched no roundless `[AS#n]` — made the same offer for a reason untrue of it, and on a slice an earlier reconciliation had reached, decisions frozen before the run already stood, which the sentence denied. And `/prd-proposal` gates `prd.md` on main, stopping with `PRD_PROPOSAL_NEEDS_PRD` where the slice holds none — the ordinary state on this route, which authors the PRD after reconciliation — so the offer handed the operator a run that stops in its first phase. **Population: every `/brd-reconcile` run that reaches the advancing branch — on 3.6.x as well.** The offer is now named only where the run froze at least one `[CD#n]`, for the reason that holds — a customer decision no proposal written before the run could cite — and says that it prices the slice once its `prd.md` is on main, after `/product-workflows:create-prd <SLICE-KEY>` where it holds none yet; a run that froze none names no proposal.

### Fixed — `/brd-reconcile`'s sweeps named ids bare in another BRD's files

The propagation sweep's dispositions are written into a dependent BRD's `decisions.md`: a `reopened` record named *"this run's `[CD#n]` as its cause"*, and an `inherited-unchanged` or `reverted` one was recorded against *"the changed id"*, with nothing saying whose. A dependent's register numbers its own records and ships in the dependent's own package, so a bare `[CD#3]` there named the dependent's own `[CD#3]` where it held one — a wrong cause, which `bundle-packaging.md` §6.2's relation 1 then resolved green — and nothing where it did not, a dead citation the dependent's next package stops on with `BRD_PACKAGE_DEAD_CITATION` wherever the write is the plugin's own prose. The stale cross-reference sweep's `updated` outcome, correcting a sentence in the parent's or a sibling slice's artifact *"naming the `[CD#n]` that changed it"*, had the same defect. **Population: every propagation sweep that writes into a dependent BRD, and every `updated` correction written into another BRD's artifact — on 3.6.x as well.** Every id a sweep write names now carries the key of the BRD whose numbering it is — this slice's for a `[CD#n]`, `[VD#n]`, `[AS#n]` or `[SR#n]`, the parent's for a `[BR#n]` or `[DEF#n]` — always in prose, `<BRD-KEY> [CD#n]`, the one qualified spelling `bundle-packaging.md` §6.2 fixes, and at a place the sweep's own table names. A `reopened`, `reverted` or `withdrawn` write puts it in a closing paragraph appended to the dependent record's `argumentation`, opening `Reopened`, `Reverted` or `Withdrawn` and the date; an `inherited-unchanged` item writes nothing into the dependent's record, and its row, naming both records qualified, is in the reconciliation record. **Never `decision-register-format.md` §5's slash shape, `<BRD-KEY>/[CD#n]`, in a sweep write**: an earlier draft of this fix sent a structured field to that shape, but no sweep write names a changed id in one of a record's thirteen fields, and §6.2's relation 1 reads the slash shape as qualified only in the declared fields its table lists — `conditional_on` among them, which a `reverted` write restores to the value the record already held, in that field's own shape, naming the changed id only in its closing paragraph. `decision-register-format.md` §4 now says where **any** reopening records its cause — that closing paragraph, beneath the customer's quoted reason on a `[CD#n]` and never inside it, `bundle-packaging.md` §6.3 counting only the customer's quoted words as theirs — that what stands in `argumentation` stays, that **§4 now names one set — a record's decision fields, all thirteen §1 defines — with the per-field rule under it**, so a file needing to name the fields of a decision record cites that set instead of enumerating part of it; that a re-decision and a `reverted` write alike take afresh the seven of them a decision's own writing fills — `statement`, `options_considered`, `chosen`, `evidence`, `defects`, `conditional_on` and `status`, §1's set for a decision's own writing, `evidence` and `conditional_on` under §6 as well — and append their own closing paragraph after it, never over it or what stands above it. **Three more of the thirteen are settled beside that set rather than left to a reader**, because a register that keeps them stale is read wrongly by the commands downstream of it: `round` takes the round that produced the position now on record (the re-decision's own; on a reversion, the round of the position being restored, a propagation sweep being no round), `consumed_by` returns to `none` so `/create-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` report the record as unconsumed at their own altitude while the artifact they wrote still holds the position that was replaced, and `altitude` stays, `id` and `settles` standing with it — all thirteen accounted for. `/brd-interview` carves the re-decision out of both places that said a decision carries every field §1 defines (its `[V]` phase and its register phase), and `/brd-package`'s round-set derivation now says what a round that only re-decided contributes to it — those records, by the `round` §4 moves — and why the earlier round the field moved off may fall out of the set, and that a cause from another BRD is named with that BRD's key; the sweep's `withdrawn` write now carries the reason §3 says a withdrawn record keeps, and its `reverted` row, which said the record's `chosen` **and `argumentation`** return to the earlier position — which on a record reopened and re-decided earlier in the same sweep would have erased the `Reopened` paragraph and the re-decision's reasoning — now restores the decision fields and appends. `/brd-interview`, which reopens decisions on a new grounding finding, now names that reopen — the `status` move, the closing paragraph and the re-decision that follows it, in the same run or a later one, which takes the record's decision fields afresh — as the second of two admitted changes to another run's record, beside a `[CDF#n]`'s re-disposition, where its *Resolve inputs* phase said there was exactly one, and said of the re-decision that it wrote three fields where its own *Put each `[V]` to the operator* and *The will-change rule* phases move `options_considered`, `evidence` and `conditional_on` too. The documentation page says the same. Where a reopening's cause went had never been said, on 3.6.x either.

### Fixed — the roles page said what a phase `/frames` charges means by a folder's progress, and put a slice's frame set in `prd-creation`

`docs/roles-and-phases.md` said being in *prd-creation* means the PRD does not yet have a merged specification or design, and that `/workflows-core:frames` lands there on a PRD folder or one of its Epics — while `workflows-core`'s `cost-emission.md` §7 charges a frame set by the kind its folder asserts, so a BRD-route slice, a `PRD-` folder asserting `kind: brd`, is `brd-to-prd`, and an Epic folder that already holds a merged specification or design is `prd-creation` all the same. The page now keeps the no-merged-specification meaning for `/idea` and `/create-prd`, says `/frames` lands there on a folder asserting `prd` or `epic` — an Epic folder under a slice included — where the phase marks product-definition spend and nothing about the folder's progress, and says under *brd-to-prd* that it lands there on a folder asserting `kind: brd`, a container or a slice, where the phase likewise says nothing about progress. That paragraph's *"rather than a PRD already existing for it"* was false on the route itself too — a later interview round, package or reconciliation runs on a slice that already holds its PRD — and now says so. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of the page.**

### Fixed — an accepted self-review finding could make a package unshippable

`/brd-package` part 9 quotes every self-review finding the operator disposes `accepted-risk` in the reviewer's own words, and the plugin-free scan stops the run on any `§`, command or agent name, and the citation-resolution check on any working filename, in the finished prompt. `brd-package-reviewer`'s findings routinely cited a plugin reference's section, a working filename or a command — in one measured run, 9 of 12 findings carried such a token — and nothing may reword an attack, so an accepted finding carrying one stopped the run with no remedy the text allowed. **Population: every `/brd-package` run on 3.6.x whose operator accepted a finding carrying such a token.** The reviewer now writes each finding's target, attack and what would settle it in words a customer can read without this plugin — the test it applies stated in words, a package document named by what it is, a passage of the customer's cited with its captured path — so an accepted finding passes both as quoted, and the command still never changes an attack's words.

### Fixed — a wikilinked file in a BRD was dropped without a trace

`/brd-intake` Phase 2 recognised three link forms — the inline markdown image or link, the reference-style definition and the HTML `<img src>` — and `[[` occurred in neither `commands/brd-intake.md` nor `references/brd-format.md`. A file the document linked as `[[notes]]` or embedded as `![[shot.png]]` was therefore not copied and not logged, which broke the phase's own rule that every link not copied is named, never dropped in silence. It is the likeliest shape to meet: Obsidian writes a pasted image as `![[Pasted image ….png]]` and saves it to the vault's attachment folder, usually outside the note's own folder. **Population: every BRD authored or converted in Obsidian that links a page or embeds an image that way.** The link walk now recognises all four forms (`references/linked-sources.md` §1), resolves a wikilink against its own file's directory — as written, then with `.md` added where the name does not already end in a markdown or image extension, so `[[appendix]]` beside `appendix.md` resolves in a customer's folder with no vault at all and `[[notes.v2]]` finds `notes.v2.md` — and only then by filename across the vault — the nearest ancestor of the document holding `.obsidian/` — and reports a name matching more than one file as `ambiguous`, with every candidate, choosing none — the link log names each candidate by its path relative to the vault's root, never by an absolute path that would write the operator's own directory layout into the specs repository.

**Re-running `/product-workflows:brd-intake <BRD-KEY> @<brd-file>` over the same folder captures them.** Nothing captured before is removed and the inventory's reconciliation keeps every existing id, so a row drawn from a file captured this time takes the next id after the highest in use; every defect already logged keeps its id too (below). **Ids are kept, and so is wording over an unchanged file**: a row the reconciliation matches keeps its id and, wherever the file its anchor points into is unchanged since the inventory's rows were last reconciled, its wording (below). The re-run is not free where the BRD has already been split: Phase 0 step 7 names every disposition the ledger holds and asks first, because a re-run whose read finds a requirement rewrites every ledger row to `unallocated` and `/brd-split`'s walk has to be taken again — that step lists what a re-run keeps and what it discards, and a read that finds none keeps the ledger as it stands (below).

### Fixed — a re-run of `/brd-intake` logged every defect a second time

`/brd-intake` Phase 4 gave each confirmed defect the next `[DEF#n]` and had no rule for a re-run, which re-extracts the inventory — and `brd-reader`, reading the same document, proposes the defects already logged all over again. Each was confirmed again under a new id. Measured on a byte-identical source, one re-run took a log of 15 `open` entries to 31, 13 of the new ones restating an old one on the same row and class, and left 13 of the old entries cited by no inventory row. The only other reading of the phase, numbering from `[DEF#1]` again, would have reused ids for different defects. It cited `brd-format.md` §3–§4 for *"ids are never reused or renumbered"*, and neither section said so. **Population: every `/brd-intake` re-run over a folder holding a defect log — on 3.6.x as well — and, with this release, every such duplicate of a defect in the five classes `/brd-interview` asks — `unsourced` is not among them — becoming a second customer question**, since `/brd-interview` now puts each such open defect to the customer where a slice owns it (above), and a re-minted id is one no slice has asked. **The worst case is a duplicate of an entry `/brd-reconcile` had already resolved**: the re-minted entry is `open`, so it asks the customer a question they have already answered. The wikilink fix above tells every Obsidian-authored BRD to re-run.

`brd-format.md` §4 now states that a `[DEF#n]` id is permanent — never reused, never renumbered, never deleted — as a `[BR#n]` is. On a re-run, Phase 4 matches each candidate against the log on file before asking, every row taken as Phase 3's id reconciliation numbers it: the same class raised on the same row, or, for a `conflict` or a `duplicate`, the same rows joined — in either direction, since a split can be raised from the whole naming each part or from each part naming the whole, so a candidate matches where entries on file of its class already join every pair of rows it joins. A match is that entry, or those entries — not asked again, ids, reasons and resolutions unchanged, so a defect `/brd-reconcile` resolved stays resolved. Only an unmatched candidate is walked, numbered after the highest id on file. Where several candidates and entries share one class and row they pair in order, and the order decides only which pairs: a candidate left over is walked with every entry on file of its class and row shown beside it, and the question says *Reject — not a defect* is the answer where it restates one; a `conflict` or `duplicate` only some of whose pairs entries on file join is walked with each entry of its class joining any of them shown beside it the same way, and, confirmed, is recorded for the pairs none of them joins and no other — each joined pair is a defect on file already, so confirming the whole candidate would log it twice, and rejecting it would drop the pairs nothing on file joins — its question naming both sets; and a group of one read's candidates, walked as one, is matched against the log the same way before it is walked, since its pairs — its row with each other row the group joins — need not be any of its members'. An entry this read did not propose again — no candidate of its class raised on its row, matched or not, so an entry the pairing left over still counts as re-raised; for a `conflict` or a `duplicate`, one no pair of whose rows a candidate of its class joins, since such an entry counts as re-raised wherever any pair it joins is, so one a candidate matched is never also reported not re-raised — is kept as it stands and reported as *not re-raised by this extraction*, and every row that cited it keeps citing it; a `conflict` or `duplicate` entry only some of whose pairs a candidate joins stays re-raised, and the report names beside it the pairs no candidate joined. **A log an earlier re-run already doubled is not repaired**: every id in it is permanent now, and which of two entries restating one defect to resolve, and how, is a person's call.

### Fixed — the four record files `/brd-intake` writes had no layout, and a verbatim requirement did not fit the one every run chose

`/brd-intake` Phase 2 wrote `brd/brd-link-log.md` "in the shape `brd-format.md` §1.1 fixes", and §1.1 listed what the log holds and fixed no shape; §2, §4 and `coverage-ledger-format.md` §2 fixed the fields of the inventory, the defect log and the ledger and no file around them. So each verification run invented a layout: one named the document in a label line above the title and another in the title itself, one gave an `ambiguous` link's candidates a column and another folded them into the reason, and each annotated its `files copied` count by hand because nothing said whether the document counts — while `brd-format.md` §1.1 had every reader of the name, a slice's inventory header and `/brd-package` Phase 0 among them, take it off the log's "opening line", whichever line a run had put first. And the markdown table every run chose cannot hold a requirement verbatim: a quoted row of the customer's own table carries `|` and a multi-line list item carries line breaks, which one run escaped as `\|` and `<br>` and nothing said to, changing the quote's bytes. **Population: every `/brd-intake` run, every `/brd-split` run that creates a slice, and every reader of the four files.** Each file now has one layout, fixed in its owner — `brd-format.md` §1.1 for the link log, §2 for the inventory, §4 for the defect log and `coverage-ledger-format.md` §2 for the ledger: frontmatter carrying `kind` and `key`, a fixed opening line where a reader needs one — the link log's `Source document: <basename>` — fixed count labels, and a table with named columns. The inventory's `kind: brd` is the folder's carrier, as before; the other three name their own document — `brd-link-log`, `brd-defect-log` and `coverage-ledger` — a kind `workflows-core:addressing` §4 passes over, so a ledger at a root BRD's top level is never read as the folder's identity (`workflows-core` 1.7.1's §4 now says the inventory is the only artifact there naming a folder kind). The link log's `files copied` leaves the document itself out, as Phase 1's count does. `brd-format.md` §2.3 fixes **one cell encoding for all four**, and for the figures file's *Annotations* table: a literal `|` is written `\|`, every backslash the customer wrote directly before one doubled first — so their own `\|` is written `\\\|` — and a line break `<br>`; "verbatim" means verbatim after decoding. **Every read parses and decodes by one rule, fixed there once** — a row splits on a delimiting `|`, one that opens the row or has a space directly before it, since every writer puts one space on each side of a cell, so a cell ending in the customer's own backslash — `C:\reports\` — never escapes the `|` that closes it — whatever it reads a cell for: to show it to the operator (`/brd-intake` Phase 4, `/brd-split`'s offer and walk), to work from it or hand it to an agent (`/brd-split`'s placement, `/prd-ground`'s claim list), or to compare it (`/brd-intake` Phase 3's match, relation 1, and `/brd-package`'s corpus parse, the ledger's included, and verbatim spans — `bundle-packaging.md` §2.1, §6.1, §6.3). A markdown link inside a quoted requirement is quotation: kept as written, never followed or repaired, though its target resolves to nothing from `brd/` — and `/brd-package`'s de-Obsidianising pass, which rewrites every wikilink the package wrote, leaves one quoted in an inventory or ledger `text` cell as written (`bundle-packaging.md` §2.1). A file written before this release is read as it stands, and rewritten in its layout by the next run that writes the whole file; a writer that edits a cell in place — `/brd-split`'s walk, `/brd-reconcile`'s resolutions and dispositions — leaves it in its own layout until then, and `/brd-split` encodes a cell of a pre-layout parent's inventory as it copies it into a slice. A pre-3.7.0 link log whose opening line names no single document under `brd/source/` makes its reader ask the operator rather than choose, and a pre-3.7.0 defect-log entry is read with the first row it was recorded against as its `raised on` and any other as its `names`. `/brd-split` writes each slice's inventory and ledger in the same layouts. Beside them, `brd-format.md` §4 said a `customer-amended` amendment "is held in the ledger beside the original", where no ledger column holds it; the row now names the returned review the amendment came from, which is where `/brd-reconcile` reads it, and that command's two sentences holding it "beside the original", and its documentation page's, now name the slice's returned review too — no run's behaviour changed.

### Fixed — a split became one defect per part, and a `conflict` or `duplicate` was listed on whichever rows a run chose

`brd-format.md` §2 recorded a split — one numbered item binding the delivery team to several obligations, inventoried as several rows — as **one** `duplicate` naming the sibling rows, while `brd-reader` put a `duplicate` candidate on every sibling and `/brd-intake` Phase 4 confirmed each: in two verification runs one three-way split became three defects, each naming the other two, and each an open defect `/brd-interview` would put to the customer as a question of its own. The split rows' `text` fitted neither of §2's forms, and the agent improvised, a blank line between lead-in and item for one sibling and a `…` line for the next. Phase 4 said to record a confirmed defect "against every `[BR#n]` it was raised on" without saying whether a `conflict`'s or `duplicate`'s counterpart row lists it too, fixed no order inside a class, which decided the numbering, and fixed the answer array for each candidate but not the question. **Population: every intake of a source holding an item with more than one obligation, every intake confirming a `conflict` or `duplicate` or more than one defect of a class, and every Phase 4 question.** `brd-reader` now proposes one `duplicate` per split, on the first row the split produced, naming every other row it produced, and Phase 4 confirms it once. **Every other `conflict` or `duplicate` is raised once too, from one end** (`brd-format.md` §3) — a part on the whole, a restatement or a clash on the lowest-numbered of its rows. `brd-reader` had no such rule, so a clash between two rows could be proposed from each, each confirmed, and the customer asked about one clash twice; Phase 4 now also groups, before walking, the candidates of one read and one class that share a pair of rows, directly or through another candidate of the group, and walks each group as one candidate — raised on the row §3 fixes, naming every other row the group joins — the other candidates folded into it, their reasons on an *Also raised* line, so a read that raises one relation from both ends, or among three or more rows as several candidates each naming some of the others, asks it once. **Population: every first intake whose document states such a relation.** A split row's `text` takes one fixed form, `<lead-in> … <item>` (`brd-format.md` §2), so siblings sharing one anchor differ in text; a split row an earlier release wrote in another shape — its lead-in, then a line break or a line holding an ellipsis alone (`…`, `[…]` or `...`), then its item behind a list marker — is rewritten into that form before a re-run's Phase 3 compares it, so it matches the new read's row rather than being minted again beside it (below); one whose lead-in or item itself spans more than one line cannot be split into the two and is not rewritten, and where the same-or-new question applies it is shown there beside the new read's row. A `conflict` or `duplicate` is listed in the `defects` column of the row it was raised on only, its counterparts named in the entry (§4) — the reading `/brd-reconcile` Phase 8 already took — and `/brd-interview`'s requirement-defect source, which said a log entry does not say which rows list its defect, now reads a defect's rows off its entry — the row it was raised on and every row it names — and decides ownership over all of them, lowest-numbered live row first: on the one-row listing alone, a split whose first row no slice builds was withheld as `no live row` while another slice built a sibling row. **Population: every split, restatement or clash whose raised-on row no slice builds while a row it names is built.** Ids are assigned when the walk ends, in the inventory order of the row raised on, then §3's class order, then the order `brd-reader` returned them in, and after them those raised from documentation, in the order the grounder returned them — never in the order the walk asked, which documentation grounding reorders. Each candidate's question now carries the same facts: its class, its row and that row's text, its reason, each counterpart a `conflict` or `duplicate` names, and, for a row drawn from an image, the image's path. **A log an earlier run already split three ways is not merged**: every id in it is permanent, and a re-run matches the one new candidate against those entries by the rows they join, none of the three then reported as not re-raised (above).

### Fixed — a re-run reworded requirements over an unchanged source, and filed a row it had only not re-read as one the source no longer contains

Phase 3 matched a returned row to one on file "by `source_anchor`, else by its `text`". The anchor half assumed one anchor names one row, while the rows one split produced share one — three in one verification run, each matching all three; the text half never said whether text must match exactly, or past a `<br>`; and a row matched by anchor silently took the new read's wording. Measured on a byte-identical source, a re-run rewrote the `text` of 10 of 14 inventory rows and their ledger rows, and in another a row lost *"finance approval completes it"*, the dropped words then minted as a new id. A row the read did not return was kept as one *this source no longer contains* and passed over by relation 1 even where the source had not changed, and nothing said whether it kept its `defects`. And an `EMPTY` read over an intaken BRD rewrote its inventory with no row while `brd/brd-defect-log.md` kept entries citing the dropped ids, so the next readable re-run numbered from `[BR#1]` and every kept entry named whichever requirement took its old id. **Population: every `/brd-intake` re-run over an intaken folder, on 3.6.x as well — and, for the defect log below, every first intake whose read found no requirement.** Phase 2 now records each file a re-run captures as unchanged, replaced or new (below). Phase 3 matches in two passes — by resolved anchor, one existing row to one returned row, and among rows sharing an anchor by text alone; then by text — comparing text decoded (`brd-format.md` §2.3) with every run of whitespace collapsed. A matched row keeps its wording wherever the file its anchor points into is unchanged since the inventory's rows were last reconciled, and takes the new wording, reported old → new, only where Phase 2 records that file replaced. **That is judged against a record the inventory now carries, never against the copy on disk**: its frontmatter's `document:` names the document the rows were read from, and its `captured:` map holds the SHA-256 of every captured file as the rows were last reconciled (`brd-format.md` §2). Judged against the copy, a run that stopped between Phase 2's copy and Phase 3's write, or a read that found no requirement, left a replaced copy that the next run found unchanged, and a revised requirement kept its old wording. The document is judged against the map's entry for the one `document:` names, whatever either is called — never against the document `brd/brd-link-log.md` opens by naming, which every Phase 2 rewrites, so a run that copied a renamed document and stopped before Phase 3 would have left the next run judging it new and keeping its predecessor's text — and a revised document sent under a new filename rewords the rows anchored in it. `/brd-split`, which names the document in a slice inventory's `source:`, and `/brd-package`, which names it in the bundle, read the same `document:` — the parent's on a slice — and not the log's opening line, which after such a stopped run names a document no row was read from; on an inventory carrying no `document:` — one written before 3.7.0 that no 3.7.0 intake has run over — each still reads the log (`brd-format.md` §1.1). **The first 3.7.0 intake over an inventory written before 3.7.0 that holds a row writes that inventory's `document:` in Phase 2, before it copies anything or rewrites the log**, naming the document as §1.1 tells a reader to — off the log it is about to replace, or by that section's rules for an older log or none — and adding that one line alone, `kind: brd` and `key:` unchanged, so the folder is never keyless; it writes no `captured:` map, which nothing on file could fill, and Phase 2 now tests for the map rather than for both fields. Before, a run that stopped before Phase 3 left such an inventory with no `document:` beside a log naming the document it copied, which every reader then took for the one the rows were read from; `brd-format.md` §1.1 stated that as a limit, and no longer does — what it still states is the same case left by an intake before 3.7.0, which no record tells apart. **Population: every 3.7.0 re-run over a BRD intaken before 3.7.0 whose inventory holds a row, and every `/brd-split` or `/brd-package` run after such a re-run stopped before Phase 3.** A file recorded replaced whose copy already stood with its bytes — an earlier run copied it and reconciled no row against it — is named so in the final report, so the operator sees why a file nothing was written to is called replaced. **A one-time migration comes with it**: an inventory written before this release carries neither record, so its first re-run on this release records every file whose copy already stands replaced, and the document whatever its name, and rewords every matched row to the new read's wording, once, each change reported old → new, and writes the map every later run judges against — **population: the first 3.7.0 re-run over each folder intaken before 3.7.0 that holds an inventory row**. Where a returned row is still unmatched over a file recorded unchanged, beside a row on file still unmatched under the same anchor, Phase 3 now asks the operator whether it is the same requirement — showing every row on file still unmatched under that anchor, a typed `[BR#n]` choosing among them — rather than minting a second id for one a read worded or cut differently, and every id a re-run mints is reported by id, never as a count. **Of the two measured cases, the second is only partly fixed, and is recorded as such**: `[BR#7]` now keeps its words over the unchanged file, but the read's row for *"finance approval completes it"* carries a different anchor, so no pass matches it and the same-or-new question, which asks only about rows sharing an anchor, never reaches it — it is still minted, as `[BR#14]`, and is now reported by id with its text, where the operator can see that it restates part of `[BR#7]`. **Population, for the record: every re-run after a run that copied a revised file and reconciled no row against it, and every re-run over a document revised under a new filename; for the question: every re-run over an unchanged file holding rows that share an anchor.** A row nothing matched is kept whole, `defects` included: as *not re-extracted by this read* where its anchor still resolves, which relation 1 still tests, and as one *this source no longer contains* only where it does not. An `EMPTY` read over an earlier intake's inventory keeps the inventory, its `document:` and `captured:` map included, the defect log, the ledger and each figures section's *Rows* line exactly as they stand and renumbers nothing — a *Rows* line naming kept rows, `illustrates` included, is not recomputed from a read that returned none (`brd-format.md` §1.2), its final report's ledger line resolves any `covered-by` row one hop, as `coverage-ledger-format.md` §6 fixes, and Phase 8 offers `/brd-split` only where a ledger row is still `unallocated`, since a bare run over a ledger its walk already settled moves nothing; on a first intake it still writes the inventory and the ledger with no row, and now the defect log too, which Phase 7 declared and nothing wrote. Phase 0 step 7's confirmation, skipped where every ledger row was still `unallocated` although the re-run rewrites the inventory, now fires wherever an earlier intake's inventory holds a row, and says what the re-run keeps — every id, every defect with its resolution, a row's wording over an unchanged file, an unchanged image's transcription — and what it may change, every ledger disposition among them. The remedies that name this re-run as the way to re-take an inventory — in `/create-prd`, `/create-ard`, `/specify`, `/epics` and `coverage-ledger-format.md` §5, and the `/create-prd` and `/prd-ground` documentation pages — said it rewrites the ledger with every row `unallocated`, which an `EMPTY` read does not do; each now says so wherever its read finds a requirement, citing that step for the rest.

### Fixed — `coverage-ledger-format` §3 said no command moves a row back to `unallocated`, beside the one that does

§3 said *no command ever moves a row back to `unallocated`*, and the root-level remedies in `/create-prd`, `/create-ard`, `/specify` and `/epics` quoted it — while each of the same remedies offers a `/brd-intake` re-run, which rewrites a source-owning BRD's ledger with every row `unallocated`, as it did on 3.6.x. §3 now names that re-run as the one exception: taken only on the operator's consent at its Phase 0 step 7, and never reaching a slice's ledger. The four quotes follow it; the reason those remedies give for a bare `/brd-split` being a no-op now says nothing *but that re-run* moves a terminal row back; the three sentences about a slice's non-empty `CREATE_PRD_BRD_NOT_ELIGIBLE` branch — in `/epics`, `/update-prd` and §5.2 — now say no command moves a *slice's* row back; the removal repair in `/brd-split`, its documentation page and §3.2, which said `unallocated` is a value no command may write, now says `/brd-split` never writes it onto a row carrying a fate, and the `/brd-split` re-cut's seeding sentence scopes the same claim to a slice's ledger; and `docs/brd-workflow.md`, which said no command returns a row to `unallocated`, now names the re-run that reopens every row at once, as does the 2.1.0 entry below that said the same. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of those sentences — an agent taking one as proof that no allocation can be re-taken.**

### Fixed — a revised document was written over the copy `brd-format.md` called immutable, and nothing said so

`brd-format.md` §1 said nothing under `brd/source/` is ever edited after intake; §1.1 called the document's re-copy on a re-run "additive"; and Phase 2 copied a revised document over `brd/source/<basename>` without comparing it or reporting that it had. **Population: every `/brd-intake` re-run over a revised document, or over one whose linked file at the same path changed.** §1 now states the rule both sections follow: `/brd-intake` Phase 2 alone writes under `brd/source/`, only ever a byte-for-byte copy; a re-run replaces a file's copy only where its bytes changed, git keeping the earlier one, leaves an unchanged file untouched and removes nothing; and the final report names every file it records replaced. A document revised under a new filename is copied at that name beside the earlier copy, which stays, and the final report names each slice whose inventory's `source:` still names an earlier document, whose rows keep resolving against it. `brd/source-external/` never replaces a copy at all: a changed file lands beside the old one under collision rule 2's name.

### Fixed — five smaller `/brd-intake` defects

- **A path typed before the key became the key.** Phase 0 step 1 took the first token that is neither a flag nor a flag's value, so `@<brd-file>` typed first stopped the run with `BRD_INTAKE_NEEDS_KEY`. It now passes over a token opening with `@`, which is step 2's source wherever it stands. **Population: every run whose operator types the path first.**
- **"Kebab-cased" did not say what becomes of punctuation.** Step 7's slug now lowercases the first heading, turns every run of characters outside `[a-z0-9]` into one `-` and trims both ends — `Acme reporting — business requirements` gives `acme-reporting-business-requirements` — falling back to the filename by the same rule, and to `brd` where both leave nothing. **Population: every new BRD whose first heading carries anything but letters, digits, spaces and hyphens.**
- **A declined handoff had no outcome line, and the final report asked for a note defined nowhere.** Phase 7 named an outcome line for its first choice only; the other two now emit `workflows-core:phase-handoff` §4.1's *Declined by the user* line with the advisory clause the array promised. The final report's "`brd` prefix note" is gone. **Population: every run whose handoff was declined, and every final report.**
- **`<how to cut it>` had no value.** Phase 8's `/brd-split` offer now says it is printed as it stands, a placeholder the operator fills with their own slicing instruction. **Population: every run offering it.**
- **`Proceed with <folder>` did not say name or path.** It is the folder's name. **Population: every run.**

### Fixed — a new BRD folder was keyless from its first copy until its inventory was written

`workflows-core:addressing` §4 requires the command that creates a folder to write a keyed artifact into it **in the same act**, so a folder is never keyless. `/brd-intake` Phase 2 created the BRD folder by copying the customer's document into it, and nothing in that folder carried `kind:` and `key:` until Phase 3 wrote `brd/brd-inventory.md` — so every stop between them (a failed copy, a `figure-reader` batch that could not run, an interrupted session) left a folder `resolve-address` could not identify, and a re-run over it resolved `absent`. Phase 2 now **keys the folder first**: where the folder holds no `brd/brd-inventory.md` — always so for a new one — it writes that file's header, `kind: brd` and `key: <BRD-KEY>` between `---` lines and no row, before anything is copied, and Phase 3 fills in the rows below it. A run that stops in between leaves a header-only inventory — as a Phase 3 `NOT_FOUND` stop and a completed first intake whose read was `EMPTY` do — and Phase 3 now says an inventory holding no row is **no prior inventory**, whatever left it so: a re-run numbers as a first intake does, every first-intake rule applying. Phase 3's `EMPTY` branch said to write "an empty `brd/brd-inventory.md`", which read as a zero-byte file would un-key the folder again; it now writes the header alone. `workflows-core` 1.7.1's `addressing` §4 names `brd/brd-inventory.md` as the carrier a root BRD is resolved by, which is what makes the header enough. **Population: every `/brd-intake` run that creates its folder**; on a folder already holding an inventory, Phase 2 leaves it as it stands.

### Fixed — `brd-format` showed the inventory header without its frontmatter delimiters, and gave a reason about `addressing` §4 that no longer holds

§2.1's header block listed `kind:`, `key:`, `parent:` and `source:` with no `---` lines, where the family's other artifact templates show their frontmatter inside the block. It now shows the delimiters and says the file opens with frontmatter; the paragraph under it says `workflows-core:addressing` §4 reads a source-owning BRD's identity off this file where no top-level file carries `key:` beside a `kind:` naming a folder kind, that `/brd-intake` writes the header, with no row, before it copies anything into a new folder (its Phase 2), and that a slice's inventory, which carries the pair too, is never the slice's carrier — its `brd-link.md` is. It had called the two fields *"how the folder asserts its own identity"* of every inventory, a slice's included. §1.2's bullet on `brd/brd-figures.md`'s frontmatter justified carrying `key:` and no `kind:` by §4 reading "the first artifact in it carrying both" with "nothing fixes which is first"; `workflows-core` 1.7.1 fixes the order and names the inventory as the one file under `brd/` it reads, so the bullet now gives that as the reason. **Population: every writer of an inventory's header — `/brd-intake` Phase 2, which writes a source-owning BRD's before anything is copied, and `/brd-split` Phase 3 step 4, which writes a slice's and cites §2.1 — and every reader of either section.**

### Fixed — `/brd-package` stopped on its own inventory's section anchors

The plugin-free scan's classes include *"any `§`"*, Phase 8 rule 7 runs that scan over every document in the finished bundle, and on 3.6.x its only exemption was the customer's source document. The inventory ships in every bundle, and a `source_anchor` locates a requirement in the customer's own numbering — `brd-format.md` §2.2 records that every anchor in the corpora it was measured on opens with a `§` and a section number. So read literally, rule 7 stopped every package whose inventory carries such an anchor, on text the plugin copied from the customer. **Population: every `/brd-package` run on 3.6.x over an inventory with one.** A customer-derived locator's leading section reference, in its fixed position — opening a `source_anchor`, or opening what follows the ` › ` after a captured file's path — is now no hit at all (`bundle-packaging.md` §6.3 rule 1), and the reader resolves it by opening the customer's file, which the bundle carries.

### Changed — `/brd-package` ships what the customer wrote, and reports rather than stops on it

The bundle now carries every markdown file `/brd-intake` captured, from `brd/source/` and `brd/source-external/`, byte for byte as the source document always was; `brd/brd-figures.md` where it exists, its frontmatter replaced by a note naming the customer's document; and every image the figures file holds a section for, save one a later intake no longer captured and no preserved row still anchors on, together with every image a captured file reaches through a wikilink or from outside its folder, found through the link log (`bundle-packaging.md` §1.1, §2.1). The manifest maps each captured file's path relative to `brd/` — the form an appendix or image anchor, a figures heading and an interview question naming an image all use — to its bundled filename. Each bundle document's filename now **begins** `<BRD-KEY>-`, the form the citation-resolution check already tested for; 3.6.x required only that the name carry the key, and a document carrying it elsewhere was one that check's filename relation skipped. The prompt's *documents to review* part names the manifest and then every other document `bundle-packaging.md` §1.1 admits, by those bundled filenames; 3.6.x filled it from the manifest, which a later phase writes.

**A plugin token in the customer's own words, or in a customer-derived locator, is now reported rather than stopped on**, by the plugin-free scan and the citation-resolution check alike. 3.6.x gave that treatment to the customer's source document alone. It now reaches every captured file; the parts of the figures file that transcribe the image's own words — its *Text*, the *Says* of its annotations, its *Flow*; the inventory's and the ledger's requirement text, where it quotes the customer; and every customer-derived locator — the customer's names for their own files, headings and links, in the positions `bundle-packaging.md` §6.3 recognises them in, including this package's own writing. Hits there are grouped, one line per file and token with a count, and the operator decides whether to ship: a `D3` in a customer's diagram or a `references/` folder in their layout had no repair but renaming their files, retitling their headings or making a transcription say something the image does not, and each of those falsifies the record. Every other hit stays a hard stop.

**Population: every package** — the report-not-stop treatment reaches the inventory's and the ledger's text and the anchors of any BRD — **and, for the larger bundle, every package over a BRD whose intake captured a markdown file beside the document or wrote a figures file.** **What it costs**: that larger bundle, carrying every captured page and image and the transcriptions, and a grouped report of hits inside the customer's own content that the operator rules on before the package ships, where 3.6.x either stopped or never looked. **An appendix the current document no longer links still ships**, with the images it embeds: a captured file is never removed from `brd/source/` or `brd/source-external/`, and nothing records which intake captured a markdown file, so the bundle cannot tell a page the latest intake read from one an earlier intake captured.

**One position is deliberately not relaxed.** A customer heading or section number quoted in this package's own prose **without its captured file's path** — `(see § 4.2)` — stays a hard stop, as every `§` in plugin-written text was on 3.6.x: a pathless `§` there cannot be told apart from a plugin citation. The repair is to write it as a path, `source/<basename> › § 4.2`, whose leading section reference is exempt.

### Added — `/brd-intake` reads what it captures

On 3.6.x the intake copied every file the document linked from its own folder and read none of them — `brd-reader` read the document alone — so a requirement stated only in an appendix or only in a screenshot yielded no `[BR#n]`, and no later command read those pictures either. Customers' BRDs routinely carry annotated screenshots of the current product, mockups, sample outputs the result must match, and diagrams. Now:

- **Phase 1 walks every link first, read-only** (`references/linked-sources.md` §4), before anything is written: it shows how many files of each kind — markdown, image, other — lie inside the document's folder and outside it, and every target it could not resolve. Where anything lies outside the folder it asks first — *Capture all*, *Only the document's own folder — capture nothing outside it*, or *Stop* — and where the taken set holds a file that is neither markdown nor an image it asks next, with *Stop and convert them first* recommended: `BRD_INTAKE_UNREAD_ATTACHMENTS` names each such file, since it would be copied and never read.
- **Files from outside the folder land in `brd/source-external/`**, at their basename under `idea-format.md`'s collision rule, immutable as `brd/source/` is and never replaced by a later intake, a changed file landing beside the old one under a new name; `brd/brd-link-log.md` gains a second table mapping every captured link that does not resolve as written — a wikilink, an outside file, an absolute path — to its copy, and never rewrites the customer's link.
- **Phase 2.5 has `figure-reader`, a new Opus agent, transcribe every captured image** — each label, header, field, legend and annotation verbatim, what each annotation points at, a diagram's flow, and one sentence on what the image depicts — at most 10 images per dispatch and 4 dispatches at once, with no cap on the total. It never reads the document that links the image, so a transcription cannot be bent toward the prose. The transcriptions go to `brd/brd-figures.md` (`brd-format.md` §1.2), the plugin's record of its reading beside the link log, and a later intake re-uses a section whose image's content hash still matches instead of reading the image again.
- **`brd-reader` reads the document, every captured markdown file and the transcriptions together.** A row can now anchor in an appendix (`<path> › <heading path or line range>`) or on an image (`<path> › "<element quoted verbatim>"` or `annotation <n>`), paths relative to `brd/`. An obligation only an image states, with no prose anywhere stating it, is proposed with an `ambiguity` candidate — *stated only in `<image>` — binding force unknown* — unless a passage linking the image makes it binding; an image contradicting a prose row is an ordinary `conflict`. Phase 4 puts an image-drawn candidate to the operator with its transcription and tells them to open the picture, since the transcription is the plugin's reading and the picture is what the candidate is about.
- **The coverage check reaches every captured file.** Every top-level section of each captured markdown file must hold a row or be accounted for, as the document's always had to, and every image must yield a row, illustrate one, or be accounted for; Phase 5 records which on each image's *Rows* line. A section holds a row where an anchor names it, or where it links an image or a captured markdown file that yields one — so a section whose only content is a link to an appendix is not put to the operator as empty. `brd-format.md` §2.2 now defines a top-level section: where one heading titles a file, the sections at the shallowest level beneath that title, so the ordinary shape of one `#` title over `##` sections is checked at `##` granularity rather than as one section that is always held — and every heading path, in an anchor or a figures file's *Linked from*, leaves that title out. **Population: every intake of a document one heading titles** — on 3.6.x the check ran over the document with the term undefined, and read literally could never fire on such a document — **and what it costs**: more Phase 3 coverage prompts, and different ones, since each `##` section holding no row is now put to the operator, while a section whose only content links an appendix that yields a row no longer is.

**What it costs.** Every image the intake takes is transcribed on Opus on first intake, ten to a dispatch, and the intake may ask one or two more questions at Phase 1. A BRD linking a PDF or a spreadsheet, which 3.6.x copied without comment and never read, now meets a question whose recommended answer stops the run until the file is converted, or until the operator records that it carries no obligation. **Transcription fidelity on real images is unmeasured** — low resolution, scribbled mark-up and where an arrow lands are caught, when they are caught, by Phase 4's operator and by the customer, whose review now confirms or corrects the transcriptions of their own images (`customer-review-schema.md` §4, section 4). A correction the transcription itself must carry comes back as a section-12 required change naming the figures file, and `/brd-reconcile` Phase 6 applies it there in place — to a section's *Text*, *Annotations*, *Flow*, *Illegible* or *Depicts*, never to the image, and never to an inventory row quoting the corrected element, which goes to the reconciliation record's *what still needs a human*. A corrected transcription is not corrected requirement text, so it closes no defect: one the row carries stays open for its own question. A later intake keeps the corrected section for as long as the image's bytes are unchanged. **A re-intake after such a correction mints a second `[BR#n]` for the image-drawn row**: the read quotes the corrected element, which no longer matches the earlier row, so the earlier row is kept as one this source no longer contains, and the new row's binding-force `ambiguity` is a new defect that may be asked again.

### Changed — `/idea` walks every link and asks rather than caps

`idea-reader` followed links two levels deep and read at most twelve markdown files, the source among them, and six images, reporting the rest `reason: cap` or `reason: depth`. Those bounds are gone. **Phase 1.5** runs the same walk `/brd-intake` runs, from a markdown source, and asks nothing where the walk stays inside all three old bounds; where it reaches past any of them — a markdown file deeper than two levels, more than twelve markdown files, or more than six images — it names every file past the bound and asks: *Read all*, *Only what the source links directly*, or *Stop*. A file left out is reported `excluded`. `figure-reader` then transcribes every taken image, in parallel, before `idea-reader` runs, and each image's digest `description` is the transcription's `depicts` sentence; `idea-reader` reads the source, exactly the pages the walk took, and the transcriptions, and follows no link and opens no image itself. A `[[name]]` resolves the way it does for `/brd-intake`, so two runs over one vault no longer resolve a bare name differently. **What it costs**: an image is now read on Opus rather than on `idea-reader`'s Sonnet tier, and Phase 4.5 vendors every page the walk took and every image `figure-reader` read, with no cap of its own, so a source linking a hub note can leave dozens of copies for its handoff to stage.

### Fixed — nine defects in `/idea` that its verification runs found

Nine defects the verification runs of this release met in `/idea`, each confirmed on the tree as it stood before the release began; none is introduced by it.

- **Phase 0 said it created a folder it had no name for.** It resolved the key and said `PRD-<KEY>-<slug>/` is "created there when absent", but the only slug a run holds is `idea-reader`'s `candidate_slug`, which arrives in Phase 2, and Phase 4 then wrote into "the folder Phase 0 resolved" — none, on `absent`. Phase 0 now creates nothing: on `absent` the folder is created by Phase 4's first write, as `PRD-<KEY>-<candidate_slug>/` with `idea.md` in it, and Phase 4 and Phase 5 name it as the folder resolved or created. The command's documentation page says so, no longer says a key that resolves to nothing stops the run, and names Phase 0 as the command does. Phase 4's `kind`/`key` step, which said this command creates the folder and so writes its only keyed file, now says so only where Phase 0 returned `absent`. **Population: every `/idea` run on a key that has no folder yet** — the ordinary first run; each verification run created the folder in Phase 4 on its own judgement.
- **A correct `<KEY> @<file>` invocation read as a path that resolves to nothing, and every prompt read its own key as idea text.** Phase 1 classified the argument with only the flags removed, so the Phase 0 key token and the `@` were still in it, and a literal reading lands on Case B's *Re-enter the path* on a run with nothing wrong. Phase 1 now removes the key token and every flag with its value, and a leading `@` marks a file whose path is the text after it — the path rule 1 tests and Phase 2 hands `idea-reader`, whose input now says so. **Population: every `/idea` run** — over a markdown source a literal reading lands on Case B, and on a prompt the key went into the raw idea text handed to `idea-reader`.
- **The `docs grounding:` line was shown before anything had resolved it.** Phase 1 showed it "as resolved", while the command's only `resolve-docs-grounding` call sat in Phase 2.5. Phase 1 now runs that procedure itself and shows the line from what it returns, so its one-time question about building the docs index comes before the link walk and the readers, as `workflows-core:docs-grounding` step 3.5 asks; Phase 2.5 dispatches the docs grounder on the state Phase 1 resolved and resolves nothing again. **Population: every `/idea` run** — as written, only under `--no-docs` was the line's value knowable at Phase 1.
- **Phase 2.5 said to dispatch "both grounding agents" in one response.** It dispatches one, the docs grounder; Phase 2.6 says in its own words why code grounding is kept out of Phase 2.5. The sentence now says so. `/create-prd` Phase 2.5 carried the same sentence, and beside it a BRD-route paragraph handing a `known_refs` input to no agent — both left behind when the prior-art finder was removed — and it now names the one agent and the inputs it takes. **No run's behaviour changed**: neither Phase 2.5 has a second agent to dispatch. **Population: every reader of either phase.**
- **`--docs <path>` was missing from the Flags paragraph**, which Phase 0 cited as "the flag list below" although it stands above; Phase 0 and Phase 1 both parse `--docs` as a flag that takes a value. The paragraph lists it and Phase 0 cites it as the paragraph above. **Population: every reader looking the flag up** — the parsing was already right.
- **A link in `idea.md` to the source file itself was never repointed at its copy.** Phase 4.5 copies the source into `attachments/`, but the rewrite map was keyed only from the digest's link arrays, which never hold the source, so a `## Signals & evidence` citation of the source brief kept pointing at the operator's disk beside its own copy — two of the three verification runs left one there, and the third repointed it only by treating the reference as a key the text never named. The map now has a row for the source, keyed by its `source_refs[].ref` exactly as the digest records it and valued at its `attachments/` copy, and Section 5 names that as the form to cite the source in. **Population: every `/idea` run over a markdown source whose brief links the source file**; a brief written before this release keeps that link as it stands.
- **Whether a linked page or image is an "ingested source" was left open.** `sources` listed "every ingested source", and Phase 4.5 recorded `vendored:` "beside each vendored entry's `ref:`", while `sources:` held the source alone. It is not one: `sources` names the one source each run ingests and, where it was copied, its `vendored:` path, and a linked page's or image's copy is recorded by the rewritten link in `idea.md` that points at it and, for an image, by its index row. **Population: every `/idea` run that vendored a linked page or image** — each verification run wrote the one entry, which is now the rule.
- **An index row's `Linked from` named a path on the operator's machine.** `idea-format.md` made it the image's digest `from`, an absolute path, while `workflows-core:grounding-format` §6.2's example showed a relative one. It now names the linking file by its vendored copy's path relative to the PRD folder (`attachments/p3.md`), its own path where it already sat inside the folder, or `—` where its copy failed; §6.2 owns the rule, changed in `workflows-core` 1.7.1. **Population: every `/idea` run that vendored an image**; a row an earlier run wrote keeps its absolute path, because §6.2 step 2 preserves every described row whose frame is still there verbatim.
- **`idea-reader` described `NOT_FOUND` as an invalid key, and could omit `signals`.** It takes no key: `NOT_FOUND` is a missing or unreadable source file, in the agent and in Phase 2. And `signals` joins the arrays returned as `[]` when empty and never omitted — one verification run's reader omitted it, and the rule as written allowed that. **Population: every `/idea` run whose source carries no demand signal**, and every reader of the `NOT_FOUND` line.

Three more stale lines sat beside the text these fixes changed, and are corrected with them: Phase 1 said two cases put the classification to the operator where only Case B is left, Case A having gone with the tracker export; `idea-format.md` Section 6 said a prior-art bullet takes "one of two shapes" directly above the paragraph saying there is one; and Phase 4 cited the `ambiguous` stop as `workflows-core:addressing` "§4 rule 5", a rule §4 never had — the stop is §3's, `resolve-key` step 5. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of any of them.**

**Two `provenance` values nothing produces are gone.** `idea-format.md`'s `sources` enum listed `rfe` and `doc-grounding`, `idea-reader` listed `rfe` among the hints it takes and the provenances it returns and kept an `rfe` split for product feedback, `/idea` Phase 1's future `--as` override named `rfe`, and `workflows-core:prd-format`'s propagated `sources` listed it with an "RFE key" ref, as `/create-prd`'s propagation note did. No command produces either value — Phase 1 classifies a prompt or a markdown file and `idea-reader` upgrades a markdown file to `community-post` or `prd` — so each list now carries only what a run writes, and `idea-reader`'s hint is the two values its caller computes. A value left in an existing `idea.md`, or propagated from one into a PRD, stays readable: nothing decides anything on it, and `idea-format.md` says so. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of those lists.**

### Fixed — `/idea` accepted any folder its key named, left `source_refs` unpinned, and grounded on themes nothing produced

Four `/idea` defects that predate this release:

- **`IDEA_NOT_AN_IDEA_FOLDER` is new.** Phase 0 treated any folder a key resolved to as the one to write the brief into, whatever it was. A key naming a `BRD-` container, a BRD-route slice (a `PRD-` folder carrying `brd-link.md`) or an `EPIC-` folder had an idea brief written where no PRD is ever seeded from one. Phase 0 now tests the folder itself, never the kind it asserts, since a slice asserts `kind: brd`: a `brd-link.md` naming a `parent:` at its top level first, then its prefix where the folder resolved through the prefixed glob, and positive evidence instead where it resolved through the legacy unprefixed fallback, whose name starts with the key, so a legacy folder keyed `BRD-12` is not taken for a BRD container by its name — a `coverage-ledger.md` or `brd/brd-inventory.md` for a BRD container, a resolved `kind: epic` for an Epic folder, as `workflows-core:addressing` §4.1 places a folder with no prefix. Every such folder stops the run with `IDEA_NOT_AN_IDEA_FOLDER`, naming the folder and what it is, before anything is read or written, and naming the run that does take that folder's work — `/create-prd <KEY>` for a slice; `/brd-split <KEY> "<how to cut it>"`, or `/create-prd <SLICE-KEY>` on a slice already carved, for a BRD container; `/update-prd <PRD-KEY>`, `<PRD-KEY>` being the key of the folder above, read off its carrier, or `/epics <KEY>` or `/specify <KEY>` for an Epic folder — beside a key of the idea's own for a genuinely separate idea. The paragraph after it, which said the key is checked against nothing, now says it is checked against no tracker and resolved only against the specs tree. **Population: every `/idea` run whose key already named a BRD, slice or Epic folder.**
- **`source_refs` is pinned.** `idea-reader`'s `source_refs` was an unbounded list of `path | KEY | url` refs, and nothing produces a key or a URL any more. It now holds exactly one entry for a markdown source, whose `ref` is the source's path exactly as the argument gave it, and none for a prompt. `idea-format.md`'s `sources` entry follows it: `{provenance, ref}` for a file, with `vendored` where the file was copied, and `{provenance: prompt}` with no `ref` for a prompt. `/idea`'s refine step writes that shape. `workflows-core:prd-format`'s propagated `sources` template now shows the `vendored` field and a prompt entry with no `ref`, and `/create-prd` propagates `vendored` with the rest of the entry where the idea it read is the `idea.md` in the PRD's own folder, and drops it otherwise: the path is relative to the folder holding that `idea.md`, so from any other folder it names nothing. **Population: every `/idea` run** — the ref that release 3.7.0's source-link rewrite keys on is now fixed rather than improvised.
- **The capability themes now have a producer.** Phase 2.5 handed the docs grounder the digest's `signals` as its `themes`, and those are demand bullets, empty for most sources.
