Phase 2.6 proposed repos from, and handed its scanners, "the `idea-reader` digest's themes", a field the digest does not have. Phase 2 now ends with the orchestrator naming 1–5 capability themes from the digest's `raw_context`, without changing `idea-reader`'s contract, and both phases use those. **Population: every `/idea` run with documentation grounding on, and every `--ground-code` run.**
- **Two smaller corrections.** Phase 0 said `--docs` and `--ground-code` "each consume the token after them", but `--ground-code` takes its value only on the conditions the Flags paragraph sets. The documentation page called the argument after the key "the single positional argument". Both now say what the command does. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of either.**

### Fixed — a declined `/idea` handoff sent the operator to a `/create-prd` that does not read the brief in contract

On `status: refined`, `/idea` Phase 5 presents `workflows-core:phase-handoff` §4.3's **gated — falling back** array, whose second option said the next phase *"reports the artifact as un-landed and proceeds from the resolved folder"*, and then recommended only `/create-prd <KEY>` with a merge clause. A declined handoff leaves `idea.md` on no ref, and `/create-prd <KEY>` on that state (row F) names the file and does not read it in contract: it goes on down its idea ladder — back to the brief only through its same-session rung or a path the operator types, otherwise to a PRD grilled from scratch. The draft branch already offered the form that reads such a file in place, `/create-prd <KEY> @<path>`; the refined branch, which leaves the same on-disk state after a decline, did not. It now says that on option 2 or 3 `handoff-to-main` does not run and §4.1's *Declined by the user* line is emitted, the run's emitter tail still running as it does after option 1, recommends `/create-prd <KEY> <merge-clause>` whichever option was taken, and **on a decline, or where §4.1's *Gate failed* line was emitted, offers `/create-prd <KEY> @<the absolute path of this idea.md>` beside it** — neither outcome committed anything — the route that does not wait for the files to land. The array's wording is `workflows-core` 1.7.1's to fix (its §4.3 now reads *"the next phase does not stop on this, but until this is on main it might not read your copy"*); `/idea` cites it, and the draft branch's own sentence about a bare `/create-prd <KEY>` — which said it *"grills the PRD from scratch, ignoring this file"*, where that command names the file and first reaches its same-session rung and its path prompt — now says what it does. `/create-ard` quotes the same array for an ARD handed off alone, and its copy moves with §4.3. The `/idea` documentation page's `/create-prd` line says the `@<path>` form is what reads an un-landed brief, and that a bare `/create-prd <KEY>` finds the brief once the handoff's pull request is merged. **Population: every run that presents the array — `/idea` on `status: refined` and `/create-ard` whose set is the ARD alone — and, for the `@<path>` route, every such `/idea` run whose handoff was declined or whose gate failed.**

### Fixed — four grill commands asked about the docs index after their work had started

`/create-prd`, `/update-prd`, `/create-ard` and `/specify` each showed the `docs grounding:` line in Phase 1 "in the form `workflows-core:docs-grounding` resolved", while the `resolve-docs-grounding` call that produces it ran later:

- `/create-prd` ran it in Phase 2.5 and `/update-prd` in Phase 2, so Phase 1 printed a result nothing had computed yet.
- `/create-ard` (Phase 3 step 5) and `/specify` (Phase 4) ran it only after their `code-scanner` dispatches.

`workflows-core:docs-grounding` step 3.5 asks its one-time question about building the docs index before any of the run's real work. In the last two commands that question therefore came after agents had run, and in all four the line could not be what it claimed to be.

Each command now runs `resolve-docs-grounding` in Phase 1, step 3.5's prompt included, and shows the line from what it returns. The later phase dispatches the docs grounder on that result and resolves nothing again. This is the same pattern `/idea` now follows, and the one `/brd-intake`, `/epics`, `/prd-ground` and `/release-notes` already did. `/update-prd`'s key step (Phase 0 step 1) also read "`--docs` each consume the token after them" of its one flag; it now reads "consumes the token after it".

**Population: every run of the four with documentation grounding in reach** — `$DOCS_PATH`, or its `/workspace/docs` default, a readable directory holding markdown, or a `--docs <path>` given. **Where `qmd` is installed and no collection covers that root**, `/create-ard` and `/specify` — the two whose ordering moved — now ask the index-build question before any agent is dispatched. The other runs see the line computed before it is shown, and nothing else changes.

### Fixed — seven commands cited a numbered section `workflows-core:docs-grounding` does not have

`/create-prd`, `/update-prd`, `/create-ard`, `/specify`, `/epics`, `/brd-intake` and `/prd-ground` each said `--docs <path>` is declared for every consumer by `workflows-core:docs-grounding` *"§1's *Flags first* rung"*, and `/create-ard`'s address step said the same section declares the flags' shape. That reference has no numbered sections: *Flags first* is step 1 of its *Procedure*. All eight sites now cite *Procedure* step 1 (*Flags first*); `docs-workflows` 1.2.2 corrects the same citation in `/release-notes` and in its repository-resolution reference. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader following the citation.**

### Fixed — four commands placed a folder by the kind it asserts, and none placed a folder with no prefix

`/create-ard` Phase 0 step 1 opened its altitude list with *"The resolved kind decides the altitude"*, while a BRD-route slice's resolved kind is `brd` — `workflows-core:addressing` §4 reads it off the slice's `brd-link.md` — and the list beneath it decided by the folder's prefix, with no entry for a folder that has none: one §5's legacy fallback resolves, or an unprefixed folder an `@<path>` names. `/specify` said *"The kind decides the altitude"* in its step 1, both commands' one-address stops said *"The kind of the folder the address resolves to is what sets the altitude"*, and `/epics` cited `/specify`'s derivation as *"the resolved folder's kind decides the altitude"*. `workflows-core` 1.7.1's `addressing.md` §4.1 now owns the rule, and `/create-ard` and `/specify` cite it: the prefix places a folder — its name beginning `<KIND>-<the resolved key>-`, so a legacy folder whose key begins with a kind token is not taken for a prefixed one — and a folder with none is placed by positive evidence, the container test first: a resolved `kind: epic` is Epic-level; a resolved `kind: prd`, or a `brd-link.md` naming a `parent:`, PRD-level. `/create-ard` and `/specify` now place such a folder by it — a legacy slice by its `brd-link.md`, onto the BRD route — both stops say the folder's prefix sets the altitude, and `/epics` says its gate decides by the artifact the folder holds, which is what it always did — it places no folder by §4.1, and takes from it only what a prefix is (below). `/prd-ground` refused an `EPIC-` folder by its prefix alone, so a legacy Epic folder fell through to the interrupted-intake stop naming `/brd-intake`; it is now refused as an Epic folder. The container test — `coverage-ledger-format.md` §5.1, and the copies in `/create-ard`, `/specify`, `/epics`, `/prd-proposal` and `/brd-proposal` — was scoped to a folder §5's fallback resolved, so an unprefixed root BRD an `@<path>` named, which §3 resolves without the fallback, reached none of them; each now takes it. §5 says its `BRD-` prefix is §4.1's, so a legacy folder keyed `BRD-12` is not a container by its name. The four refusals that test a prefix themselves — `/create-prd`'s, `/epics`', `/prd-proposal`'s and `/brd-proposal`'s, the last refusing a `PRD-` slice — said only *"the directory prefix"*, which a literal reading takes a legacy folder's name to carry wherever its key begins with the kind token tested, so an idea-route folder keyed `BRD-12`, named `BRD-12-checkout/`, was refused as a container; each now defines the prefix as §4.1 does. `/create-prd`'s two sentences, the `/create-ard` and `/specify` documentation pages', and §5's prose and §5.2's table, which said a container refusal is decided on the folder's kind, now say its prefix; and the `/create-ard`, `/specify` and `/prd-ground` documentation pages state the placement. `dev-workflows` 4.1.2's `/ready`, `/design` and `/implement` take the same rule. **Population: every `/create-ard` or `/specify` run on a folder with no kind prefix, every `/prd-ground` run on a legacy Epic folder, every `/create-ard`, `/specify`, `/epics`, `/prd-proposal` or `/brd-proposal` run handed an unprefixed root BRD by `@<path>`, every run of those four refusals handed a legacy folder whose key begins with the kind token it tests — `/create-prd` by that key, the other three by an `@<path>` naming it, since their text already took a folder §5's fallback resolved as carrying no prefix — and every reader of the steps.**

### Fixed — `/specify` and `/create-ard` tested a `focus_key` they never defined

Both commands' Phase 0 step 1 carries `<EPIC>` — the resolved `EPIC-` folder's key, or `null` for a `PRD-` folder — while their later phases test and set `focus_key`, a name neither command defined anywhere, as `dev-workflows`' `/design` did until its 4.1.2. Each now defines it where step 1 carries `<EPIC>`: one value under two names, which `/specify`'s Phase 2 Step A picker may set on a `PRD-` address. **No run's behaviour changed where the name was read as `<EPIC>`. Population: every reader of the two commands' later phases.**

### Fixed — `/specify` derived its candidate repositories from one folder's implementation record

Phase 3 step 1 took repositories from *"the resolved folder's `implementation.md`"* — one file, the folder the address named. `/dev-workflows:implement` writes a record into the folder of the unit it implemented — from `dev-workflows` 4.1.2 an Epic's folder whichever way the Epic was chosen, which `workflows-core` 1.7.1's `implementation-format.md` §1 now states — so a run addressed to a PRD that chose an Epic in Step A read the PRD folder's record and not that Epic's, and a broad PRD-level spec read no Epic's at all, although its Step B reads every Epic folder. Step 1 now reads the records in the folders Step B read: the focus Epic's where `focus_key` is set, the PRD folder's and every `EPIC-` folder's where it is null. The documentation page says the same. **Population: every `/specify` run whose Epics hold an implementation record — a re-specification after `/implement` has run — on 3.6.x as well.** The list is a candidate set the operator confirms, so what was lost was a starting candidate, never a gate.

### Fixed — `/epics` looked up a re-refine run's ARD by the Epic's key

Phase 2.5 ran `workflows-core:ard-resolution` with `prd = key`, the run's own key, which on a re-refine run — an `EPIC-` address, Phase 0 step 1b — is the Epic's. That reference resolves `prd` to the folder whose `ard.md` it reads at PRD level, so a re-refine run read the Epic folder's own `ard.md`, where one exists, as the PRD's, and never the PRD's. It now passes `<PRD-KEY>`, the PRD folder's key step 1b derives, and its note on `epic: null` no longer says Epics do not exist yet, which a re-refine run contradicts. **Population: every `/epics` re-refine run whose PRD or Epic folder holds an ARD.**

### Fixed — `/create-prd` said a `rejected` row cites a defect, and nothing else

`/create-prd`'s `CREATE_PRD_BRD_NOT_ELIGIBLE` table said a `rejected` gate-set row "cites the `[DEF#n]` justifying it". A slice's ledger can now also hold `rejected: <SLICE-KEY>/[CD#n]`: the customer's withdrawal, which `/brd-reconcile` records (`coverage-ledger-format.md` §3). The row now names both, as §5's table it mirrors does. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every `/create-prd` refusal on a slice whose rejected rows include a customer withdrawal.**

### Fixed — `/create-prd` said an unset `key` made a slice resolve as a BRD

`/create-prd`'s frontmatter step said that, with `key` unset, *"a folder whose only `kind:`+`key:` carrier is `brd-link.md` (`kind: brd`) resolves as a BRD rather than a PRD"* — implying that setting it makes the slice resolve as a PRD. `workflows-core` 1.7.1's `addressing` §4 fixes the order a folder's carrier is found in, and a slice's `brd-link.md` sorts before its `prd.md` whether or not `prd.md` carries a key, so a slice resolves as `brd` either way — the reading this plugin's container refusals already rest on. The sentence now names the defect that was real, the empty key `/document` and `/release-notes` build their commit scan from, and says the folder still resolves. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of `/create-prd` Phase 3's frontmatter step.**

### Fixed — `specification-format.md` said `specification.md`'s `kind:` and `key:` are how its folder asserts its identity

The frontmatter section said both fields *"are how the folder asserts its own identity"*, citing `workflows-core:addressing` §4. `workflows-core` 1.7.1's §4 now fixes which file carries a folder's identity — the first top-level file whose `kind:` is a folder kind (`brd`, `prd` or `epic`), else `brd/brd-inventory.md` — so `kind: specification`, which names the document and not the folder, is passed over, as it had to be for the resolution record to hold one of those three. The section now says `key:` records the folder's key so nothing parses one out of a directory name, `kind:` names the document, and §4 reads the folder's identity off the folder's carrier in its order — `brd-link.md` in a BRD-route slice, `idea.md` or `prd.md` in an idea-route PRD folder, `epic.md` in an Epic folder — never this file. **No run's behaviour changed. Population: every reader of the specification format's frontmatter section.**

### Changed — `brd-reader` runs on Opus

`brd-reader` was pinned to Sonnet on the grounds that *"extraction is mechanical"*. That holds for splitting requirements and is false for the half of its job that proposes defect candidates: a missed conflict, an ambiguity read as settled or a misread transcription yields no candidate, so Phase 4's human never sees it, and the failure is silent in exactly the direction that matters. It is now frontmatter-pinned to Opus, as `figure-reader` is, and both degrade to the best available model — recorded in the run's notes and final report — where Opus does not resolve. **A text-only BRD now costs more to intake than it did on 3.6.x.** **The Opus default for both agents is unmeasured**: it was taken on that asymmetric-cost argument, and no comparison of tiers on real customer documents and screenshots was run (spec §13).

### Fixed — `BRD_INTAKE_DANGLING_ANCHOR`'s remedy could not get past the stop

The stop told the operator to correct the anchors by hand and re-run, and in the same breath not to re-run `brd-reader` over the whole document *"which would renumber every row"*. The re-run it named does re-run `brd-reader` over the whole document, and nothing is renumbered — Phase 3's reconciliation keeps every existing id — but that reconciliation wrote the anchor `brd-reader` returned over the one the operator had just corrected, so a re-run met the same stop on the same row. A matched row now keeps its existing `source_anchor` where that one resolves and the returned one does not (Phase 3), and the stop names the re-run and says what it keeps. **Population: every intake that met this stop on 3.6.x and followed its remedy.**

### Changed — a `§` anchor that names no heading now stops the intake

`brd-format.md` §2.2 branch 1 resolved a leading section reference "directly", matching the number against nothing, so an anchor reading `§ 99` passed relation 1 in a file whose sections stop at 12. It now resolves only to the one heading carrying that number — opening its text, after an optional `§`, followed by a space, by a full stop and a space, or by nothing — so `§ 7` finds `§ 7 Retention`, `7. Retention` or `7 Retention` and never `7.1 Scope`. A number no heading carries, or more than one does, falls to branch 4, and `/brd-intake` stops with `BRD_INTAKE_DANGLING_ANCHOR`, whose remedy — correct the anchor by hand and re-run — keeps every id. **Population: every intake whose inventory holds a `§` anchor naming no heading of its file, or naming a number several headings carry — rows that passed on 3.6.x and now stop the run.**

### Fixed — `/brd-package` recommended the repository route for a bundle it had not pushed, and settled the route from an answer it had not yet taken

Phase 7 asked *"They pull the specs repository (Recommended)"* wherever the handoff's consent choice was accepted — a choice the *Handoff* phase presents two phases later — and took the archive route only on a decline. An accepted handoff that ends *Push failed* or *No remote* leaves the bundle on a commit only the packaging machine holds, and one that ends *Gate failed* committed nothing, so the recommended route sent the customer to pull a bundle that is not there; and no reading of the phases in order gave Phase 7 the answer it keyed on. Keying it on the `Phase handoff:` line instead, as an earlier draft of this fix did, cannot work either: the delivery note Phase 7 writes is one of the files that handoff commits, so the note cannot wait on its outcome. And even a pushed bundle is on a `brd/<KEY>-<slug>` branch — suffixed `-2` where that name is taken — until its pull request merges, so a customer pulling the repository's default branch does not find it there; 3.6.x recommended that route and said nothing of the condition, and this branch's own first fix then called the note *"true as written"* on any push, which is corrected here. **Population: every `/brd-package` run — on 3.6.x as well.** Phase 7 now takes the handoff's consent itself, testing `workflows-core:phase-handoff` §2.1's gate conditions, running its push-target probe and presenting its §4.3 choice array; the *Handoff* phase executes on that answer, handing `handoff-to-main` the probe's `remote` value, and does not ask again. Declining — the array's second or third option — declines the handoff and nothing else: the run continues on the archive route, and the *Handoff* phase, which described its first choice alone, now says what those two do — `handoff-to-main` does not run, and the final report carries `workflows-core:phase-handoff` §4.1's *Declined by the user* line with the **gated — stopping** clause the array's parenthetical promised. Phase 7 asks which route, recommending the repository one, only where the gate conditions hold, the probe found an `origin` remote and the operator consented; otherwise it takes the archive route without asking, and says why. The note names no branch. The note and its delivery-route item are now printed at *Next steps*, in that order and **before** the offer that asks whether to send it, since an operator who has not read the note cannot answer it: on the repository route that item names the branch and pull request the handoff pushed and says the note is true once that pull request merges — or, where the handoff pushed nothing, what must happen first, and, for every path the handoff declared and staged nothing for (§4.1's *Declaration unaccounted for* clause, an ignored file among its causes), that the note is not true for that file on any outcome, no merge putting on the branch what nothing staged. That item is the one place the condition is printed; the final report names the note and the item rather than repeating either, and the next-step offer's *Send it* waits on the same condition. The note is never rewritten. `bundle-packaging.md` §4 and §5 and the command's documentation page say the same.

### Fixed — `/brd-reconcile`'s handoff left the corrections it applied behind

`/brd-reconcile` Phase 6 corrects a live working document in place wherever a returned review's section 12 asks and no other rule owns the field — `slices.md`, a seed file, the inventory's or `brd-link.md`'s prose, a `depends-on:` added to `brd-link.md` — but its handoff's `deliverable_paths` named none of those files, and `workflows-core:phase-handoff` §2.3 stages only what is declared. Each correction stayed in the working tree while the pull request went out without it. The handoff now declares every artifact Phase 6 changed — the parent's `brd/brd-figures.md` among them, where a slice's run applies a transcription correction — and names each file beneath `customer-sent-<YYYYMMDD>/` literally where it named the directory, which §2.3 does not stage either. **Population: every `/brd-reconcile` run on 3.6.x that applied a section-12 row to a file the handoff did not otherwise name, and every `--sent` run whose first handoff was declined.** A correction left behind that way is still in the operator's working tree, uncommitted, and the specs-repo preflight of the next run in that repository reports it as a dirty path.

### Fixed — `/brd-reconcile`'s stale-reference sweep could rewrite the customer's own files

Phase 11 reads every markdown file under the parent's folder for prose still asserting a position the customer's answer moved, and may mark a hit `updated` and correct the sentence. Its structured-record table kept that off the ledger, the inventory, the register and the code-defect log, but not off `brd/source/` — the customer's own document, which Phase 6's class 3 says is never touched at all and `brd-format.md` §1 makes immutable. So a sentence of the customer's own that stated a position their answer later moved was one the sweep could rewrite, falsifying the record of what they sent. A hit anywhere under `brd/source/` or `brd/source-external/` is now `needs-a-human`, and the file is never edited; the figures file takes the same outcome. **Population: every `/brd-reconcile` run whose second search matches a sentence in the customer's own captured files — on 3.6.x the source document and any markdown file it linked from its own directory, which that version copied into `brd/source/` as well; this release adds every file captured from outside that directory.**

### Changed — `/brd-intake` asks about each uncovered section and image on its own, re-reads at most once, and remembers the answers

Phase 3's coverage check put every top-level section and image holding no row into one question — *Re-read* or *They hold no obligation* — and its recommendation stayed on *Re-read* for a prose section the reader keeps treating as context, so a re-read that changed nothing brought back the same question with the same recommendation. The only ways out were an untrue *they hold no obligation* for the whole set, or *Cancel*: nothing let the operator record that a section binds, and one answer stood for every section at once — verification run B5 gave `1. Background`, arguably binding, and `6. References`, pointers only, one shared record. And the answer was saved nowhere but the final report, so every re-run over an unchanged document asked it again. **Re-reading is now an action on the whole set, and accounting is per item.** One question for the set offers *Re-read* — recommended unless every item it names is one a re-read cannot change — *Account for each section and image*, and *Cancel*; it is put once per run, since a re-read is allowed once. Then each item still unaccounted for gets a question of its own, at most four to a call: *They hold no obligation* (recommended), *Quote the sentence that binds*, or *Cancel* — an image that was not read has nothing to quote and is offered the first and the last. A quote is asked for with the text it is checked against shown — the section's own text, or the image's transcription — one obligation to a quote and as many quotes as the item binds, the item's question coming back after each with *Finished* recommended. A span is checked with whitespace collapsed and nothing else normalised, and becomes a row whose text is the source's own, never the operator's typing, anchored at the section — at the lines it covers, where the section's heading path names no single heading — or on the image. A span the source does not hold is never written through, and never a dead end: the item's question is put again in the form it stands at, with the failed span shown, so that form's own way out stays in reach beside *Cancel* — *They hold no obligation* while the item holds no row, *Finished* once it holds one. A typed answer to any of these questions, the set question included, is acted on as the option it expresses or asked again. A re-read that finds no requirement replaces nothing — the first read's result stands, and the re-read is spent. Every account — a section's and an image's alike — is recorded in a new `accounts:` map in the inventory's frontmatter (`brd-format.md` §2), its keys and values double-quoted with `"` and `\` escaped, written in the same act as `captured:`. A re-run carries an account, rather than asking again, only where Phase 2 recorded the item's file — or the image itself — unchanged; every write of the map, the one relation 1's stop makes included, drops the account of every item in a file recorded replaced, so an answer given about a file's earlier bytes never stands beside a record of its new ones. An image's *Rows* line shows its account and is never read back as one. The final report names each account with its words, and says which were asked and which were carried. **Population: every intake whose inventory leaves a top-level section or an image without a row — most real BRDs, nine of fifteen sections on the package the check was measured on — and every re-run over one. Present before this branch**: 3.6.x's question, over sections alone, recommended *Re-read* unconditionally, gave the set one answer, and saved it nowhere.

### Fixed — a coverage re-read left the first read's candidates standing, and on a first intake numbered its new row last

A re-read re-dispatches `brd-reader` over the whole set, and Phase 3 said only that its rows are reconciled. Nothing said whether Phase 4 and Phase 5 then took the first read's defect candidates, `illustrates` lists and notes, or the re-read's. And the re-read was reconciled against the rows the first read had already written, so on a first intake a row only the re-read found was minted after every other id — verification run P2's `Background` became `[BR#15]` — against `brd-format.md` §2's reading-order numbering, while over a replaced file a row the re-read worded differently beside a first-read row under a shared anchor matched nothing and was minted a second time. **Phase 3 now holds a read's result and writes the inventory once**, after the coverage step settles, or just before relation 1's stop, so that stop's hand-correction remedy has a file to correct. A re-read's rows, candidates, `figures` entries and notes replace the first read's wholesale — save a re-read that finds no requirement, which replaces nothing — and it is reconciled exactly as the first read was, against the inventory on file: nothing is copied between the two reads, so every file stands for it as Phase 2 recorded it, and the text rule and the same-or-new question apply to it unchanged. On a first intake the rows are numbered once, after the last read and after any row a quoted sentence adds, in reading order; on a re-run every id on file is kept and each new row takes the next id after the highest. `brd-format.md` §2's numbering paragraph now states both cases. **Population: every intake whose operator answers the coverage question *Re-read*. Present before this branch** for the numbering and the unstated candidates — 3.6.x reconciled a re-read against the rows it had just written; the second mint over a replaced file arrived with this release's text rule.

### Fixed — six smaller defects in `/brd-intake`'s figures, anchors and notes

- **An earlier run's `[BR#n]`s reached `brd-reader` through the figures file.** Phase 3 handed the agent `brd/brd-figures.md` itself, whose re-used sections carry an earlier run's *Rows* line, and an id copied from one came back as the agent's own numbering, which Phase 3 then mapped onto a different row. It now hands over a copy with every *Rows* line removed — a first intake's too — made in a `command mktemp -d` directory and removed after the phase's last read, and `brd-reader` is told the line is no input. **Population: every re-run that re-uses a figures section whose *Rows* line names a row. New in this release.**
- **"The same element" was undefined for an image anchor.** Reconciliation's first pass matched anchors resolving to "the same section or element", and `brd-format.md` §2.2 said only whether an image anchor resolves. §2.2 now fixes which element one names: a *Text* region, or a cell of a table's rows, an annotation, or a *Flow* edge; `annotation <n>` names the n-th annotation; a quote names the one element holding it, and a quote found in more than one element names none. Two anchors on one image quoting the identical string match all the same. That way a row drawn from an image — the plugin's own words, reworded between reads — never gets a second id over an unchanged image, and its image-only `ambiguity` never gets a second entry. For the same-or-new question, a quote that names no single element counts as naming every element holding it. `brd-reader` quotes a span its own element alone holds, or writes `annotation <n>`, and the pass and the same-or-new question test by those rules. **Population: every re-run over a BRD with a row drawn from an image. New in this release.**
- **A correction made in a note shipped uncorrected.** Phase 5 filled each *Rows* line from the returned `illustrates` while the agent's own notes said a list was wrong, and `brd-format.md` §1.2 reserves *Rows* to `/brd-intake`, so the error could ship only as an accepted risk — verification run P2's `[SR#7]`. `brd-reader`'s return contract now says a note never amends a returned field and a correction is made in the field, and `/brd-intake` says it writes *Rows* from `illustrates` alone and prints the notes without parsing them. **Population: every run whose reader corrects a field in a note. New in this release.**
- **The notes were printed "before the first candidate", which never comes on a run with nothing to walk.** They are now printed once at Phase 4's start, whether or not a candidate is left — the standing read's, the last read whose result was not discarded — and in the final report; after an `EMPTY` read, which skips Phase 4, in the final report alone. **Population: every run left with no candidate to walk — a re-run whose every candidate matches an entry on file, and any run, a first intake included, whose read proposed no candidate at all. New in this release** — 3.6.x reported the notes in the final report only.
- **Phase 4's *Image* line did not say what of the transcription to show.** It read *"transcription below"*. The question now shows the section's *Depicts* sentence and, on an *Element* line, the element the row's anchor names — the *Text* region or cell, the annotation's *Says* and what it points at, or the *Flow* edge — and every element holding the quote where it names none, and nothing else of the transcription. **Population: every candidate on a row drawn from an image. New in this release.**
- **A new figures section's empty *Rows* line had no written form.** `brd-format.md` §1.2 and Phase 2.5 now write it `- **Rows:**`, with nothing after it, which is what a run that stops before Phase 5 leaves. **Population: every figures file read after such a run. New in this release.**

### Fixed — `/idea`'s question past the older bounds had no text, printed no counts, and counted "reached" three ways

Phase 1.5 printed its counts line only where the walk stayed inside the older bounds, so the ask branch left the final report's walk line with no source; it named the files past a bound in prose and gave the question no text; and its headline total counted *other* files, `Read all <n>`'s `<n>` did not, while *Read all* took them. The ask branch now prints the counts line too, puts a fixed question before the array — the bounds the walk crossed, then one line per file past them with each bound it is past, then the line saying what *Read all* takes, and, where the walk reached nothing below depth 1, the line saying the second option takes that same set — and defines `<n>` as the headline total less the source, *other* files included: exactly the set *Read all* takes beside the source, as the branch that asks nothing takes it, an *other* file still never opened. *Only what the source links directly* takes every file the source links itself, of any kind. **Population: every `/idea` run whose walk reaches past a bound — `<n>` read differently only where it also reached an *other* file. New in this release**, whose Phase 1.5 is new.

### Fixed — `/idea` restated its collision rule without the fourth rule, and taught two ways to cite an image

Phase 4.5 step 2 said it restated none of `idea-format.md`'s vendoring rules and then restated *The collision rule* in part: identical content reused "at the destination", where rule 1 compares against every file in the destination directory, and no rule 4, which refreshes the copy an earlier run made of an edited source rather than minting a `_NN` twin. A run following the step as written mints an orphan beside a stale copy `idea.md` still points at. Step 2 now cites the rule — all four rules, executed from `idea-format.md` — and the final report names a refreshed name beside a substituted one, as the phase's own outcome line already did. *The collision rule* itself put all four of its rules behind "where that name is already taken". Read literally, rule 1's comparison against the whole destination never ran for a file whose basename was free, so a renamed file whose bytes were already vendored was copied beside them as a twin. Rule 1 now applies to every file and rules 2–4 to a name already taken, as `/brd-intake` Phase 2 already applied it. `idea-format.md` Section 5 said a linked image is "cited by path", two paragraphs above the rule that it is cited by its written `target` or its resolved `path`; it now says so where it opens. **Population: every `/idea` re-run over a linked page or image whose bytes changed since its copy was made, every run vendoring a file whose bytes already sit in the destination under another name, and every brief citing an image. Present before this branch** — 3.6.x carried all three: at `4387959f` the collision rule opens "where that name is already taken:".

### Fixed — a slice's `source:` had two path bases, and its `claims:` list had no spelling

`brd-format.md` §2.1 wrote a slice inventory's `source:` relative to the parent's folder — `brd/source/<basename>` — while `/brd-split` Phase 3 step 4 said to name the document the parent's `document:` records, which is relative to `brd/`. §2.1 now owns one base, the parent's folder: `brd/` put before the `document:` value, written as a double-quoted YAML string, the base a 3.6.x slice already carries and the one `/create-prd`'s documentation page reads, and `/brd-split` cites it. None of `/brd-package`, `bundle-packaging.md` or `/prd-ground` reads a slice's `source:` — `/brd-package` names the document off the parent's `document:`, and the `source:` the bundle drops is the figures file's own field — so no reader changed. And nothing fixed how `brd-link.md`'s `claims:` list is spelled, though YAML reads an unquoted `[BR#1]` as a flow sequence holding `BR#1`, which matches no ledger row's `[BR#1]`. §2.1 now shows the file and fixes the list: each entry a double-quoted bracketed id, one to a line, `claims: []` when empty; a list written before in another spelling is read id by id and rewritten this way when `/brd-split` next writes it, and `/brd-split` cites the spelling and writes the whole list again in it wherever it adds to or withdraws from the list, so an older list is rewritten the first time the command touches it. **Population: every slice `/brd-split` writes. The `source:` disagreement is new in this release** — 3.6.x's `/brd-split` cited §2.1's header; **the unfixed `claims:` spelling was present before this branch.**

### Fixed — `/brd-intake`'s outside-files count, its re-read recommendation and the row a quote added — eight defects two live runs found

A fresh intake and a re-run over the same unchanged source were executed against the command's own text, and the redesign held: the coverage set question was put once, each per-item form carried three options, a wrong quote wrote nothing and re-put the item's question with the failed span beside it, the right one landed as `[BR#1]` in reading order, `accounts:` carried over the unchanged file, and the re-run kept all 15 ids with no text changed. Eight things in the text did not. **Phase 1's outside-files question printed the wrong number**: `Capture all <n>` counted every file the walk reached — 5 in the run — while the answer decides only the files outside the document's folder — 1 — so the number an operator needs was never printed. The option now names what it captures and `<n>` counts exactly that, with the run's other counts said to be the taken set's and the copied set's. **A row the coverage step added from a quoted span was reported *not re-extracted by this read* on every later run**, in the same words as a row the reader genuinely stopped returning — the two were indistinguishable in the final report, and the designed steady state read as a signal. The inventory now carries a `quoted:` list of those rows (`brd-format.md` §2) and each is reported in its own words, *added from a quoted span; no read has returned it*, leaving the other phrase to mean what it says. **`(Recommended)` on the set question turned on whether a section's content was "apart from its heading and the words introducing its links" only links** — a judgement about what words are for, which decided a live recommendation; the test is now applied to the section's own text, piece by piece, and an unclear one defaults to re-readable, since a wasted dispatch is cheaper than an account standing where a requirement would have been. **The re-run confirmation carried no `(Recommended)` marker and did not say the omission was deliberate**, unlike the zero-row list in the same command; it now says so and why. **A matched row whose two anchors both resolved and differed had no rule** — the case `brd-format.md` §2.2 makes reachable by matching `annotation 2` against a quote of its *Says* — and now goes to the same-or-new question, because a moved row and a re-anchored one look alike from there. **The per-item questions never said how their plain-text quote prompts interleave** when two items in one call both answer *Quote…*; they run one item at a time, in the call's order. **An image anchor quoting the empty string resolved against every image and named no element of any of them** — §1.2 writes an unlabelled mark's *Says* as `""`, and §2.2 resolved an anchor by verbatim appearance, so `<path> › ""` passed relation 1, the traceability stop, on a row pointing at nothing; §2.2 now refuses an empty quote outright, says that a form the format writes where content is absent is not content to quote, and fixes `annotation <n>` as what anchors a row drawn from an unlabelled mark, which `brd-reader`'s contract now writes. And **`brd-format.md` §1.2 told the figures file's `source:` to take the document's name from `brd/brd-link-log.md`**, which §1.1 forbids wherever the inventory carries `document:` — on a re-run over a document revised under a new filename the two disagreed; §1.2 now takes the name from the document the writing run itself copied into `brd/source/`, which Phase 2 holds, so the two no longer state two rules for one field. **One change rides with that eighth fix rather than being a ninth defect**: §1.2 now states a limit of its own notation — its *Flow* list holds edges and no grouping of them, so a diagram drawing one edge in two swimlanes is transcribed as two identical list items no anchor can tell apart — and `figure-reader` now names such a grouping, where it carries meaning, within the one sentence its `depicts` already is, that being the one field which describes the drawing rather than transcribing it. A limit stated without saying where the lost information goes leaves the only party still holding the diagram with nothing to do; this is the one change in the pair that alters what ships inside `brd/brd-figures.md`.

**Population: every `/brd-intake` run — the count on any run reaching a file outside the folder, the recommendation on any run whose coverage check names a section, the reported state on every re-run over a BRD whose coverage step ever took a quote, and the `source:` disagreement on any re-run over a document sent under a new filename; the `depicts` change lands on every diagram whose grouping carries meaning, of which the verification vault holds none — checked against run A6's transcription, whose one diagram is a left-to-right chain of four boxed steps with no grouping and no repeated edge. **The empty-quote refusal has no live population and is stated as a rule the format admitted rather than a run that hit it**: the verification vault holds the ingredient — `report.png` carries an unlabelled mark — but neither `brd-reader` nor an operator wrote such an anchor in either run, and no producer would; what was wrong was that the format resolved one. New in this release, all eight**: checked against `4387959f`, where `/brd-intake` runs no read-only link walk, its capture walking links transitively in Phase 2 and inside the document's own directory only — its Phase 1 asks nothing about files outside the document's folder, so there was no `<n>` to be wrong — its coverage step is one question over the whole set with no quote option, no `accounts:` map and no row it could add, and neither `brd/brd-figures.md` nor the §1.2 that formats it exists.

### Fixed — `/idea`'s walk printed three numbers over three different sets, and a declined handoff dropped its own outcome line

A live run over a 23-file vault — 6 pages and 4 images at depth 1, reaching depth 4 — put the phase's numbers beside each other for the first time. The counts line's headline counts the walk's visited set **with** the source; its by-depth tally counts the markdown **without** it, the source having no depth to be counted at; and the twelve-page bound orders the markdown **with** it. All three were individually exact and no two said which they were, so the run printed `depth 1: 6, depth 2: 6, depth 3: 2` — fourteen pages — above a bound of twelve that three files were past. Each number now names its set, and the bound says outright that it runs one ahead of the tally beside it. **`Read all <n>`'s `<n>` sits under the list of files past a bound and counts something else** — every file the walk reached beside the source, 22 against a five-line list — which the option now says rather than leaving an operator to count five things and answer about twenty-two. **The counts line had no stated form for a zero**: "anything unresolved … or `other`" reads as *whatever there was of those*, its one example prints all four clauses, and an omitted clause is indistinguishable from a forgotten one — every clause now prints on every run, `0` included. **The past-a-bound template offered one alternation slot and no separator** where its own prose asks for "every bound it is past", and three files in that run were past two; they are joined with `, ` in the template's left-to-right order. **`idea-format.md`'s rewrite map is keyed on `(target, from)`** while every link being rewritten sits in `idea.md`, which is no digest entry's `from`, so a literal pair-match would have rewritten nothing: matching is on the written target alone, the `from` half being what makes two entries two keys for rule 3's ambiguity case. And **the Final report asked for the `Phase handoff:` line "when the handoff ran"**, which on a decline — where `handoff-to-main` does not run — drops `workflows-core:phase-handoff` §4.1's *Declined by the user* line, the one line telling the operator their files are written and not on the default branch; it is now asked for by option: `handoff-to-main`'s line on the first, the *Declined by the user* line on either other, and **none of them on a `status: draft` run**, where Phase 5 never offers one at all — so there is no entry point run and no offer declined for §4.1 to have a line about, and asking for one unconditionally would have made a draft run assert a decision its operator was never given.

**Population: every `/idea` run whose walk reaches past a bound** — the three counts and the option label on all of them, the separator on any whose over-bound set holds a file past two bounds, and the zero form on any run at all. **The rewrite map's keying is every run that vendors a linked source**, and **the handoff line is every run whose operator declines**. **Status against `4387959f` is split, and was checked rather than assumed — two of these are older than this branch.** The three counts, the option label, the zero form and the separator are **new in this release**: that commit's `idea.md` has no `## Phase 1.5` at all (its headings run Phase 1 → Phase 2), so there is no walk, no counts line and no bound question to have been wrong. The **rewrite map's keying is present before this branch** — `git show 4387959f:plugins/product-workflows/references/idea-format.md` carries the identical key row at its line 317, *"the `target` as written, together with the `from` it was written in"*, above the same *"Match each link in `idea.md` on its target string"*, and that release already vendors sources in its own Phase 4.5. The **`Phase handoff:` condition is present before this branch** too: the same *"outcome line when the handoff ran"* stands at that commit's `idea.md:534`.

### Fixed — six more `/brd-intake` defects a second live pair found, a declaration that dropped five files on a re-run among them

A fresh intake and a re-run over the same unchanged source were executed against the command's own text, after the fixes the first pair bought. Everything those fixes were for held: the set question was put once, both per-item arrays went out in one call, a whitespace-only quote and a real sentence from the *wrong* section were each refused as a span that does not occur, the correct quote landed as `[BR#1]` on the `quoted:` list, `accounts:` carried an unchanged file's answer, and the re-run kept thirteen ids and reported the quoted row in its own words. Six other things did not. **Phase 7's `deliverable_paths` dropped the customer's document and four captured files on the re-run**: its generative clause declared "every file this run wrote … and every file Phase 2 re-used under collision rule 1", while the gloss beneath it declared every file captured into `brd/source/` or `brd/source-external/` — and a re-run over unchanged bytes *writes* none of them, collision rule 1 being a `source-external/` rule. The phase names the consequence itself: an undeclared file under those directories is staged by nothing and stays untracked for ever. One sentence now governs — every file under the BRD folder this run **captured or wrote**, captured covering a copy this run made, one collision rule 1 re-used, and one a re-run left untouched. **`brd/brd-figures.md`'s `source:` could not be followed on a first intake**: it deferred to the rule preferring the inventory's `document:`, which Phase 3 writes *after* Phase 2.5 writes the figures file, so the preferred source did not exist and the forbidden one was all there was. It is now taken from the document the writing run itself copied into `brd/source/`, which Phase 2 holds — never off the ladder §1.1 gives a *later* reader, which on a re-run over a document resent under a new filename would name the previous basename while the same run's Phase 3 writes the new one into `document:` minutes later; §1.1's list of readers that take the name from the inventory names the writer of a slice inventory's `source:` and `/brd-package`, and no longer the figures writer. **The link log's second table said it carries a row for every `[[wikilink]]`**, while its `Copy` column needs a copy to name and an `ambiguous` wikilink is captured by nothing: that table now holds only links whose target the capture took, and an uncaptured one keeps its row in the first table with its reason. **Phase 1's outside-files count asserted a number it cannot make** — "every file the second answer drops save one" — where that answer also drops any inside file reachable only through an outside one, of which there may be none, one or many; the fixture's set was empty. **The reconciliation now says when one of its two report wordings has no members**, since a class printed nowhere looks exactly like a class the run never looked at — which is the whole reason a quoted row and a genuinely dropped row are worded apart. And **a first intake now says what a quoted row costs the numbering, after the item's *Finished* answer**: ids are fixed once, in reading order, so a span quoted from an early passage takes an id one of the read's own rows would have had and moves every later one down — and since a first intake prints no `[BR#n]` before Phase 4, the operator is told not that a numbering they read has changed but that the ids they will meet are not the order they quoted in. **One change rides with these rather than being a seventh defect**: Phase 1's question about a linked file that is neither markdown nor an image recommends the option that ends the run, which two runners flagged independently — a scripted or careless operator taking the recommendation gets no BRD folder at all. The marker stays, stopping to convert being the safer default, but the reason now sits where the choice is made: such a file is copied and never read, so an obligation inside it would enter the folder unseen, and the recommended option ends the run having written nothing. The option itself says so — *this run ends here, having written nothing* — where it used to say *nothing has been written*, which reads as reassurance rather than as a cost.

**Population: the declaration on every re-run over a BRD whose files are unchanged** — which is the ordinary re-run, and the one defect here that loses something rather than confusing someone; the figures file's `source:` on every first intake; the second table on every capture holding an unresolvable wikilink; the count on every run reaching a file outside the document's folder; the empty-class line on every re-run whose unmatched rows are all of one kind; and the numbering notice on every first intake whose operator quotes a passage before the last requirement the read returned. **New in this release, all six** — checked at `4387959f`, whose `/brd-intake` has no `brd/brd-figures.md` and no `brd/source-external/` at all (neither string appears in that commit's command or its `brd-format.md`), no *Captured links that do not resolve as written* table, no outside-files question — an outside file is excluded there by rule rather than offered, so there was no count to be wrong — no `captured:` map, and no *not re-extracted by this read* wording for a class to be missing from. **The rider's subject is new there too**: neither the *other*-file question nor `BRD_INTAKE_UNREAD_ATTACHMENTS` exists at that commit — both grep to 0 in its `brd-intake.md`, which asks nothing about a linked file that is neither markdown nor an image.

### Fixed — every package stopped on — or shipped — the customer-review schema's own example identifier

`/brd-package` inlines `customer-review-schema.md` from its section 2 onward into the prompt's part 10, and that body's rule on citing identifiers gave its example as *"bracketed, like `[BR#4]` or `[CG#12]`"*. The prompt is itself a bundle document, so the citation-resolution check (`bundle-packaging.md` §6.2, relation 1) resolved both against the package's own records: each example stopped the package at *Assemble the bundle* rule 8 with `BRD_PACKAGE_DEAD_CITATION` wherever its id was absent from that package's corpus — the `[CG#12]` in every package holding fewer than twelve code-grounding findings, the `[BR#4]` in every slice whose inventory, which holds only the rows the slice claims in its parent's numbering, does not claim that row — on text no operator edit can repair because it is rendered from the reference on every run; and wherever the id was present it resolved, silently, to an unrelated record the customer then reads the rule as being about. A verification run of the package case end to end stopped exactly there. **Population: every `/brd-package` run — each one either stops or ships the rule naming an unrelated record — on 3.3.0, which added the example, through 3.6.1.** The example is now the placeholder form, `[BR#n]` or `[CG#n]` with `n` standing for the number. The schema's section 1 says an identifier with a number in it never appears in the file, even as an example, and why; `bundle-packaging.md` §6.2 says a placeholder names a class and no record, so relation 1 needs no exemption for the text the run copies in verbatim; and `render-schema` now verifies that the extracted body holds no identifier with a number in it, stopping with the new `BRD_PACKAGE_SCHEMA_EXAMPLE_ID` — a reference-integrity gap, which the capture-at-block invariant now names beside `BRD_PACKAGE_SCHEMA_BOUNDARY` and `BRD_PACKAGE_PROMPT_LEAK` — before an example number can reach a customer. `/brd-package` now binds every fixed sentence it renders — into the prompt, the delivery note, the manifest and the notes the de-Obsidianising pass leaves — to the same placeholder form, where §6.2 had asserted it with nothing in the command saying so. A sweep of every passage the prompt, the delivery note and the bundle render verbatim from a reference or template found no other.

### Fixed — a hit in the customer's own words asked the operator nothing

The plugin-free scan and the citation-resolution check report, rather than stop on, a hit inside verbatim customer content or a customer-derived locator, and `/brd-package` then said to *"let the operator decide whether to ship"* — with no question, no options and no stop, so a run either shipped without asking or improvised a question of its own. **Population: every `/brd-package` run whose scans report such a hit — on 3.6.x as well, where that treatment covered the customer's source document alone.** `bundle-packaging.md` §6.3 now owns the ruling: each pass that reports such a hit prints its grouped report and asks once — *Ship it* (recommended), or *Hold the package*, which stops with the new `BRD_PACKAGE_CUSTOMER_CONTENT_HELD` and hands nothing off. A hit an earlier ruling in the run already covered — keyed by its token and the file it sits in, the bundle's copy of the prompt counting as the prompt for those of its hits whose token that ruling covered, and one carrying a token it did not put like any other — is not asked about again, and the final report opens its grouped list with a `Customer content:` outcome line, or reads `Customer content: none`. The command's own statements that such a hit "reports rather than stops" now say that a *Hold* stops the run. `/brd-package` cites it from the prompt's, the note's and the bundle's scans, and the documentation page says the same.

### Fixed — two statements of what the prompt inlines from the review schema disagreed

`bundle-packaging.md` §1 said the review schema is *"inlined in full"*, while `/brd-package`'s `render-schema` entry point — which is what runs — takes everything from the schema's section 2 onward and says *"Never the whole file"*. **Population: a reader of `bundle-packaging.md`; no run behaved differently — on 3.6.x as well.** §1 now says the schema is inlined and never the whole file, and cites the boundary the schema declares and the entry point that takes it rather than stating what is taken.

### Fixed — the image path on a requirement-defect question had no line of its own

`/brd-interview` writes a held `[C]` question's defect on a line labelled `- **Requirement defect:** [DEF#n]` — the one line `/brd-reconcile` copies a `[CD#n]`'s `settles` from and every slice reads to know the defect is asked — and added *"where the defect sits on a row drawn from an image, that image's path relative to `brd/`"* without saying where it goes, so it could land on the line a reader copies an id from. **Population: every requirement-defect question about a row drawn from an image — new in 3.7.0, which added both the question source and image-drawn rows.** The defect line now carries the `[DEF#n]` and nothing else, and the image path goes on the next line, labelled exactly `- **Defect image:**`, including where *One question per row* appends a defect to an entry already on file. `/brd-package`'s part 7 now says what it renders from each held entry, that line among it, so the customer can find the picture through the manifest; `/brd-reconcile` still reads the id from the defect line alone. The documentation page says the same.

### Fixed — whether a bundled image takes the key prefix was unstated

*Assemble the bundle* rule 1 gave every bundle **document** a filename beginning `<BRD-KEY>-` and said nothing of the images the bundle also carries — the design frames the grounding cites and, from 3.7.0, the customer's captured images. **Population: every bundle carrying an image — on 3.6.x as well, for the frames.** Rule 1 now says every file the bundle carries takes the prefix, images included, and that an embedded image in a rendered document points at that name. Where two of the package's own images share a basename — frames in different frame sets, or a captured image under `brd/source/` and one under `brd/source-external/` — each takes the shortest run of its own trailing folder names that tells it apart, joined by `-`; the result is then tested against every other name the bundle carries — not merely against the images that shared a basename, since `design/login/01.png` disambiguated to `<BRD-KEY>-login-01.png` equals the plain name of an image whose own basename is `login-01.png` — and where two names still coincide the one whose path sorts later, byte-wise, takes `-2` before its extension, the test repeating until every name is unique. So the name is decided by the paths rather than the copy order. The manifest maps each captured file, image or markdown, to its name, and every other image whose name took a folder; `bundle-packaging.md` §1.1 says the same. `bundle-packaging.md` §7.1, which said §6's relation 3 already reaches a design finding's citation of a frame, now says nothing checks one — that relation reads a bare `<name>.md` token only — a false claim since 3.5.0.

### Fixed — the propagation sweep's citation pass never read the field holding the ids it sweeps for

`/brd-reconcile`'s changed-id set carries every `[DEF#n]` that gained a resolution, and `settles` is the one **structured** field of a decision record that holds a `[DEF#n]` (`decision-register-format.md` §1) — but the citation pass read `statement`, `argumentation`, `evidence` and `conditional_on` only, so a dependent BRD's `[CD#n]` that settles a defect this run resolved was never reached, and the position resting on it was never put to a disposition. **Population: every reconciliation whose dependent registers cite a changed id in one of the omitted fields — on 3.6.x as well, the list being as old as the pass.** Which fields the pass reads is now **derived from §1** rather than listed in the command, and the derivation reads **every** field §1 defines, skipping exactly two kinds: a field whose value §1 fixes to an identifier class — on every record kind it appears on, §7's assumption rows included — where the changed set carries no id of that class, and a field that names the record itself. **Both skips fall out of the rule rather than being written down beside it**, which is the property that matters for a pass whose failure mode is a silent miss: `defects` is skipped because it holds `[CDF#n]` ids on every record kind and the set carries no `[CDF#n]`, so the day it does the pass reads `defects` with nothing in the command changing; `id` is skipped because it names the record rather than citing another. The changed-id set's own definition now names every later reader — the citation pass's item matching, this field rule's class test, the stale cross-reference sweep's text search one phase on, and the reconciliation record — so none of them is invisible from where the set is settled, and it keeps no count of them, a census being what an editor checks against and the half of that paragraph that first went wrong. Everything else is read, so a field added to §1 is read on the day it is added rather than waiting for a reader to notice it — `settles` qualifies on the class test, and an `[AS#n]`'s `evidence`, which §7 makes the why-no-evidence **statement**, qualifies because §1 fixes it to no class there. **`chosen` is read on both record kinds** — on a `[CD#n]` it can be the customer's answer quoted verbatim where they answered outside the options put, which is exactly where a changed id is named in words nobody may edit, and on a `[VD#n]` it is the delivery team's own text — and a hit there is disposed like any other while no disposition corrects the text; the stale cross-reference sweep's `decisions.md` row, which already sends every field but `argumentation` to `needs-a-human`, is what refuses a correction there, for the field it is rather than for whose words it holds. The same enumeration-instead-of-citation defect is corrected in the two `/brd-reconcile` tables that govern writes into a record: both now cite the complete set `decision-register-format.md` §4 names — all thirteen fields, the stale-reference row excepting `argumentation` alone, which is the route's own prose and what an `updated` disposition is for.

### Fixed — the propagation sweep asked for a changed id that half its items do not have

`/brd-reconcile` reaches an item two ways: the citation pass finds one naming an id in the changed set, and the `conditional_on` pass finds one **by the field**, swept whether or not it cites a changed id. The disposition step nonetheless said to present *"the item, the changed id that reached it, and what changed about that id"*, and the `inherited-unchanged` row said to record *"the changed id that was considered"* — neither of which exists for a `conditional_on` position whose prerequisite decision this run did not move, leaving the operator a blank where the run owed them a fact. **Population: every reconciliation whose sweep reaches a `conditional_on` position this run's own changes never touched — narrow, and on 3.6.x as well, the two passes being as old as the sweep.** The step now presents what actually reached each item: the changed id and what changed about it for a citation-pass item, and the prerequisite decision the field names with whether this run moved it for a `conditional_on` one, saying so in as many words where it did not. The `inherited-unchanged` row records the same, and notes that such an item takes that disposition and no other — by the other three rows' own definitions, each recording a cause that **is** a changed id. Nothing downstream demanded a non-empty value: the reconciliation record already asks for each item's disposition and reason rather than for an id per item.

### Fixed — eleven text defects a live end-to-end run of the route hit, and a double outcome line

A run of `/brd-interview` then `/brd-reconcile` over a real root and slice hit eleven places where the text could not be followed as written, plus one contract breach. **Population: stated per item; all eleven predate this branch unless said otherwise.** `/brd-reconcile`: a customer who **explicitly declines to answer** — a case `customer-review-schema.md` §5 guarantees and the reader returns — had no disposition among the four, so *Confirm* would have frozen a non-answer as customer authority; it now takes *Reject*, which records the decline and leaves the question open. A `conflict` whose counterpart is dropped no longer claims *the obligation survives in the row kept*, which is false of two incompatible rows. And the command now says outright that which branch a conflict takes turns on the lowest-numbered row carrying the entry (`brd-format.md` §3) and must be reported with the pair. The stale sweep's exclusion table was **being read as the list** of what `updated` may not touch; the rule is now the class — any structured record, anything the customer wrote — with the table as worked cases, after a run found five more file kinds that belong to it. The `--sent` redundancy check no longer runs a gate whose `path` cannot be formed where the folder holds no prompt at all. The commercial next-step paragraph is scoped by its own condition rather than by the array it sits under, so a run that froze a `[CD#n]` and took `advance_ready: no` still prints it. And the two `handoff-to-main` calls this command makes now label their outcome lines and fix which one a `<merge-clause>` resolves from, the count itself being `workflows-core:phase-handoff` §4.1's to settle. `/brd-interview`: the NOT-PROVABLE re-tag route is reachable again, the question source and the re-tag now naming the different provenances each covers; step 9's input set reads *each slice under the parent, this one included*, where *sibling* made it empty on a one-slice parent; a held `[C]` entry's heading and delimiter are pinned as `## Round <N>, question <position>`, which is what lets its two readers bound an entry; the `[V]` picker no longer authors its own free-text escape, which `workflows-core:escalation-rules` §0 forbids; and it no longer reads as two entries per option. **One change rides with those eleven rather than being one of them**: `decision-register-format.md` gains **§1.1**, the serialisation counterpart to `workflows-core:grounding-format` §2.1 that the register lacked while two commands parse it by field and §4 writes fields in place.

### Fixed — eight defects an end-to-end `/brd-package` run hit, and the register's record boundary

A `/brd-package` run that reached completion — no stop at any phase — nonetheless shipped a package its own text had shaped wrongly. **Worst: `fixed` was offered on every finding** while the command mints no `[C]`, writes nothing into the interview's question set, changes no ledger disposition and owns no verified finding, so for an attack against any of those the disposition was offered and unavailable; the run fell to `accepted-risk`, and **11 of 17 accepted-risk findings were attacks the team agreed with and could not act on**, every one shipped to the customer as a risk it had chosen to take. **Population: every package whose review attacks an artifact this command may not change — on 3.6.x as well.** `fixed` is now admissible only against what this command may change, and the agreed-but-not-actionable case takes `accepted-risk` carrying a structured **`fixed-by:`** marker, whose value is a command or, where no command writes the named artifact, the person who must write it by hand. That marker stays inside the delivery organisation — the disposition's reason, the self-review, the final report — and is **never rendered**: part 9 forbids a command name in a finding's words and the plugin-free scan hard-stops on one over the finished prompt, so a clause naming the command would have stopped the package on the common path and the only way past would have been to strip it, reverting the fix. Part 9 instead renders one generated, plugin-free sentence **from the marker's presence and never from its value** — the team agrees, and it is recorded for repair outside this package rather than accepted as it stands. A **second-pass finding restating one already disposed** now takes the standing disposition and is rendered once, where both copies used to ship and the customer read six attacks twice. `render-schema` renumbers the schema body **under the part it is rendered into** rather than restarting at 1 inside a numbered outline; part 3 excludes the prompt itself, which §1.1's first row admits and which is the document the reviewer is reading; rule 4 removes **every** bundled document's frontmatter rather than the figures file's alone; and `bundle-packaging.md` §1.1 now disposes of a captured file that is neither markdown nor an image — not bundled, named in the manifest, its links rendered as the plain description §2 requires. **`/brd-package`'s own manifest rule did not carry the obligation.** Phase 8 rule 6 enumerated three things the manifest holds and stopped, so a run working it as the enumeration it reads like wrote neither the *captured and not bundled* line §1.1 requires nor the *what the reader may not be able to see* note §2.1 obliges for a byte-for-byte copy. Rule 6 now names both and says outright that what it enumerates is this command's contribution to the manifest, not its contents. **Population: every package carrying a captured file that is neither markdown nor an image, or a customer file holding an embedded image or a one-tool block** — reachable only from 3.7.0, the §1.1 disposition being this release's (`captured and not bundled` returns nothing at `4387959f`). `/brd-interview` pins that **a re-tagged question keeps its position**, which nothing had fixed, and `/brd-package` now reads a held `[C]` entry's round off the heading `/brd-interview` pins for it. **Keeping the position put two recorded states at one round-and-position, and nothing said which governs.** The round record is append-only, so a question re-tagged and then *deferred* carries a terminal disposition (*re-tagged*) and a holding state at the same number, while the readers that ask what state a question is in — the resume rule, the closure rule and `/brd-package`'s gate on the rounds — were each written in the singular. A reader keyed on the address found the question terminal, resumed past it, and shipped a package over a deferred question. The **last** state recorded at an address now governs and the earlier is history, the same rule the record's `Status:` line already carried. **Population: every round holding a re-tagged question that then reached a holding state** — reachable only from 3.7.0, since the keeps-its-number rule is this release's (`A re-tagged question keeps its number too` returns nothing at `4387959f`). **And `decision-register-format.md` §1.1's record boundary is a positive test** — a blank line followed by a line at block indentation — because §4 mandates an appended closing paragraph inside `argumentation`, so "no blank line inside a block" would have split every reopened record in two or folded the marker `bundle-packaging.md` §6.3 reads into the customer's quotation.

**The `fixed` narrowing above had landed in the disposition table and not in the two paragraphs that act on it**, and the half that was missing is the half a run executes: the re-dispatch paragraph still told the run that `fixed` corrects "a decision, a seed or a `[C]` question" — the three the table had just made inadmissible — so an orchestrator reading it edited `decisions.md`, a write `decision-register-format.md` §4 forbids and `/brd-reconcile` refuses when a **customer** asks for it. An orchestrator reading the table instead found `fixed` unreachable in practice, because three of the five artifacts it admits are written by later phases and the row said the correction lands "before the prompt is rendered", which is incoherent for the prompt. The row now says what *corrected* means for each half — the self-review and an `[SR#n]` record are corrected at the gate; the prompt, the delivery note and the bundle's rendered copies take the correction **recorded** there and carried out by the phase that writes them, which is also why no `fixed` is ever a write into a package document. The re-dispatch fires on **any** `fixed` whatever its artifact, and the corrections reach the second pass through the self-review in `prior_reviews`. **Population: every 3.6.x package run whose review attacked a register record, a held `[C]` entry, a ledger disposition or a verified finding** — on 3.6.1 the disposition table carried no admissibility rule at all and the re-dispatch paragraph sanctioned the register edit outright (`git show 4387959f:plugins/product-workflows/commands/brd-package.md`, the `fixed` row and the re-dispatch paragraph), while §4 and `/brd-reconcile`'s own refusal row were already on file there; the P3 run had five of seventeen findings targeting `decisions.md`.

**The narrowing's two halves were each an enumeration where a rule belonged**, and a live package run found two artifacts outside both: an entry in the requirement-defect log, and the inventory's coverage record. `fixed`'s exclusion is now the complement of the five artifacts this command writes, and the `fixed-by:` marker names the command that writes the artifact rather than choosing from a fixed list. Where an artifact has no command writer at all — a defect entry a grounding finding would settle, whose `resolved-by: <SLICE-KEY>/[CG#n]` the route admits and no command mints — the marker is still written and takes the person who must write it by hand as its value, the recorded reason saying that no command writes it, because a marker naming a command that will not write it reads as routed when nothing is. **That last clause rules on the value and not on whether a marker is written**, and it was read the other way by a live `[C]`-only run of the route before this release shipped: two findings the team had agreed with went to the customer unmarked, which part 9 renders as no sentence at all — indistinguishable from the eight risks that run had genuinely weighed and accepted. The value now has exactly two shapes, a command or a person, and part 9's one generated sentence says only what holds of both. **Population: every package whose review attacks an artifact the row's own enumeration did not name** — the run met two of twenty-one findings in that state. Six smaller ones ride with it: a reviewer's own words added to the wikilink-rewrite carve-out in both the rule and `bundle-packaging.md` §2.1; the render-boundary test matched wrap-insensitively, since the sentence it seeks is hard-wrapped in the file it seeks it in; *"any `fixed`"* and *"exactly once"* separated into which dispositions open a review and how many times one opens; the restated pair printing in whichever wording says more; the *Send it* option no longer naming a route an archive-route run is not on; and relation 3's scope stated once beside its three guards. **Population for the boundary test: every package run, on any tree** — it is a stop, and a line-anchored implementation of it fires on correct content every time.

### Fixed — stale claims the release sweep found

`/brd-intake` Phase 2 and two documentation pages said a file the intake did not capture was outside the record *permanently* — *"the only chance there is"*, *"gone for good"*. That stopped being true in this release, whose wikilink fix above rests on a re-run over the same folder capturing what an earlier one missed; each now says the file stays out of the record until the document is intaken again, since nothing but that copy phase ever writes under `brd/source/` or `brd/source-external/`. `/brd-intake`'s documentation page said an `EMPTY` read makes Phase 8 offer a re-run *"instead of offering `/prd-ground`"*; the command it leaves out is `/brd-split`, the ordinary next step and the one that refuses an empty inventory. And `/brd-package` Phase 0 said nothing in the run reads the customer's document or the defect log, while its assembly phase scans every copy of both; it now says no phase before assembly reads them, nor the figures file, which assembly also reads to choose which images ship.

**No run's behaviour changed.** Four of these sentences are documentation prose or a command's rationale, and the fifth is a `/brd-package` Phase 0 sentence that gates nothing. **Population: every reader of them** — an operator deciding whether a missed file can still be captured, or an agent executing either command in order — who was told something the tree does not do.

### Fixed — `/brd-intake` on a slice key destroyed every allocation `/brd-split` had recorded

Phase 0 step 7 stated the absolute — *"A slice is never a legitimate target here"* — and then discharged it with *"say so and confirm before Phase 2 copies anything into it"*: no `choices:` array, no stop id, against four `*_ROOT_LEVEL` stops elsewhere on this route and this command's own four Phase 0 stops. A slice that claims any row holds `brd/brd-inventory.md` with those rows in it, `/brd-split` having copied the parent's in, so this release's new re-run confirmation fired and was the only well-formed array on that path — an array whose keeps-and-changes list and whose disposition warning are both written for a source-owning root, and whose warning calls `covered-here` an *illegal root* disposition where on a slice it is the legitimate terminal one. Answering it *Re-run over this folder* sent Phase 2 to write `brd/source/` into a folder that holds none (`brd-format.md` §2.1), Phase 3 to renumber rows §2.1 says are never re-extracted, Phase 4 to write a `brd/brd-defect-log.md` shadowing the parent's `[DEF#n]` namespace, and Phase 5 to replace every `covered-here` the allocate-only walk had recorded with `unallocated`. Nothing short of git recovers that. Phase 6's whole *write the seeds at the root* rule also rested on a refusal step 7 did not make. On a **standing empty child** (`claims: []`, kept against a recorded `reason:`) the same run was worse: that confirmation is skipped silently where no inventory row stands, so Phase 2 wrote into the slice with no consent asked anywhere. Step 7 now **stops**, with `BRD_INTAKE_SLICE`, taken before the re-run confirmation is composed and keyed on `brd-link.md`'s `parent:` field rather than on rows, so it covers both states; the stop names the whole recovery route — re-intake the parent, re-take the dispositions with the root walk that run offers when it finishes, and only then re-allocate a slice the walk left a row `unallocated` in, once `/product-workflows:prd-ground` has ground that slice — and takes `<PARENT-KEY>` from the resolved folder's own `brd-link.md` `parent:` field rather than parsing one out of the folder name (`CLAUDE.md`, *Resolve an identifier against a known set*). Phase 9's list of Phase 0 stops that are operator halts and never `emit-block`s names it, and Phase 6 cites it by id. **Population: every `/brd-intake` run whose key resolves to a slice — on 3.6.x as well**, where the same step carried the same unspecified confirmation with no array behind it at all; what this release added was the array that made the destructive answer look like a considered one.

### Fixed — five defects in what `/brd-intake` records and reports about the customer's images

All five are **new in this release**, whose `brd/brd-figures.md` and its §1.2 are new.

**A row anchored on an image the run did not capture was reported two opposite ways in one run.** Phase 3 decided between *not re-extracted by this read* and *this source no longer contains* off the anchor, glossing the second with *"or its file is not in this run's capture"*.
